About this text

Clarifying the Words: The Derivation of Forms Ending in ti and the Rest, known in Tibetan as Ti la sogs pa'i mtha'i bya ba tshig gsal ba and given a Sanskrit title transcribed as Tyādyantasya Prakriyāpadarohaṇa, is a manual of Sanskrit grammar composed by the master known in Tibetan as Joyful Renown. Working in the tradition of the Kātantra or Kalāpa system, whose authorities it names as Kumāra, Śarvavarman, Kātyāyana, Durga and Trilocana, it sets out step by step how Sanskrit verb forms are derived, explaining roots, suffixes, augments and the special rules for denominative verbs with a great many worked examples. After a homage to the Buddha and opening verses that liken clear words to a blossoming lotus, the treatise proceeds through its rules in concise prose, and its colophon records that it was translated into Tibetan by the Śākya monk Dharmaśrībhadra at Riphuk, through the kindness of the great translator Butön. It is Toh 4452 in the Tengyur, where it belongs among the treatises on language and grammar, the field of śabdavidyā.

Translation

In the language of India: dyadyantasya prakriyapadarohaṇa nāma. In the language of Tibet: Clarifying the Words: The Derivation of Forms Ending in ti and the Rest.

Homage to the Buddha.

May the lotus of words in full bloom,
Rich in the fragrance of its petals, the meanings of speech,
Holding the honey-essence of fine nectar,
Come to flourish in my mind.

Purified through its various chapters,
Clear even where it is condensed,
Taught by groups and taught in brief:
With few words I shall make it clear in this work.

The procedure closely studied
By Kumāra, Śarvavarman,
Kātyāyana, Durga and Trilocana,
This introductory procedure shall now be stated.

Let one who has spent twelve years on grammar
And has cast off its toil,
Seated on the lion throne of poetry,
Teach this Clarifying of the Words.

Here there are two divisions: the si series and the tī series. Of these, the si series are those that end with supa. Among them, si au jas is the first; am au śas is the second; ṭā bhyām bhis is the third; de bhyām bhyas is the fourth; ṅi bhyām bhyas is the fifth; ṅas os ām is the sixth; ṅi os supa is the seventh. Thus are distinguished the seven triads named "first" and so on, established by common worldly usage. They are to be applied after the nominal stem, in their numerical order, for the seven senses: the meaning of the stem itself, the object, the instrument, the recipient, the source, the owner, and the locus. Having stated these, now the ti series is to be stated: the endings from tī up to syāmahi.

Among them, ti tas anti si thas tha mi bas mas te āte ante se āthe dhve e bahe mahe is the barttamānā.

yāt yātām yus yās yātām yāt yām yāba yām ītā īyātām īran īthās īyāthāṃ īdhvaṃ īya ībahi īmahi is the saptamī.

tu tām antu hi tam ta āni āba āma tām ātām antāma sva āthām dhvaṃ ai ābahe āmahe is the pañcamī.

di tāma an si tam ta am ba ma ta ātām ānta thās athām dhvaṃ i bahi mahi is the hyasta. The adyatanī is just like this.

aṭ atus us thal athus a aṭ ba ma e āte ire se āthe dhve e bahe mahe is the parokṣā.

tā tārau tāras tāsi tāthās tāstha tāsmi tāsbas tāsmas tā tārau tāras tāse tāsāthe tādhve tāhe tāsbahe tāsmahe is the śvastanī.

yāt yāstvām yāsus yās yāstam yāsta yāsaṃ yāsva yāsma sīṣṭa sīyāstam sīrāṇ sīṣṭhās sīyāsthāṃ sidhvaṃ sīya sībahi sīmahi is the āśīṣa.

syati syatas syanti syasi syathas syatha syāmi syābas syāmas syate syete syante syese syethe syedhve sye syabahe syāmahe is the bhabiṣyanti.

syata syatām syan syas syatam syata syam syaba syama syata syetām syanta syathās syethām syadhvi sye syabahi syamhi is the lapsed action.

These ten sets of eighteen, named barttamānā and so on, are the divisions of the tī series.

Of these, "what is ongoing" is the barttamānā: an action from its commencement until it is not yet complete. For present time the barttamānā division is used. For injunction, invitation, summoning, request, inquiry and entreaty, that is, for injunction and the rest, the saptamī and the pañcamī are used. For permission, for enabling, and for wishing, the pañcamī is used once more. Time that took place yesterday is "yesterday"; for past time of yesterday the hyasta division is used. Time that took place today is "today"; for past time of today the adyatani division is used. What is beyond the senses is "hidden," meaning that it is not an object of the senses; for hidden past time the parokṣā division is used. Time that takes place tomorrow is "tomorrow"; for time that will come about tomorrow the śvāsta division is used. To speak auspiciously of attaining a desired aim in the future is a blessing; for future time accompanied by a wish the aśīs division is used. bhabiṣyatī means the time that will come about; for time that will come about the bhabiṣintī division is used. An action that has passed by is a lapsed action: where an action is not brought to completion because of a twofold action, for past time with a lapsed action the kriyātipatti is used.

Of the ten divisions bartamānā and the rest, the first nine words of each become those named parsmai words: ti tas anti si thas tha mi bas mas. The latter nine words of each become those named ātmane words: te āte atme se āthe dhve e bahe mahe. So it is in every case. Among the parasmai words and the ātmane words alike, the words three by three, in order, become those named first, middle and last. Thus ti tas anti is the first, si thas tha is the middle, and mi bas mas is the last; te āte ante is the first, se āthe dtve is the middle, and e bahe mahe is the last. So it is in every case.

Further, the endings named singular, dual and plural are each to be understood separately from worldly usage, as follows: ti is singular, tas is dual, and anti is plural. In the same way they are to be seen in all the triads. Because it says "to be understood separately," by the force of the fitting rule "what has no meaning of its own is a suffix," ti and the rest are to be called suffixes. Or else this is simply well known.

According to the rule "what comes before the suffix," there is the base. The base is also of two kinds: the nominal stem and the verbal root. Of these, bhū and the rest are to be stated. Among these, since those ending in the letter a occur as ones to be joined, they then express action, as ji in the sense of conquering. By the rule "what conveys action is a root," whatever is named a root is, by the rule "what comes before the suffix," the base.

By the very force of its agreement with the meaning, it is established as bearing the name "base." Where the name "base" applies, from the words "the suffix comes after," the suffix comes after the base. By the rule "in the present, the bartamā," the bartamā division comes in. Then, by the rule "the parasmai words," these bear the name parasmai words. Where the name parasmai words applies, by the rule "from the rest, for the agent, the barasmai words," they become parasmai words for the agent. Then, by the rule "the parasmai words" and the rule "the triads are first, middle and last," they become, in numerical order, those named first, middle and last.

Since there is no restriction here, a restriction is to be made. "With a noun, also the first." "With 'you,' the middle." "With 'we,' the last." With a noun, whether it is actually used or not used, it becomes the first person. With "you," whether it is used or not used, it becomes the middle person. With "we," whether it is used or not used, it becomes the last person. So it is in every case. In every case, when one wishes to express one thing, it becomes the singular for the meaning of one, the dual for the meaning of two, and the plural for the meaning of many.

From the force of the name that agrees with the meaning, "the expression of one, two or many," and by the rule "the first six of each are sārvadhātuka," they bear the name sārvadhātuka. Where the name sārvadhātuka applies, from the general rule "yaṇ in the sārvadhātuka," and by the rule "for the agent, āṅ, that is, the bikaraṇa," there is an for the agent. The letter na in an serves the particular purpose of the rule "also in the bikaraṇa." From the general rule "guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin and of the bikaraṇas," before aṇa, by "also in the bikaraṇa," there is guṇa. And what is guṇa? By the rule "ar and the first two sandhi letters," ar e o are guṇa. It comes in the place of a nāmin. And what is a nāmin? By the rule "the vowels other than the letter ā are nāmin," the vowels from i up to the letter o are nāmin. Then, by the rule "in place, the nearest," ar comes in place of the letter ri, the letter e in place of the letter i, and the letter o in place of the letter u, respectively. By the rule "e becomes aya," it is changed to aya: jayati.

By the rule "when the meaning of what precedes and what follows is perceived, a word," it bears the name "word." From the general rule "at the end of a word, the first of the dhuṭas," and by the rule "the visarga of ra and of the letter śa," the letter śa is changed into a visarga: joyatiḥ. By the rule "before a and the sandhi letters, a becomes those, and is also elided," a is taught in place of the letter a, and it is elided by the following letter a. By the rule "of a dhuṭa in the instruction, anusvāra," the letter na becomes anusvāra. Again, by the rule "before a class consonant, the final of that class," the anusvāra becomes the letter na: jayanti jayasi jayathāḥ jayatha. By the rule "lengthening of a before ba and ma," there is lengthening. By the rule "a consonant without a vowel is drawn to the following letter," it goes over to the following one: jayāmi jayābaḥ jayāmaḥ.

pra parā apa sama anu aba nida du ṅa babhi bi adhi su uda ati ni prati pari api, together with uba āṅ: these twenty are the group of prefixes, as the poet has said. By the rule "these twenty prefixes are joined with the action," they have the name "prefix" from worldly usage, and likewise, it is said, they are to be joined before the root. It is also said: "ca and the rest are to be known as particles, and pra and the rest are prefixes; because they are themselves manifest, their joining with the action is ascertained from the world."

Because it has meaning by way of making a meaning clear, by the rule "what has meaning, apart from a root and a case ending, is a nominal stem," it bears the name "nominal stem." By the rule "after it, the case endings," and so on, the case endings sī and the rest come after the nominal stem. When all of si and the rest could apply, by the rule "for a word in the meaning of the nominal stem, the first case ending," it takes the first case ending. Then, when one wishes to express one thing, for one thing there is the singular si. The i of si is for the sake of pronunciation. From the general rule "after others it is elided," and by the rule "also after an indeclinable," si is elided.

By the rule "ji after bi and parā," there are ātmane words. And again, what are ātmane words? By the rule "the latter nine are ātmane," they are ātmane words. By the rule "for the agent, after those of the ruca group and those with the marker ṅa," they become ātmane words for the agent. It is not without guṇa, and it is changed to aya: bijayate. By the rule "also of āte āthe," the letter i replaces the letter a. By the rule "a with the letter i becomes e," it becomes e itself, and the following one is elided as well: bijayete, by the rule for a preceding word and a sandhi letter. bijayante bijayase bejayethe bijayedhve. The rule for a and the sandhi letters is this: the letter e comes in place of the letter a, and the following letter e is elided: bijaye. There is lengthening of a: bijyābata bijayāmate. In the same way, parājayate barājayete parājayante and the rest are to be derived.

ṣmid is in the sense of laughing slightly. The letter da serves the particular purpose of the rule "for the agent, after those of the ruca group and those with the marker da." Those that do not come after svaradanti and so on are the ones taught with ṣa. So too are smi svadi svadi svabi. sṛ sṛbi sṛji smṛ styā sīkṛ sekṛ are left aside. By the rule "the sa at the beginning of a root," the letter sa replaces the letter ṣa. By the rule "for the agent, after those of the ruca group and those with the marker ṅa," because of the marker itself, there are ātmane words: smayate smayete smayante bismayate bismayante.

bhaja and śriña are in the sense of resorting to. The marker letter ña serves the particular purpose of the rule "after those with in and ña, and after yaja and the rest, both": śrayati śayataḥ śyanti. With pari before it: pariśayati bariśayata bariśayanti. With ut before it: by the rule "at the end of a word, the first of the dhuṭas," the letter ta replaces the letter da. By the rule "after the first letters of the classes, the letter śa followed by a vowel or by ya ba ra becomes the letter cha, or optionally does not," the letter cha replaces the letter śa.

By the rule "the letter ta, before la and before the ca and ṭa classes, takes the form of what follows," the letter cha replaces the letter ta. By the rule "before an unvoiced consonant, the first," the letter ca replaces the letter cha: ucchvayati, samucchvayati, atyucchvayati. With ni before it: niśrayati. By the rule "after those with in and ña, and after yaja and the rest, both," because of the marker ña the forms of both sets of endings occur: śrayate, śrayete, śayante.

ṭu o śvi is in the sense of going and of growing. The syllable ṭu serves the particular purpose of the rule "after one with the marker ṭu, athu." The letter o serves the particular purpose of the rule "also after lū and the rest and after those with the marker ot": śvayati, śvayataḥ, śvayanti.

Those ending in the letter i: ṭīd is in the sense of going through the sky: ḍayate, ḍayate, ḍayante. With ut before it: by the rule "at the end of a word, the first of the dhuṭas," the letter ta replaces the letter ḍa. By the rule "the letter ta, before la and before the ca and ṭa classes, takes the form of what follows," the letter ḍa replaces the letter ta: uḍḍayate, uḍḍayate, uḍḍayante.

ṇīña is in the sense of obtaining. The teaching with ṇa covers all such roots; nriti, nandi, narṅhi, naṭi, nādhri and nāthṛ are left out. Therefore, by the rule "na in place of ṇa," the letter na replaces the letter ṇa: nayati, nayataḥ, nayanti. By the rule "after the prefixes pra and pari standing before it, where there is no compound, the na of what is taught with ṇa," ṇa itself appears: praṇayati, pariṇayati. By the rule "after those with in and ña, and after yaja and the rest, both," because of the marker ña the forms of both sets of endings occur: nayata, nayete, nayante.

Those ending in the letter u: ṣu, sru, dru dru, ṛccha, gamli, si sli are in the sense of going: savati, pravati, davati, bravati. cyud, chyuṅ, jyud, jhyud, sruṅ, sluṅ, gāṅ, śyiṅ are in the sense of going: cyavate, chyavate, jyavate, jhyavate, pravate, slavate.

Those ending in the letter u: bhu is in the sense of existing: bhavate, brabhavati, praribhavati. With sam before it: by the rule "before a consonant, anusvāra in place of m," anusvāra replaces the letter ma. By the rule "before a class consonant, the fifth of the class," the fifth appears: sambhavati, anubhavati, vibhavati. With ud before it: by the rule "the first of a class at the end of a word, before vowels and voiced sounds, becomes the third," the letter da replaces the letter ta: uddhavati, abhibhavati.

bluṅ is in the sense of purifying: pavate. mūd is in the sense of binding: mavate.

Those ending in the letter ṛ: smri and dhyai are in the sense of thinking. The guṇa is ar: smarati, vismarati, saṃsmarati, apasmarati, anusmarati. spri is in the senses of sound and of affliction: svarati. By the rule "after saṃ: gam, ṛcchi, pṛcchi, spri, śru, ve, rti and driśa," the ātmanepada endings occur: saṃhvarate.

hṛña is in the sense of seizing: harati, prahati, anuharati, viharati, apaharati, abhiharati. By the rule "before a vowel that is not similar to it, i becomes ya, and what follows is not elided," the letter ya replaces the letter i: abhyavaharati, samabhyavaharati. With āṅ before it: the letter ṅa serves the particular purpose of the rule "always after āṅ and mā": āharati, vyāharati. Because of the marker ña, the forms of both sets of endings occur: harate.

Those ending in the letter ṝ: dṝ is in the senses of swimming and of carrying across: tarati, saṃtarati, vitarati, avatarati.

Those ending in the letter i: dheṭ and pā are in the sense of drinking. The letter ṭa serves the particular purpose of the rule "it shall also be stated after śve and dheṭ": dhayati, dhayataḥ, dhayanti. beña is in the sense of weaving. Because of the marker ña, the forms of both sets of endings occur: vayate, vayate. hveña is in the sense of challenging: hvayate. By the rule "hva after ni, saṃ, vi and upa," the ātmanepada endings occur: nihvaate, saṃhvayate, vihvayate, uvahvayate. By the rule "āṅ in the sense of challenging," the ātmanepada endings occur: āhvayate, sāmāhvayate.

maiṅ is in the sense of giving in return. Because of the marker ṅa, the ātmanepada endings occur: mayaste. When pra stands before it and ni stands before it, the na of ni becomes ṇa, by the rule "also in nada, gada, pata, pada, those named dī, miṅ, māṅ, syati, henti, yāti, pāti, drāti, psāti, vayati, vahati, śamyati, cinoti and degvī": praṇimayate. deṅ and traiṅ are in the sense of protecting: praṇidayaste.

Those ending in the letter e: treṅ. By the very force of the letter e being taught, there is no guṇa. By the rule "ai becomes āya," the letter ai is changed to āya: trāyate, paritrāyate. glai is in the sense of the waning of joy: glāyati. mlai is the decline of the body: mlāyati. kai, gai and rai are in the sense of sound: kāyati, gāyati, rāyati. daib is in the sense of purifying. The letter ba serves the particular purpose of the rule "dā and dhā, other than those with the marker ba, are called dā": dāyati, abadāyati. dhyai: dhyāyati, pradhyāyati, nidhyāyati. ṣṭyai and styai are in the senses of sound and of gathering together. Because both are taken up here, the change to sa under the rule "sa in place of ṣa at the beginning of a root" does not take place: ṣṭyeyati, styāyati. As for what is spoken of as ending in o and so forth, roots ending in the letter o simply do not occur.

Those ending in consonants, and those with the letter ā as penultimate: ḍāpacaṣa is in the sense of cooking. The syllable ṭu serves the particular purpose of the rule "after one with the marker ḍu, strimaka in the sense of what is accomplished by it." The letter a is for the sake of pronunciation. The letter ṣa serves the particular purpose of the rule "after those with the marker ṣa and after bhid and the rest, aṅ": pacati, pacatai.

yaja is in the senses of worshipping the gods, keeping company, and giving: yajati, yajate. bhaja: bhajati, bhajate. śapa is in the sense of cursing: śapati. By the rule "śapa in the sense of an oath," the ātmanepada endings occur: śapate. ṭuvapa is in the sense of sowing seed: vapati, vavate, praṇivavati, praṇivavate. vaha is in the sense of obtaining: vahati, vahate, praṇivahati, praṇivahate. By the rule "vaha after pra," the parasmaipada endings occur: pravahati. Here, because it is treated as a group, the parasmaipada endings occur. These are the explanations of the roots that take both.

tyaja is in the sense of abandoning: tyajati. phala is in the sense of coming to completion: phalati. vasa is in the sense of dwelling: vasati, pravasati, upavasati, nipasati. hase is in the sense of laughing. The letter a serves the particular purpose of the rule "optionally, for those ending in a consonant and the like that take iṭ, for those not marked with it, and for those ending in hma or ya, and for kṣaṇa and śvasa": hasati, prahasati, vihasati, upahasati. daha is in the sense of reducing to ashes.

dahati. ṇada is in the sense of indistinct sound: nadati, pranadati, praṇinadati. gada is in the sense of speaking clearly: gadati, praṇigadati, nigadati. pala, śala and patḷ, and laṃ as well, are in the sense of going. The letter ḷ serves the particular purpose of the rule "also after puṣa and the rest, dyuta and the rest, those with the marker ḷ, and arti, sarti and śati, in the parasmaipada": patati, nipatati, praṇipratati.

ṣvaṇa is in the sense of sound: svarati. By the rule "also after vi, of svana in the sense of eating," ṣa appears: biṣṇadi, apaṣvaṇati. naṭa is in the sense of dancing. Because it is not taught with ṇa, there is no ṇa: pranaprati.

tapa and dhuba are in the sense of tormenting: tavati. By the rule "tapa after vi and ut," the ātmanepada endings occur: vitapaste, uttapaste. By the rule "tapa when it has an object," the ātmanepada endings occur. And there, by the rule "of tapa, tipa, after one having an object," yaṇ applies in the sense of the agent: tapyate, tavastvapasaḥ. These are the parasmaipada forms.

rabha is in the sense of beginning: rabhate. With āṅ before it: aḍbhate. ḍulabhaṣa is in the sense of obtaining: labhate, ālabhate. Because of the prohibition in the rule "of rabhi and labhi, not in the vikaraṇa and the parokṣā," the letter na does not come as an augment. hada is in the sense of voiding excrement: hadate. ramu is in the sense of playing: damate. By the rule "rama after vi, āṅ and pari," the parasmaipada endings occur: viramati, āramati, bariramati. With upa before it: by the rule "also when it has an object," the parasmaipada endings occur: devadattamuparamate. These are the explanations of the ātmanepada.

Those with the letter ā as penultimate: ṭuyācṛ is in the sense of begging. The letter ṛ serves the particular purpose of the rule "not of śasu and of those with the marker ṛt": ācati, yācate. These are the explanations of the roots that take both.

Those with the letter a as penultimate: hladī is also in the sense of happiness. The letter ī serves the particular purpose of the rule "not for ḍīd and śvi, for those with the marker īt, and for those that take iṭ optionally, other than pati and niṣku": hlādate. ślāghṛ is in the sense of praising: ślāghate. nādhṛ and nāthṛ are in the senses of affliction, of lordship, and also of wishing: nādhate, pranādhate. By the rule "nātha in the sense of wishing," the ātmanepada endings occur: nāthāte, pranāthate. Otherwise the parasmaipada endings themselves occur: māṇavakamupanāthati. These are the explanations of the ātmanepada.

Those with the letter ī as penultimate: miha is in the sense of washing. Because it is stated short, it is light. By the rule "what comes before the final is the upadhā," it is named upadhā, and this follows from the general rule "of a light penultimate called upadhā." By the rule "also before a vikaraṇa, an," guṇa occurs: mehati, pramehate. citī is in the sense of perceiving: cetati. kita is in the sense of dwelling, and also of curing disease: ketati, which is in the sense of dwelling. Otherwise: cikatmite. ṣidhu is in the sense of going: sedhati, sedhati. By the rule "also of sthā, seni, sedhati, sica, manja and svanja, in the reduplication and elsewhere," ṣa appears: pratiṣedhati. Through the prohibition in the rule "sedhati, when not in the sense of going," there is no ṣa: parisetahi.

Those with the letter ī as penultimate: jība is in the sense of sustaining life. Because the penultimate is not light, there is no guṇa: jīvati. śīla is in the sense of meditative absorption: śīlati, pariśīlati mitvaṃ. mīla, smīla and kṣmīla are in the sense of closing the eyes: mīlati, smīlati, kṣmīlati. With saṃ before it: by the rule "before a fifth, the fifths, or else, not optionally, the thirds," the letter na replaces the letter ta: unmilati. krīḍaṃ is in the sense of abiding: krīḍati. By the rule "also of krīḍa after anu and pari," the ātmanepada endings occur: anukrīḍate, parikrīṭate. With sama before it: by the rule "after sam, when not in the sense of akūjana," the ātmanepada endings occur: saṃkrīḍate. These are the parasmaipada forms.

Those with the letter u as penultimate: guhū is in the sense of covering over completely. The letter ū serves the particular purpose of the rule "svarati, sūti, sūyate, and after those with the marker ūt." Guṇa occurs. By the rule "ūt for the penultimate of gohi," the letter u replaces the letter o: guhati, nighūhati, gūhate, nigūhate, avagūhati, avagūhate. These are the explanations of the roots that take both.

ṣṭubhu is in the sense of stiffening: stobhate. By the rule "after a prefix, of sunoti, subhati, syati and stobhyati, also with aṭ and elsewhere," ṣa appears: abhiṣṭabhate. These are ātmanepada forms.

Those with the letter ū as penultimate: kṣīja, kūja and guji are in the sense of indistinct sound: kūjata. This is a parasmaipada form. ūhe is in the sense of conceptual thought. It takes the ātmanepada endings: ūhata. By the rule "after a prefix, optionally for asyati and ūhi," the ātmanepada endings occur optionally: samūhati, samūhate.

Those with the letter ṛ as penultimate: bṛtu is in the sense of proceeding. The letter u serves the particular purpose of the rule "for those with the marker ut, and for pū and kliśa, before ktra." Guṇa occurs: vartate, vartte, varttanda. śṛdhu is in the sense of contemptible sound: śardhate. kṛpū is in the sense of being capable. By the rule "la for the ri of kṛpa," la replaces the repha: kalbate, prakalbate, sikalbate, bikalbate, avakalpate.

Those with the letter e as penultimate: ṭubisri and kabi are in the sense of moving: bebati. The letter i serves the particular purpose of the rule beginning "of those without the marker it." For this very reason, by the rule "by the force of exclusion, there is na," the letter na is an augment: kampate. ejṛ, bhrejṛ and bhrajṛ are in the sense of blazing: ejate. By the rule "the a of a prefix is elided before a root's e and o," the letter a of the prefix is elided: prejate, upejate, barejate, bhrejate. edha is in the sense of growing: edhate. Through the prohibition in the rule "not of iṇ and edhati," the letter a of the prefix is not elided. By the rule "a with i becomes e, and so too ...," ai takes the place of e: ubedhate. hetha is in the sense of harming: baihethate.

Those with the letter e as penultimate: lokṛ and locṛ are in the sense of seeing: ālokastai, vilokate, vyalokate, ālocate, samālocate. okhṛ: o vati. The letter a of the prefix is elided: prokhati.

Those with the letter au as penultimate: dhaukṛ and traukṛ: dhaukate, traugate. In the same way it should be seen in other cases too. sbaddha is in the sense of complete rejoicing.

sbaddhate, pisbardhāte. ṣvakka is in the sense of going. By the force of the words "of ṣvakka there is no change," the root-initial ṣa does not become sa: ṣvakkate. These are ātmanepada forms.

ṭu o phūrjjā is in the sense of the vajra's crushing. The letter ā serves the particular purpose of the rule "also after those with the marker āt." By the rule "of a nāmin before ra and va, but not of kur and chur," the letter u is lengthened: pūrjjāti. narda and garda are in the sense of sound: nardati, pranardati, gardati. lunca is in the sense of removing. Anusvāra and the class-final occur: luncati, niluncati. bancu is in the sense of going: bancati. ancu is in the senses of going and of worshipping: ancati. With ut before it: by the rule "the first of a class at the end of a word, before vowels and voiced sounds, becomes the third," the letter da replaces the letter ta: udancate. This is a parasmaipada form.

śransu and bhaṣsu are in the sense of falling downward: śvisate, bhraṃsate. dhvensu is in the sense of going: bhraṃsate. śranbhu is in the sense of breathing out: biśrambhate. These are ātmanepada forms. danśa is in the sense of biting with the fangs. ṣanja is in the sense of clinging. These take parasmaipada forms. ṣvañja is in the sense of embracing. It takes ātmanepada forms. ranja is in the sense of dyeing. It takes the forms of both. By the rule "of dañji, sanji, ṣvañci and ranji, before an," the nasal is elided: daśati, sajati, svajate. By the rule "also of sthā, seni, sedtati, sica, sanja and ṣvañja, in the reduplication and elsewhere," ṣa appears: abhyiṣvajate, anuṣvejate, pariṣvajite, rajati, rajata.

ṇṛkṣa is in the sense of kissing. It takes parasmaipada forms: nikṣati. There, for nisi, nikṣi and ninba, ṇa is optional: praṇikṣati, praṇikṣati.

Those with the marker it shall now be stated. ṇidi is in the sense of reviling. The letter i serves the particular purpose of the rule "of those without the marker it, no guṇa." By this very thing, the letter na is an augment. Anusvāra and the class-final occur: nindati, praṇindati, pranindati. bāchi is in the sense of desiring: bāñchati. These take parasmaipada forms.

sbadi is in the sense of moving slightly: spyandate, praspandate, parisvandate. labi is in the sense of hanging down: lambate, pralambate, alambate. khadi is in the senses of speaking clearly and of praising: bandatai, apibandate. śaki is in the sense of doubt: śaṅkate, īśvaṅgate. mathi and kathi are in the sense of grief: manthate, utkanthate. jabha and jṛbhi are in the sense of the body's slackening. By the rule "of radhi and jabha, before a vowel," the letter na is an augment: jambhate, jṛmbhate. These are ātmanepada forms.

ṣṭhibu and kību are in the sense of expelling. It does not become sa under the rule "sa in place of ṣa at the beginning of a root," because of the teaching "an of ṣṭhibu, klamu and ācama." klamu is in the sense of weariness. camu, chamu, jhamu and jimu are in the sense of eating. By the rule "of ṣṭhibu, klamu and ācama, before an," lengthening occurs: ṣṭhibati, niṣṭhabati, klāmati, ācamati. kramu is in the sense of stepping. By the rule "of krama, in the parasmaipada," lengthening occurs: krāmati, saṃkrāmati, upakrāmati. By the rule "optionally, when there is no prefix," the ātmanepada endings occur: krāmaste.

gamḷ is in the sense of going. iṣu is in the sense of wishing. yamu is in the sense of cessation. By the rule "cha for gam, iṣu and yam," cha appears. By the rule "after a vowel, the letter cha is doubled," there are two chas. By the rule "before an unvoiced consonant, the first," the letter ca appears: gacchati. By the rule beginning "after si: gamba, ṛcchi, pṛcchi," the ātmanepada endings occur: saṅgacchate, icchati, yacchati. By the rule "after āṅ, yam and hana too, when their object is a limb of one's own," the ātmanepada endings occur: āyacchate.

dheṭa and pā are in the sense of drinking. By the rule "biba for pa," it is changed to piba. From this it is changed into a form ending in a. Then comes a, and the procedure for the letters in junction. Therefore guṇa does not occur, because it is the penultimate. By the force of the statement "or of one ending in a consonant," guṇa does not occur: bibati, pibataḥ, bibanti. ghrati is in the sense of taking in. By the rule "jighrā for ghra," it is changed to jighra: jighrataḥ, jighrataḥ, jighranti. dhmā is in the senses of sound and of kindling fire. By the rule "dhama for dhā," it is changed to dhama: dhamati, dhamatiḥ, dhamanti, bidhamāti.

ṣṭhe is in the sense of ceasing to go. Sa replaces the root-initial ṣa. By the principle "when the cause is absent, what depends on the cause is absent too," tha replaces ṭha. By the rule "tiṣṭha for sthā," it is changed to tiṣṭha: tiṣṭhati, tiṣṭhataḥ, tiṣṭhanti. By the rule "sthā after sam, ava, pra and vi," the ātmanepada endings occur: saṃtiṣṭhate, avatiṣṭhate, pratiṣṭhate, bitiṣṭhate.

mnā is in the sense of repeated practice. By the rule "mana for mnā," it is changed to mana: manati. dāna is in the sense of giving. The letter ṇā serves the particular purpose of the rule "yaccha for dāna." By the rule "yaccha for dāṇa," it is changed to yaccha: yacchati, ti, prayacchati.

dṛśir is in the sense of seeing. The letter i and the repha serve the particular purpose of the rule "optionally, after those with the marker ir." By the rule "baśya for dṛśi," it is changed to baśya: paśyati. By the rule beginning "after sam: gam, ṛccha, pṛcchi," the ātmanepada endings occur: saṃbaśyate. ṛ is also in the sense of obtaining. By the rule "ṛccha for artti," it is changed to riccha: ricchāti. By the rule beginning "after sam: gami," the ātmanepada endings occur: samṛcchati.

sṛ is in the sense of going. By the rule "dhava for siddhi," it is changed to dhava; this is held to be in the sense of going swiftly: dhavati. Otherwise: sarti, anusarti, saṃsarati, pratisarati, abhisarti. ṣadḷ is in the sense of decaying. By the rule "of śadi, in ān," the ātmanepada endings occur. By the rule "śīya for śada," it is changed to śīya: śīyate. ṣadḷ is in the senses of moving about, of going, and of despising. By the rule "sida for sadi," it is changed to sāda: sāditi, āpasāditi, prasīdate. By the rule "of sadī, when not after prati," ṣi appears: niṣīdani, biṣīdati, pāriṣīdati. After prati it does not change: pratisīdati, pariṣīdati. After prati it does not change: pratisīdati. These are parasmaipada forms.

gubū is in the sense of protecting. tapa and dhūpa are in the sense of tormenting. liśa and biccha are in the sense of going. paṇa is in the sense of trading, and also of praising; and so is bana. By the rule "āya after gupū, dhūpa, bicchi, paṇi and pani," the suffix āya is added. Guṇa of the light penultimate occurs. By the rule "these are roots," they receive the name root. Then an, and a, and the procedure for the letters in junction.

gopāyaste, dhūpayati, bacchayati, panāyati, panāyate. Paṇī and pani take the ātmanepada forms. Some say: "Because it goes together with pani, āya is to be formed from paṇi only in the sense of praising." Here, however, āya is to be formed from paṇi in the sense of trading.

Ṛta is a root that arises from the sūtra. It applies in the sense of compassion. By the rule "after ṛti, ṇīyaṅ is also to be stated," the suffix ṇīyaṅ is added. The letter ṇa serves the particular purpose of the rule "not for those with the marker ṇa and for cakrīyita," and therefore guṇa does not occur: ṛtīyate.

kamu is in the sense of desiring. By the rule "after kami, the kārita suffix eni," the suffix iniṅ is added. The letter ṅa is for the sake of the ātmanepada forms. The letter i is for the sake of pronunciation. The letter na serves the particular purpose of the rule "before in, ic and aṭ, a penultimate a is lengthened, and the vṛddhi of the nāmins." Kāmi; then an; guṇa occurs; it is changed to ay: kāmayate. With this, the roots of the bhū class and the rest, which take the general vikaraṇa an, are complete.

Ad and the rest shall now be stated. at and psā are in the sense of eating. The endings are those of the vartamānā. Then an. By the rule "after ad and the rest, the vikaraṇa is lost," an is lost. By the rule "before unvoiced sounds, the first, for those that are not ṣiṭ," the letter da becomes ta; and by the rule "a consonant without a vowel is drawn to the following letter," it passes over to the following one: atti, attaḥ, adanti, atsi, atthaḥ, attha, admi, adba, admaḥ. psā: psārti. By the rule "with a similar vowel it becomes long, and what follows is elided," what follows the long vowel is elided: praṇipsāti.

śas is in the sense of sleeping. An is lost: sasti, sastaḥ, sasanti. baśa is in the sense of being lovely. By the rule "of cha and śa, ṣa," ṣa appears. By the rule "after ṣa and the ṭa class, the ṭa class in place of the ta class," the ṭa class replaces the letter ta: biṣṭi. By the rule "also in the dual and the plural, in the parasmaipada," there is no guṇa. By the rule "of grahi, jya, bayi, byadhi, biṣṭa, byaci, pracchi, praṣchi and bhrasji, where there is no guṇa," saṃprasāraṇa occurs: the letter u replaces the letter pa: uṣṭaḥ, uṣanti. Ṣa appears. By the rule "ka for ṣa and dha before sa," ka replaces ṣa, and because of the conditioning factor, ṣa appears: pakṣi, uṣṭhaḥ, uṣṭha, baṣmi, uṣbaḥ.

hana is in the senses of harming and of going. An is lost. By the rule "anusvāra for ma and na before a dhuṭ," anusvāra replaces the letter na. By the rule "before a class consonant, the class-final," the letter na replaces the anusvāra: handi. By the rule "the consonants other than the semivowels and the nasals are dhuṭ," they receive the name dhuṭ. By the rule "of handi before a dhuṭ, in the sārvadhātuka," the letter na is elided: hata. By the rule "of gam, han, jan, khan and ghas, the penultimate before a vowel-initial ending other than aṇ, where there is no guṇa," the penultimate a is elided. By the rule "also when the penultimate is deleted," gha replaces ha: ghnandi, hansi, hathaḥ, hatha, hanmi, hanvaḥ, hanmaḥ, nihanti, praṇihandi, yrahandi. By the rule "of hanati preceded by pra, optionally before pa and ma," na appears: prahammi, prahaṇvaḥpra, prahaṭmaḥ, prahanmi, prahasvaḥ, prahanmaḥī.

With ī before it: by the rule "after āṅ, am and han too, when their object is a limb of one's own," the ātmanepada endings occur. By the rule "for all, in the sārvadhātuka ātmanepada, except the last of the pañcamī," there is no guṇa. Na is elided: āhate. When the penultimate is deleted, gha also appears: āghāte. By the rule "after what is not a, in the ātmanepada," na is elided: āghāte, āhase, āghāthe, āhadhve, āghāe, āhanmahe, āhanmahe.

dyu is in the sense of advancing. yu is in the sense of mixing. ṇu is in the sense of praising. kṇu is in the sense of sharpening. ṣtu is in the sense of oozing. ṭu kṣu, ru and ku are in the sense of sound. ṣu is in the sense of begetting. An is lost. By the rule "vṛddhi of ut before a consonant-initial sārvadhātuka ending that has guṇa," vṛddhi occurs. If one asks, "What, again, is vṛddhi?": by the rule "ār and the following ones are also vṛddhi," āra, ai and au receive the name vṛddhi. By the rule "there, by place, the closest," ār stands in place of the letter ṛ, ai in place of the letter i, and au in place of the letter u: dyauti, dyatāḥ. By the rule "iya and uva for a root's final i and u before a vowel-initial ending," it is changed to uv: dyavanti, dyauṣi, dyuthaḥ, dyutha, dyaumi, dyuvaḥ, dyumaḥ. In the same way: auti. Na replaces ṇa: nauti. By the rule "nu in the sense of asking," the ātmanepada endings occur: ānute, avate, kṣṇauti. Sa replaces the root-initial ṣa. Because of the presence of the conditioning factor, ṇa appears: snauti, prasnauti, barisnauti, kṣoti, rauti, kauti, sauti.

iṇ is in the sense of going. The letter ṇa serves the particular purpose of the rule "ga for iṇa." An is lost. By the rule "in the singular forms, guṇa of a root ending in a nāmin and of a vikaraṇa": itaḥ. By the rule "also after in," ya appears: ante, eṣi, ithaḥ, itha, emi, ivaḥ, imaḥ. With uba before it: where there is guṇa, through the prohibition in the rule "not of iṇ and idhati," the letter a of the prefix is not elided. By the rule "before e, e; and also before the letter e," e replaces the letter a, and what follows is elided: upaiti, upaiṣi, upaimi. Where there is no guṇa, by the rule "the letter a with the letter i becomes e," e appears: upaitaḥ, uyanti, udeti.

ika is in the sense of remembering. The letter ka serves the particular purpose of the rule "of ika too, as of iṇ." It always has adhi before it. An is lost. Guṇa occurs. By the rule "of ika too, as of in," it is changed to ya: adhyeti, adhītaḥ, adhiyanti, adhīṣi, adhīthāḥ, adhyami, adhīvaḥ, adhīmaḥ.

bi is also in the senses of begetting, of being lovely, of abiding and of eating: beti, bitaḥ. By the rule beginning "a root's final i and u before a vowel-initial ending," it is changed to iya: biyanti, beṣi, bīthaḥ, bitha, bemi, bāvaḥ, bīmaḥ.

bha is in the sense of shining: bhāhi, bhātaḥ, bhandi, bhāsi, bhāthaḥ, bhātha, bhāmi, bhāvaḥ, bhāmāḥ, pratibhate. yā is in the sense of obtaining: yāti, yātaḥ, yanti, praṇibāti. bā is in the senses of going and of smell: bāti, bātiḥ, banti, praṇibāti. ṣṇa is in the sense of cutting: snāti, snātaḥ, snanti. śrā is in the sense of cooking: śrāti, śrātaḥ, śranti. drā is in the sense of going in a contemptible way: drāti, drataḥ, dranti. With ni before it: nidrāti, praṇidrātiḥ, praṇīdranti. pā is in the sense of protecting: pāti, pātaḥ, panti. rā and lā are in the sense of taking.

rāti, rāteḥ, ranti; lāti, lātaḥ, lanti. dāp is in the sense of cutting. The letter pa serves the particular purpose of the rule "dā and dhā, other than dāb, are called dā": dāte, dātaḥ, danti. khyā is in the sense of proclaiming: khyāte, khyātaḥ, khyanti. prā is in the sense of filling: prāti, prātaḥ, pranti. mā is in the sense of measuring: māti, mātaḥ, manti. brita: the group ends. These thirteen are bhā and the rest.

bid is in the sense of knowing. An is lost. By the rule "of a light nāmin as penultimate," guṇa occurs. By the rule "before unvoiced sounds, the first, for those that are not ṣiṭ," ta replaces da: bitti, bittaḥ, bidanti, betvi, betthaḥ, bittha, bidmi, bidbaḥ, bebmaḥ. As for the rule "āha for prū in the five, and likewise for pita in the nine": from the statement "aṭ and the rest are optionally made the endings in place of ti and the rest, in their order," aṭ and the rest are to be made the endings, according to their number, in the same way as the nine endings named parasmaipada, ti and the rest, that first arise after vid. In place of ti, aṭ; in place of tas, atus; in place of anti, us; in place of si, thal; in place of thas, athus; in place of tha, a; in place of mi, aṭ; in place of vas, va; in place of mas, ma: they are changed into these. The ṭa of aṭa serves the particular purpose of the rule "of a penultimate a." The la of thal serves the particular purpose of the rule "thal after one ending in ṛ." An is lost. Guṇa of the light penultimate occurs: beda, bedatuḥ, biduḥ. By the rule "before unvoiced sounds, the first, for those that are not ṣiṭ," the first appears: bettha, bidthuḥ, bida, bidpa, bidma.

With sam before it: by the rule "after sam: gam, ṛccha, praccha, spṛśu, bithi, artti and dṛśa," the ātmanepada endings occur: sambitte, sambidāte, sambidate. By the rule "optionally after vetti," ra optionally appears: sambitrate, sambitse, sambidvethe, sambidhve, sambide, sambidvahe, sambidvamahe.

as is in the sense of existing. An is lost: aste. By the rule "the first of asta," the letter a is elided: staḥ, santi. By the rule "of ati, before si," the letter sa is elided: asi, sthaḥ, stha, asmi, sbaḥ, smaḥ. With byati before it: by the rule "here too, optionally, where it is not in the sense of going, of harming, of sound, or of laughing," the ātmanepada endings occur: byatiste, byatisate, byatisate. There, by the rule "before se," because the letter e is what is meant, sa is elided: byatise, byati sāthe. By the rule "the fourths of the dhuṭas become the thirds," the letter da replaces the letter pa: byatidhve. Some say that it is elided, since stating dhve with the third makes no distinction. By the rule "it shall be stated before e," the letter ha replaces the letter sa: byatihe, byatisvahe, byatismahe.

mṛju is in the sense of cleansing. The letter u serves the particular purpose of the rule "svarati, sūti, sūyati, and also after those with the marker ūt." An is lost. The guṇa of the light nāmin as penultimate is ar. By the rule "mārja for mārjja," it is changed to marjji. The mention of i is a conversion together with the case ending, not a vowel. By the rule "ṣa for bhrija and the rest," the letter śa replaces the letter ja. By the rule "after ṣa and the ṭa class, the ṭa class in place of the ta class," the letter ṭa appears: mākṣi, mṛṣṭaḥ. There, as the commentary explains, "it shall be stated as optional before a vowel without guṇa," and so it is changed to mīrjja: mārjjinda, mṛjanti. By the rule "ka for ṣa and ḍha before sa," the letter ka replaces the letter ṣa: mārkṣi, mṛṣṭhaḥ, mṛṣṭha, mārjmi, mṛjvaḥ, mṛjmaḥ.

baca is in the sense of speaking. An is lost. By the rule "ki for the ca class before a dissimilar letter," ki appears. This serves the particular purpose of the statement "the mention of i is a conversion together with the case ending, not a vowel": bakti, bagtaḥ, bacinta. Others, however, all say that vaci is not to be used in the plural of the first person of the parasmaipada vartamānā: bakṣi, bakthaḥ, baktha, bacmi, bacveḥ, bacmaḥ.

Rud and the rest shall now be stated. rudir is in the sense of shedding tears. ñiṣvaba is in the sense of sleeping. ṣaśvasa is in the sense of the breath of life, and so is ana. jakṣa is in the senses of eating and of laughing. An is lost. From the general rule "the augment iṭ at the beginning of a non-sārvadhātuka ending that begins with a consonant other than one beginning with a," and by the rule "after tud and the rest, in the sārvadhātuka," the augment iṭ is added. The letter ṭa serves the particular purpose of the rule "after grahi, lengthening of iṭ when it is not parokṣā." Guṇa of the light penultimate occurs: riditi, rudita, rudanti, rodiṣi, rudithaḥ, ruditha, rudimi, rudivaḥ, rudimaḥ. In an auspicious sense, ṇañ is to be prefixed. Then the letter ña serves the particular purpose of the rule "artha without nañ, when not before si, arthanti": nadodīti. svapitaḥ, svabanti. śvasiti, śvasitaḥ, śvasanti. anati, anitaḥ, antina. By the rule "because iṭ and the rest are mentioned, also at the end of aniti," ṇa replaces na: prāniti. jaṃkṣiti, jakṣataḥ. By the rule "jakṣa and the rest too," it has the name abhyasta, and so, by the rule "after an abhyasta, the na of anti is elided," the letter na is elided: jakṣati, jakṣiṣi, jakṣithaḥ, jakṣatha, jakṣimi, jakṣivaḥ, jakṣimaḥ. These are the five, ruṅ and the rest.

jāgi is in the sense of waking from sleep. An is lost. By the rule "of a vowel ending a nāmin and of a vikaraṇa," guṇa occurs: jāgardi, jāgrataḥ. As before, the letter na is elided. By the rule "ra for the letter ṛ," ra appears: jāgrati, jāgarṣi, jāgṛthaḥ, jagṛtha, jāgarmmi, jāgṛvaḥ, jāgṛmaḥ. daridrā is in the sense of faring badly. Daridrā, jāgṝ and dīdhī: these are, in any case, roots with a single vowel. For roots ending in āt, the marker too is to be established: dāridrāti. By the rule "i for daridrāti," the letter i appears: daritītaḥ. By the rule "the ā of the abhyastas," the letter ā is elided: daridrāti, daridrāsi, daridrīthaḥ, daridrītha, daridrīmi, daridrīvaḥ, daridrīmaḥ.

cakāpri is in the sense of setting ablaze: cakāsti, cakāstiḥ, cakāsati, cakāssi, cakīsthaḥ, cakāstha, cakāsmi, cakāsbaḥ, cakāsmāḥ. śasuḥ is in the sense of instructing: śasti. By the rule "it for the penultimate of śasi before aṇ and consonants," i replaces the letter ā. By the rule "also of śāsi, vasi and ghasi," ṣa appears. After ṣa, the ṭa class appears: śithāḥ, śāsati, śāssi, śīṣṭhaḥ, śīṣṭha, ṣīsmi, śīṣṭhaḥ, śīṣmaḥ. bṛt: the group ends. These are the five, jekṣā and the rest. These are the explanations of the parasmaipada.

cakṣiṅ is in the sense of clear speech. The letter ṅa signifies the ātmanepada endings. The letter i serves to distinguish it, for the rule "khyañ for cakṣi." an is deleted. By the rule "also at the end, of s and k at the beginning of a cluster," the letter ka is elided. After ṣa, the ṭa class appears: āceṣṭe, ācakṣāta, ācakṣate. The letter ka is elided. By the rule "ka for ṣa and dha before sa," ka appears. By the rule "after a conditioning sound, ṣa replaces sa standing in a suffix, a modification or an augment," ṣa appears: ācakṣe, ācakṣātha. The letter ka is elided. By the rule "after ṣa, the ṭa class," ḍha replaces dha. By the rule "the third of the dhuṭ letters," ṣa becomes ḍa: ācaḍhve, ācakṣe, ācakṣvahe, ācakṣmahe.

īr is in the sense of going, and also of stirring. an is deleted: irtte, īrāte, īrate, īraṣe, īrātha, īrdhe, īre, īrbahe, īrmmahe. With sam prefixed: samīrtte. Because it stands in the middle of a word, the repha does not become visarga.

īḍa is in the sense of praising. an is deleted. The ṭa class appears. By the rule "before the voiceless, the first of those that are not sibilants," ḍa replaces the letter ṭa: īḍe, īḍāte, īḍāti. By the rule "also before sa and dhva, of īḍa and jana," there is the augment īṭ. ṣa appears after i: īḍiṣe, īḍāthe. There is the augment iṭ: iḍidhe, īḍi, īḍahe, āḍmahe.

īṣa is in the sense of lordship. By the rule "also of cha and śa," ṣa appears, and also the ṭa class: īṣṭe, biṣātai, īśāte. By the rule "of īśa, before sa," there is the augment īṭ: īśiṣe, īśāthe. Because the rule reads "also before sa and dhva, of īḍa and jana," the word "also" draws in īśa. Therefore for īśa too iṭ comes in when dhve follows: īśidhve, īśi, īśvahe, īśmahe.

āsa is in the sense of extolling. an is deleted: āste, āsāti, āste, āsse, āsāthe. By the rule "before the fourths, the third of the dhuṭ letters," da replaces the letter sa. Since, for letters joined with those of their own class, there is no separate distinction in pronunciation between one letter and more than one, there is no fault even in sa becoming the third. Some say it is elided: ādhve, āse, āsvahe, āsmahe, ubāste, ubhubāste, udāte, ānmate, adhyate, svaste.

āṅ-śāsu is in the sense of wishing: āśāste, āśāsāste, āśāsate, āśāsse, āśāsāthe. As before, sa becomes the third: āśādhve. Some say it is elided: āśāse, āśāshe, āśāsmahe.

vasa is in the sense of covering over: baste, basāhe, basate, basse, basāthe, basadhve, base, basvahe, basmahe.

kasi is in the sense of going and of sharpening. Because it has i as its indicatory letter, there is the augment na: kaṃste, kaṃsāte, kaṃsate, kaṃsse, kansāte. Before a class consonant, the final of that class appears: na replaces the anusvāra: kandhe, kaṃse, kaṃsvahe, kaṃsmahe.

ṇisi is in the sense of kissing. By the rule "na for ṇa," na replaces the letter ṇa: nīṃste, nīṃsāte, niṃsate, niṃsse, niṃsāthe, niṃddhva, niṃsa, niṃsvahe, niṃsmahe. With pra prefixed: there, by the rule "optionally also of nisi, nikṣi and nanda," ṇa appears after r: praniṃste, briniṃste.

ṇiji is in the sense of purifying. an is deleted. By the rule "ki for the ca class before a letter that is not homogeneous," ka appears. Then the anusvāra and the class final: nidke, niñjāte, niñjāte. ki appears. After the conditioning sound, ṣa appears: nidkṣe, niñjathe, nidghve, niñje, niñjvate, niñjmahe.

śiji is in the sense of an indistinct sound: śidkte, śiñjāte, śiñjate.

pṛjī is in the sense of abandoning: prikte, pṛjāte, pṛjate, pṛske, pṛjātha, pṛghve, pṛja, pṛjvahe, pṛjmahe.

pṛcī is in the sense of joining together: pṛkte, saṃpṛkte.

ṣūd is in the sense of a living creature giving birth from the womb. an is deleted. By the rule "for the sārvadhātuka ātmanepada endings other than the latter ones of the pañcamī," there is no guṇa: sūte. By the rule "before a vowel, of what ends in i or u," u is changed to va: subāte, sūbate, sūṣe, sūbāthe, sūdhve, sube, sūbahe, sumahe, prasūte.

śliṅ is in the sense of lying down. Although the absence of guṇa would follow from the rule "for the sārvadhātuka ātmanepada endings other than the latter ones of the pañcamī," guṇa appears by the rule "of śiṅ, in all sārvadhātuka endings": śete, śayāte. By the rule "after śete, ri at the beginning of ante," the repha appears: śerate, śeṣe, śayāthe, śedhve, śeye, śepahe, śemahe.

īṅ is in the sense of reciting, always with adhi prefixed. an is deleted. By the rule "before a homogeneous letter it becomes long, and the following one is elided": adhīte. By the rule "before a vowel, of a root ending in i or u," i is changed to iya. By the rule beginning "before a homogeneous letter, long," it is lengthened: adhīyāte, ādhīyāt, adhīṣe, adhīyāthe, adhīdhve, adhīye, adhībahe, adhīmahe.

dīdhīṅ is in the sense of shining and of playing. bibīṅ has the same sense. Some say these belong to metrical (Vedic) usage. With āṅ prefixed: ādīdhīte. By the rule "ya for e not preceded by a cluster, in a word of more than one syllable," ya appears: īdīdhyāte, adīdhyate, ābebīte, abebyate.

hnuṅ is in the sense of removing: hnute, hnubāte, hnubahe, nihnute, nihnubāte, ābahnute. These are the ātmanepada forms.

dviṣa is in the sense of displeasure. an is deleted. In the singular, guṇa of a light penultimate. After ṣa, the ṭa class appears: dviṣṭi, dviṣaḥ, dviṣanti. By the rule "ka for ṣa and ḍha before sa," ka appears: dvakṣi, dviṣthaḥ, dviṣtha, dviṣmi, dviṣvaḥ, dviṣmaḥ, dviṣṭe, dviṣāṭe, dviṣāthe, dvikṣe, dviṣahe. After ṣa, the ṭa class appears: ḍha replaces the letter dha. The third of the dhuṭ letters appears: ḍa replaces ṣa: dviḍhbhe, dveṣe, dviṣahe, dviṣmahe.

duha is in the sense of milking. an is deleted. Guṇa of a light penultimate. By the rule "gha for an initial," gha replaces the letter ha. By the rule "after gha, ḍha, dha and bha, except after dhā, dha replaces ta and tha," dha appears. The third of the dhuṭ letters appears: ga replaces gha: dobbdhi, dubdhaḥ, duhanti. By the rule "before sa and dhva, the fourth replaces the third of a root whose initial is so and which ends in gha, ḍha, dha or bha," dha replaces the letter da.

By the rule "before the voiceless, the first of those that are not sibilants," ka replaces gha. After the conditioning sound, ṣa appears: dhakṣi, dugdha, dundhaḥ, dehmi, duhvaḥ, duhmaḥ, dugdhe, duhāte, dhukṣi, duhāthe, dugdhve, duhe, duhvahe, duhāhe.

diha is in the sense of accumulating: digdhiḥ, digdhiḥ, dihanti, dhikṣi, digdhe, digdhe, dihmi, dihāḥ, dihmaḥ, degkṣi, dihāte, dehante, dhikṣe, dihāthe, dighve, dihvahe, dihmahe, ubadegdhi. With pra and ṇi prefixed: praṇidegdhe.

liha is in the sense of tasting. an is deleted. In the singular, guṇa of a light penultimate. By the rule "dha for ha," dha replaces the letter ha. By the rule "after gha, ḍha, dha and bha, except after dhā, dha replaces ta and tha," dha replaces ta and tha. By the rule "after ṣa, the ṭa class," ḍha replaces dha. By the rule "before ḍha, ḍha is elided, and the penultimate is also lengthened," ḍha is elided. Just as the elision of ḍha occurs in forms without guṇa, so does the lengthening of the penultimate: ledhi, līdhaḥ, līhanti. ḍha replaces ha. By the rule "ka for ṣa and ḍha before sa," ka replaces dha. After the conditioning sound, ṣa appears: lekṣa, līḍhaḥ, līḍha, lihmi, lihvaḥ, lihmaḥ, līdhe, lihāte, lihate, likṣe, lihāthe, liḍhve, lihe, lihvahe, lihmaho.

ūṇuña is in the sense of covering over. an is deleted. There is guṇa, as in uṇṇoti. Guṇa comes by the option stated for sārvadhātuka endings beginning with a consonant that take guṇa. Vṛddhi comes by the rule beginning "in one view, vṛddhi of ut before an initial consonant." In the singular endings, guṇa, as an exception to vṛddhi, is optional: ūrṇṇoti, ūrṇṇutaḥ, ūrṇṇubanti, ūrṇṇoṣi, ūrṇṇuthaḥ, ūrṇṇutha, ūrṇṇomi, ūrṇṇubaḥ, ūrṇṇumaḥ. When there is vṛddhi: ūrṇṇoti, ūrṇṇauṣi, ūrṇṇaumi. ūrṇṇute, ūrṇṇubāte, ūrṇṇubate, ūrṇṇubaṣe, ūrṇṇubāthe, ūrṇṇudhve, ūrṇṇube, ūrṇṇubahe, ūrṇṇumahe. So it is. It is always preceded by pra. By the rule "a before u," for the sound of pra, ā replaces the letter a, and the following one is elided: prārṇṇoti, prārṇṇauti, prāṇute.

ṣṭuña is in the sense of praising. There is vṛddhi of ut: stauti, studthastu, bante, staṣi, stuthaḥ, stutha, stomi, stubaḥ, stumaḥ, stute, stubāte, stubate, stuṣe, stubāthe, studhve, stuge, stubahe, stumahe. With abhi prefixed: by the rule "after a prefix, for sunoti, subati, syati, stoti and staubhati, also before aṭ and elsewhere," ṣa appears. After ṣa, the ṭa class: abhiṣḍaute, adhiṣṭutaḥ, abhiṣṭuphanti, abhiṣṭute, pariṣṭauti, pariṣṭuti.

bruña is in the sense of clear speech. an is deleted. These are the parasmaipada endings. In the singular endings, by the rule "after bru, iṭ for the first endings," there is the augment īṭ. The letter ṭa serves to distinguish it, for the rule "also iṭ, after iṭ." Guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel and of the vikaraṇas. By the rule "av for o," it is changed to av: prabāti, brutaḥ, brubanti, brabīṣī, brūthaḥ, brūtha, brabime, brubaḥ, brūmaḥ.

From the wording of the rule "After brū, for the first five, and likewise after vid, for the nine, aṭ and the rest are to be made as particles, for ti and the rest, in their order," it follows that after brū, in place of the endings named parasmaipada, ti and the rest, there are the five, aṭ and the rest, in their numbered order. In place of these five, ti, taḥ, anti, si and thas, these five, aṭ, atus, us, thal and athus, are put by particle formation, optionally. And by this special formation brū is changed to āha: āha, āhatu, āhuḥ. For this very reason, by the particle formation, before thal, ta replaces the letter ha: āttha, āhathuḥ, brūtha, brabīmaṃ, brūbaḥ, brūmaḥ, brūte, brubāte, brūbate. These are the forms of both voices.

At the end of ad and the rest, one should state juhoti and the rest, which take guṇa.

hu is in the sense of giving. an is deleted. By the rule "in the sārvadhātuka endings of juhoti and the rest," it is reduplicated, and by the rule "the former is the abhyāsa," it has the name abhyāsa. By the rule "ja for ha," ja replaces the letter ha of the abhyāsa. By the rule "not always, in an operation on what has a prior name, by this," there is no vṛddhi of ut. In the singular endings, guṇa comes by the rule "guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel and of the vikaraṇas": juhoti. The vṛddhi forms are excluded by the specific mention "of juhoti." By the rule "also in the dual and plural, in the parasmaipada," there is no guṇa: juhutaḥ. By the rule "both are abhyasta," it has the name abhyasta. By the rule "after an abhyasta, na of anti is elided," the letter na is elided. Where the change to uv would follow, va alone should be established by the rule "in the sārvadhātuka endings of juhoti": juhvati, juhoṣi, juhutha, juhomi, juhobaḥ, juhumaḥ.

ñibhī is in the sense of fear. ñi is the indicatory letter. ñi as an indicatory letter serves to distinguish it, for the rule "kta, in the senses of wishing, mind and worship." an is deleted. It is reduplicated. By the rule "short," it is shortened. By the rule "the first and third for the second and fourth," ba replaces the letter bha of the abhyāsa. Guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel: bibheti. By the rule "for bhī, it is to be stated optionally," ī appears, by option, before sārvadhātuka endings beginning with a consonant that take no guṇa: bibhītaḥ. By the rule "ya for the letter i not preceded by a cluster, in a word of more than one syllable," ya appears: bibhyeti, bibheṣiḥ, bibhithaḥ, bibhitha, bibhimi, bibhibaḥ, bibhābaḥ, bibhimaḥ, bibhīmaḥ.

hrī is in the sense of shame. an is deleted. It is reduplicated. By the rule "the initial consonant of the abhyāsa is to be left remaining," the meaning is that the initial consonant of the abhyāsa is to be left remaining, that is, preserved, and not elided. It is shortened. Guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel: jihreti, jihvītiḥ. From the wording "not preceded by a cluster," ya does not appear where a cluster precedes. It is changed to iy: jihviyati, jihriṣi, jihrīthaḥ, jihritha, jihrimi, jihribiḥ, jihrimaḥ.

pṛ is in the sense of protecting and of filling. an is deleted. It is reduplicated.

By the rule "a for ṛ," the letter ṛ of the abhyāsa becomes ā. By the rule "also of arti and piparti," i replaces the letter a. Guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel: pibartti, pipṛtaḥ, biprati, biparṣi, bipṛthaḥ, bipṛtha, biparmmi, biphṛbaḥ, bipṛmiḥ. Some think it ends in a long vowel, as pṝ. Yet even for those who hold this, there is no difference where there is guṇa. Elsewhere, by the rule "ur also for a labial penultimate," it is changed to ur. By the rule "long, before a consonant, for the r and v of a nāmin, except in kur and chur," it is lengthened: pipūrttaḥ, piburati, pipari, pipūrthaḥ, bipurtha, piburmmi, bipurbbaḥ, bipurmmaḥ.

ohāka is in the sense of abandoning. The letter ka serves to distinguish it, for the rule "of ohā and praha, when not padhi." The o serves to distinguish it, for the rule "after lū and the rest, and after what has o as its indicatory letter." ja replaces the letter ha of the abhyāsa: jahati. By the rule "before an initial consonant, except dā, of both," the letter i appears: jihitaḥ. As an exception to the general absence of guṇa, by the rule "for jahāti, i is to be stated optionally before sārvadhātuka endings beginning with a consonant," ī appears optionally for jahāti: jahitaḥ, jihītaḥ. By the rule "a of abhyastas," the letter ā is elided: jahati, jahāsi, jahithe, jahīthaḥ, jahitha, jahītha, jahāmi, jahibaḥ, jahībaḥ, jahimaḥ, jahīmaḥ.

ghri is in the sense of causing change and of shining. a for ṛ. By the rule "the ca class for the ka class," the ka class of the abhyāsa becomes the ca class: jaghartti, jaghriti, jighiti. As for the others, it is generally said that they are to be expressed according to usage in ti and the rest.

hri is in the sense of reducing to ash: jaharti, jahritaḥ, jahiti.

ṛ and sṛ are in the sense of going. an is deleted. It is reduplicated. a for ṛ: irtti. By the rule "also of bibasti," ī appears. By the rule "before a dissimilar letter, of the abhyāsa," it is changed to iy. Guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel: iyartti. By the rule "also in the dual and plural, in the parasmaipada," there is no guṇa: iyṛtaḥ. By the rule "ra for ṛ," ra appears: iyarti, iyarśi, iyrithaḥ, iyṛtha, iyrami, iyribaḥ, iyrimaḥ.

sri is in the sense of going: sasartti, sasṛtaḥ, sasrata.

bhasa is in the sense of reproaching and of shining: babhate, babhastaḥ, babhasati.

ki and kita are in the sense of knowing. It is reduplicated. Guṇa of the light penultimate of the root: ciketa, cikati. By the rule beginning "ya for the letter i not preceded by a cluster," ya appears: cekyati. By the rule "the ca class for the ka class," ca replaces ka of the abhyāsa. Guṇa of the root: cikirtti, cikittiḥ, cikitati, ciketsi, cikatthaḥ, cikattha, cikitmi, cikitvaḥ, cikitmaḥ.

tura is in the sense of speed: tutortti, tutūrttaḥ, tuturati.

dhiṣa is in the sense of sound: didheṣṭi, didhiṣṭaḥ, didhiṣati.

dhana is in the sense of grain: didhanti, didhantaḥ, dadhanti.

jana is in the sense of producing: jajanti, jajantaḥ. By the rule beginning "gam, han, jan," the a of the penultimate is elided. By the rule "before a conjunction with the ca and ṭa classes, the ca and ṭa classes for the ta class," ña replaces the letter na: jajñati. With vyati prefixed: by the rule "optionally, not restricted, in the senses of going, harming, sound and laughing," because it belongs to ruca and the rest, the ātmanepada endings apply: byatijajanta, byatijajate, byatijajñate. By the rule "also before sa and dhva, of īḍ and jan," there is the augment iṭ: byatijajñaṣe, byatijajñāṣe, byatijajñāthe, byatijajñidhve, byatijajñe, byatijajanvate, byatijajanmate. These are the parasmaipada forms.

ṇijir is in the sense of cleansing and of nourishing. vijar is in the sense of separation. viṣḷ is in the sense of pervading. an is deleted. It is reduplicated. By the rule "guṇa in the sārvadhātuka endings of niji, vaji and viṣi," there is guṇa of the letter i of the abhyāsa in all forms, that is, before what follows the root. In the singular, guṇa of a light penultimate. By the rule "ki for the ca class before a letter that is not homogeneous," ki appears: nenekti, neniktaḥ, nenejati, nenikṣi, nenikṣaḥ, neniktha, nenijmiḥ, nenijaḥ, nenijmaḥ, nenikṣe, nenijāte, nenijate, nenikṣi, nenijaithe, nenighve, nenije, nenijvehe, nenijmahe. bebekti, bebektaḥ, bebījāti, bebakṣi, bebikte, bebījate, bebijati. bebiṣṭi, bebiṣṭaḥ, bebiṣate, bebiṣa, mepīṣate, bebiṣate.

ḍudāña is in the sense of giving. an is deleted. The vowel of the abhyāsa is shortened: dādāti. By the rule "a of abhyastas," the letter ā is elided. Before the voiceless, the first of those that are not sibilants appears: dattaḥ, dadāti, dadāsi, dhatthaḥ, dattha, dadāmi, dadvaḥ, dadmaḥ, datte, dadāte, dadāete, dvatse, dadāthe, daddhva, dede, dadvaha, dadhmahe. By the rule beginning "na after a prefix," ṇa appears: praṇidadhāti, praṇidhatte. With ti and ad prefixed: by the rule "after āṅ, dāñ, when not in the sense of opening oneself," because it belongs to ruca and the rest, the ātmanepada endings apply: ādatte, āddāte, ādate.

ḍudhāña and ḍubhṛña are in the sense of holding and of nourishing. an is deleted. It is reduplicated. The abhyāsa is shortened. By the rule "the first and third for the second and fourth," da replaces dhā: dadhāti. By the rule "of dadhāti, before ta and tha," dha replaces the letter da: dhattaḥ, dadhāti, dadhāsi, dhatthaḥ, dhattha, dadhāmi, dadhvaḥ, dadhamaḥ, dhatte, dadhāte, dhatsa, dadhāthe. By the rule "the third for the fourths of the dhuṭ letters," the letter da replaces dhā: dadha, dadho, dadhvahe, dadhmahe, bidadhāti, bidhatte, paridadhāte, paridhadte, abidadhāti, abidhatte, saṃdadhati, saṃdhatte, praṇidadhāti, praṇidhatte.

ḍubhṛña is in the sense of holding and of nourishing. an is deleted. It is reduplicated. a replaces the letter ṛ of the abhyāsa. By the rule "i of bhṛñ, hāṅ and māṅ," i replaces the letter a. By the rule "the first and third for the second and fourth," ba replaces bha. In the singular endings, guṇa of roots ending in a nāmin vowel: bibhatti, bibhitaḥ. By the rule "ra for ṛ," ra appears: bibhrati, pibhṛ, bibhāte. These are the forms of both voices.

mā is in the sense of measuring, and also of sound.

ohā is in the sense of going. As before, i appears in the abhyāsa.

By the rule "ī in place of ā, before an initial consonant, except dā, of both," ī replaces the root's ā: mimāte, mimāte. By the rule "a of abhyastas," the letter ā is elided: mimāte, mimīṣe, mimāthe, mimīdhe, mimi, mimībahe, mimīmahe, pramimāte, praṇimimīte. For ohā: jihīta, jihāte, jihīṣe, jihāthe, jetādhve, jihe, jihībahe, jihīmahe. These are the ātmanepada forms. "These are the twenty-four, juhoti and the rest": with this, the ad class and the rest, in which the vikaraṇa is deleted, are complete.

Now div and the rest are to be stated. divu is in the senses of playing, wishing to dedicate, transaction, loveliness, praising, beauty and going. Where an would follow, yan is to be established by the rule "yan after div and the rest." The letter na serves to distinguish it, for the rule "of e before yan." Because of the restriction "and before a vikaraṇa, before an," there is no guṇa of the light penultimate. The penultimate before r and v is long: dībyati, dībyataḥ, dībyanti, dībyasi, dībyathaḥ, dībyatha, dībyāmi, dībyapāḥ, dībyāmaḥ.

ṣibu is in the sense of sewing thread: sībyati, sībyataḥ, sībyanti. By the rule "after pari, ni and vi, for seva, sita, saya, sibu, saha, suṭ, stu and svañja," ṣa appears: pariṣībyati, niṣībyati, bāṣibyati.

śribu is in the sense of going and of drying: śrībyati. ṣṭhibu and kṣibu are in the sense of casting out: ṣṭhibyate, kṣibyati. ṣṇusa is in the sense of eating: snusyati. kṇusu and kṣusu are in the sense of forceful placing and of shining: kṇusyati. nṛtī is in the sense of moving the body: nṛtyati.

trasī is in the sense of dismay. Because it belongs to div and the rest, yan would always follow; but by the rule "also after bhrāsa, bhrāsa, bhrāmu, kramu, tasri, truṭi, leṣi, yasi and saṃyasi," the suffix yan is optional: trasyati. In one view, an is the vikaraṇa for the agent: drasati.

kutha is in the sense of putrefying: kuṣyati. yutha is in the sense of harming: yuthyati. gudha is in the sense of encircling: gubhyati. kṣipa is in the sense of driving away: kṣibyati. puṣpa is in the sense of blossoming: puṣpyati. tima, ṣṭim and ṣṭima are in the sense of becoming fresh: timyati, stimyati, stimyati. brīḍya is in the sense of disputing: bṝḍyati. By the rule "ḍa and la are one," it is optionally la: brīlyati. iṣu is in the sense of going. With pra prefixed: priṣyati, anviṣyati. ṣaha and ṣuha are in the sense of being able: sahyate, suhyate. jṝṣa and jhṛṣa are in the sense of the decline of youth. By the rule "for what ends in ṛt, when there is no guṇa," ir appears. The penultimate before r and p is long: jreryati, dhīryati.

śo is in the sense of making thin. By the rule "of o before yan," the letter o is elided: śyāti, niśyati. che is in the sense of cutting: chyati. ṣo is in the sense of the act of ending: ṣosyati, anusyati. do is in the sense of destroying: dyati, abadyati, praṇityati.

rādha and sādha are in the sense of complete accomplishment. Since radhi applies both with and without an object, after radhi yan becomes the suffix: rādhyati, sādhyati. Otherwise it is like su and the rest: rādhoti. In the sense of growth, this one belongs to div and the rest. Otherwise, from the wording "having div first," this indication of aṣa is intended.

vyadha is in the sense of striking. By the rule "when there is no guṇa, of grahi, jyā, vayi, vyadhi, vaṣṭi, vyaci, pracchi, praṣchi and bhvasji," in samprasāraṇa the letter i stands in place of the letter a. Because of the prohibition "not in samprasāraṇa," it does not become samprasāraṇa again: bidhyati.

puṣa is in the sense of nourishing: puṣyoti. śuṣo is in the sense of drying: śuṣyati. duṣa is in the sense of being altered: duṣyati. śliṣa is in the sense of embracing: śleṣyati. ṣvidā is in the sense of bodily change. The letter ā serves to distinguish it, for the rule "also after what has āt as its indicatory letter": svibyati. kṣudha is in the sense of hunger: kṣudhyati. śudha is in the sense of purity: śudhyati, biśudhyati. ṣidhu is in the sense of being fully pleased: sidhyati, abhisidhyati. radha is in the sense of harming: radhyati. dṛba is in the sense of joy: dṛpyati. dṛpa is in the sense of joy and of release: dṛpyati. muha is in the sense of bewilderment: muhyati. druha is in the sense of wishing to harm: druhyati. ṣṇuha is in the sense of vomiting: snuhati. ṣṇihaḥ is in the sense of joy: snihyati. ṇaśa is in the sense of disappearing: naśyati, binaśyati. These are the eight, radha and the rest, and the five, muha and the rest. vṛt: the group ends.

Now śam and the rest are to be stated. śamu and damu are in the sense of becoming calm. tamu is in the sense of desire. śramu is in the sense of austerity, and also of weariness. bhramu is in the sense of not staying still. kṣamuṣ is in the sense of forbearance. klamu is in the sense of weakness. madī is in the sense of joy. By the rule "long before yan, of śam and the rest," the vowel is lengthened: śāmyati, dāmyate, tāmyati, śrāmyati, biśrāmyati, bhrāmyati, kṣāmyati, klāmyati, mādyati, pramādyati, unmādyati. These are the eight, śam and the rest.

asu is in the sense of throwing: asyati, udasyate. By the rule "after a prefix, optionally for asyati and uhi," the ātmanepada endings apply optionally, as for ruca and the rest: narisyati, narisyati, paryasyate, paryasyati.

yasu is in the sense of striving. By the rule "also after bhrāśa, bhlāśa, bhramu, mu, trasī, truṭi, laṣi, yasi and saṃyasi," the suffix yan is optional: asyati. In one view, an: yasati, saṃyasyati, saṃyasati. After other prefixes, yan always appears: āyasyati, brayasyati.

jasu is in the sense of liberation: jasyati. tasu and dasu are in the sense of setting down: tasyati, dasyati. basu is in the sense of stiffening: basyati. pyuṣ is in the sense of dividing: pyuṣyati. pluśa is in the sense of burning: pluṣyati. bisa is in the sense of driving: bisyati. kuṣa is in the sense of joining: kuṣati. puṣa is in the sense of giving away: puṣyati. muṣa is in the sense of destroying: muṣyati. masī is in the sense of measuring: masyati. luṭa is in the sense of churning: luṭyatri. uca is in the sense of gathering: ucyati. bhṛśu and bhraṃśu are in the sense of falling down. The anuṣaṅga is elided: bhṛśyati, bhraṣyati. briśa is in the sense of obscuring: bṛṣyati. kṛśa is in the sense of making thin: kṛśyati. ñitṛṣa is in the sense of thirst: triṣyati. tuṣa and hṛṣa are in the sense of satisfaction: tuṣyati, hriṣyati. kupa, krudha and ruṣa are in the sense of anger: kubyati, krudhyati, ruṣyati. ḍipa is in the sense of throwing: ḍipyati. ṣṭūpa is in the sense of raising up.

stūbyati. gupa is in the sense of agitation: gupyati. yupa, rupa and lupa are in the sense of bewilderment: yupyati, rupyati, lubyati. lubha is in the sense of attachment: lubhyati. kṣubha is in the sense of being shaken throughout: kṣubhyati. ṇabha and tudha are in the sense of harming: nabhyati, tubhyati. klidu is in the sense of becoming moist: klidyati. ñimidā is in the sense of moistening. By the rule "of midi," there is guṇa: medyati. ñiṣvidā is also in the sense of release: ṣvidyati. ṛdhu is in the sense of growth: ridhyati. gṛdhu is in the sense of longing: gṛdhyati. These are the sixty-four, puṣa and the rest. vṛt: the group ends. These are the explanations of the parasmaipada.

ṣūd is in the sense of living beings giving birth: sūyate, prasūyate. dū is in the sense of torment: dūyate, through dejection of mind. dīd is in the sense of wasting away: dīyate, abadīyate. dhiṅ is in the sense of disrespect: dhīyate. mīd is in the sense of harming: mīyate, pramīyate. rīṅ is in the sense of listening: rīyate. līṅ is in the sense of joining: līyate, bilīyate. ḍād is in the sense of going: ḍīyate, uḍḍayate. briṅ is in the sense of covering: brīyate. These are sū and the rest, which have oṭ as their indicatory letter.

pīṅ is in the sense of drinking. māṅ is in the sense of measure: pīyate, māyate, praṇimīyate. īṅ is in the sense of going: īyate. prīd is in the sense of joy: prīyate. janī is in the sense of being born. There is yan. By the rule "jā for jani before the vikaraṇa," it is changed to jā: jāyate, prajāyate, upajāyate, bijāyate, as of a sprout.

biḍā is in the sense of shining: dībyite. pūrī is in the sense of satisfying: paryate. jūrī is in the sense of being agitated: jñāryate. tūrī is in the sense of speed and of living: tūryate. dhūrī, guri and ghūrī are in the sense of harming: dhūryate, gruryate, gyuryate. śūri is also in the sense of certainty: śāryate. cūdri is in the sense of burning: cūryate. tapa is in the sense of lordship: tapyate. Some, holding the view that reads the word "optionally" here in another position, give by the rule "also in the sense of lordship, because it belongs to div and the rest": tapati. Or else pritu is in the sense of holding: prityate. Some say that in one view it applies because it belongs to bhū and the rest. Others do not accept "or else britu." In that case, optionally: prityate.

kliśa is in the sense of torment: kliśyate. kāśṛ is in the sense of shining: kāśyate. bāśri is in the sense of sound: pāṣyate. pada is in the sense of going: padyate, prabadyate, ābadyate, saṃpadyate, ubpadyate, dibadyate, praṇibadyate. bida is in the sense of lack: bibyate. bida is in the sense of existing: bidyate. budha is in the sense of understanding: budhyate, prabudhyate, bibudhyate. yudha is in the sense of fighting: yudhyate. With anu, rudha is in the sense of desire. The meaning is that rudha preceded by anu, in the sense of desire, belongs to div and the rest: anurudhyate. mana is in the sense of knowing: manyate. an is in the sense of breath: anyate. yuja is in the sense of samādhi: yujyate. sṛja is in the sense of separating: srajyati, utsijyate, bisrajyate. laśa is in the sense of becoming small: liśyate. These are the ātmanepada forms.

śaka and mṛṣa are in the sense of forbearance: śakyati, śakyate, mṛṣyati, yīti. In senses other than forbearance, because it belongs to su and the rest, the suffix is nu: śaknoti. Because inclusion in the list is not obligatory: barimiṣyati, a parasmaipada form. Likewise pratimṛṣyabati.

īśucir is in the sense of becoming pure. The letter i serves to distinguish it, for the rule "not for ḍī, śī, what has ī as its indicatory letter, and what optionally takes iṭ, except pati and niṣkuṣa." The letter i and the repha serve to distinguish it, for the rule "also after what has ir as its indicatory letter": śucyati, śucyati.

ṇaha is in the sense of binding: nahyati, nahyate, saṃnahyati, saṃnahyati. By loss of a letter, the a of api is optionally elided: pinahyati, abinahyati, pinahyati, apinahyate, braṇinahyati, praṇinahyate.

rañja is in the sense of dyeing. There is yan. By the rule "when there is no guṇa, of those that do not have it as an indicatory letter," the anuṣaṅga is elided: rajyati, rajyate. śala is in the sense of reviling: śabyati, śabyate. "These are the explanations": with this, div and the rest, which take the vikaraṇa yan, are complete.

bhrājṛ, ṭubhrābṛ and ṭubhlābri are in the sense of shining. bhramu is in the sense of moving. kramu is in the sense of stepping. drasī is in the sense of weariness. cuṇa, cuṭa, kuṭa and ṭuṭa are in the sense of cutting. laṣa is in the sense of beauty. yisu is in the sense of striving. By the explanation "also bhrāsi, bhūsa, bhlāsa, bhramu, kramu, klamu, drasī, truṭi, laṣa, yasi and saṃasi," the suffix is yan. bhrāsi and bhlāsi take the ātmanepada endings; the others take the parasmaipada endings: bhrāsyate, bhrāsate, bhlāsyate, bhlāsate. By the rule "long before yan, of śam and the rest," the vowel is lengthened: bhrāmyati. Here too, in one view: bhrāmati. By the rule "of krama, in the parasmaipada," the vowel is lengthened: krāmyati, kramati, drasati, drasati. In one view, an, because it belongs to tud and the rest: truṭati, truṭati. Because it belongs to tud and the rest, there is no guṇa: laṣyati, laṣyati, abhilaṣati, yasyati, saṃyasyati, asati, saṃasati. Where another prefix is shown, the suffix is always yan.

Now su and the rest are to be stated. ṣuña is in the sense of pressing out. Where an would follow, nu is to be established by the rule "nu after su and the rest." Because of the restriction "also before a vikaraṇa, before an," at the end of the vikaraṇa there is no guṇa of a root ending in a nāmin vowel. In the singular endings, there is guṇa by the definition of what ends in a nāmin vowel: sunoti, sunutaḥ. The change to uv being blocked, va appears by the rule "the letter va for nu of the vikaraṇa": sunvinta, sunuṣi, sunuthaḥ, sunutha, sunomi, sunuba, sunumaḥ. By the rule "the letter u is optionally elided before va and ma," the letter u is optionally elided: sunvaḥ, sunmaḥ. Because it has ña as its indicatory letter, it takes the ātmanepada endings: sunote, sunvāte, sunate, sunuṣe, sunvāthe, sunudhre, sunve, sunahe, sunubahe, sunmahe, sunumahe.

ṣiña is in the sense of binding: sinoti, sunutaḥ, sinvanti, sunute, sinvāte, sinvate. śiña is in the sense of sharpening: śinoti, śinutaḥ, śinanti, śinute. ḍubiña is in the sense of throwing: minoti, minute. caña is in the sense of gathering.

ciniti, cinutaḥ, cinute, bracinoti, braṇicinoti, braṇicinute. stiña is in the sense of covering over. By the rule "After ra, ṣa and the letter ṛ, a na that is not final becomes ṇa, even when vowels, ha, ya, va, the ka class and the pa class stand between," it is ṇa: stṛnoti, stṛṇute. kṛña is in the sense of harming: kṛnote, kṛnute. briña is in the sense of obscuring: bṛnoti, bṛnute. dhuña is in the sense of shaking: dhunati, dhurmyote. These are forms in both voices. ṭudhu is in the sense of tormenting: ḍunoti. hi is in the sense of going, and also of increasing: hinoti. Preceded by bra: by the rule "by hi, nu and mī," it is ṇa: brahinoti. bṛ is in the sense of joy: bṛnoti. smṛ is also in the sense of moving: smṛṇoti, smṛṇutaḥ, smṛnvanti, smṛnoṣi, smṛnuthaḥ, smṛṇutha, smṛṇomi, smṛṇubaḥ, smṛṇvaḥ, smṛṇumaḥ, smṛnmaḥ.

asli is in the sense of pervading: āsnoti, prāsnoti, byāsnoti. śaklṛ is in the sense of being able: śaknoti, śaknutaḥ. Here, on the general rule "of one not preceded by a conjunct," by the rule "and also of the nu of the vikaraṇa," it only undergoes the change to uv: prāsnubanti, śaknupanti. rādhva and sādha are in the sense of being fully accomplished: rādhnoti, sādhnoti. tika, tiga, ṣagha, rikṣi, kṛbi, ciri, jiri, dās and druha are in the sense of harming: tiknoti, tignoti, saghnoti, rikṣnoti. Because it has i as its indicatory letter, there is an augment.
