From the statement "It should be explained that dhinvi and kṛṇvi also become dhi and kṛ," it becomes kṛ: kṛnoti, ciriṇoti, jiriṇoti, dābsnoti, druhnoti. ñidhṛṣā is in the sense of boldness: dhṛṣṇoti. dabbhu is in the sense of deceit. The nasal (anuṣaṅga) is elided: dabhnotvi. ṛdhu is in the sense of increasing: ribhnoti. dhibi and tripa are in the sense of pleasing. Because it has i as its indicatory letter, there is an augment. By the rule "dhinvi and kṛṇvi also become dhi and kṛ," it becomes dhva: dhinoti, trisnoti. These are parasmaipada forms.

aśū is in the sense of pervading: aśnute. ṣṭigha is in the sense of going everywhere: ṣṭighnute. These are ātmanepada forms. śru is in the sense of hearing. By the rule "śru also becomes śri," there is the suffix nu and also the change to śri: śriṇoti. By the rule beginning "after so, gami," it takes the ātmanepada endings: saṃśriṇute. akṣū is also in the sense of gathering; it is listed because of a different usage. By the rule "optionally after akā," the suffix nu is optional. After ṣa it becomes the ṭa class: na becomes ṇa: akṣṇoti, akṣati. takṣu and tvakṣu are in the sense of paring. By the rule "also after takṣā," the suffix nu is optional: takṣṇoti. Preceded by nir: the ra becomes visarga, and by the rule "before ta and tha, sa," the visarga in turn becomes the letter sa: nistakṣṇoti, nistakṣati. Because of the words "in the sense of paring," this does not happen in another sense: saṃtakṣati vāgbhiḥ, which means "he rebukes the pupil." By the statement "These, śru and the rest, which occur among bhū and the rest, are stated here in the context of forming nu," the roots with the vikaraṇa nu, su and the rest, are complete.

Now tuda and the rest will be stated. tuda is in the sense of afflicting. The vikaraṇa is an. When guṇa would be obtained by the rule "also before a vikaraṇa, before an," by the rule "before the an of tud and the rest," it does not become guṇa: tudati, tudataḥ. Then the procedure for the letters in sandhi with a: tudanti, tudasi, tudathaḥ, tudatha. The a is lengthened before va and ma: tudami, tudāba, tudāmaḥ. tudate, tuṅate, tudante. ṇuda is in the sense of driving: nudati, nudate. diśi is in the sense of utterly relinquishing: diśiti, diśate, ādiśati, saṃdiśati, nirdiśati, uddiśati, upadiśati.

bhraja is in the sense of roasting. By the rule "grahi, jyaṃ and the rest," there is saṃprasāraṇa: the ra becomes the letter ṛ. The dhuṭ letters become the third of their class: the letter sa becomes the letter da. Joined with the ṭa class or the ca class, the ta class becomes that class: the letter da becomes the letter ja: bhṛjjati, bhṛjjate. kṣipa is in the sense of driving away: kṣīpati, kṣipati, nikṣipati, ākṣipati, praṣyikṣipati, adhikṣipati. Preceded by prata and abhi: because the marking of the group is not invariable, by the rule "kṣipa after prati, abhi and ati," it takes the parasmaipada endings: pratikṣipati, ebhikṣipati, atikṣipati. kṛṣa is in the sense of plowing: kṛṣati. mucḷ is in the sense of releasing. The vikaraṇa is an. By the rule "after the vowel of muc and the rest, before the ān of the vikaraṇa, a letter is added as an augment," the letter nā is added as an augment, and then come the anusvāra and the final nasal of the class: muñcati.

luslṛ is in the sense of cutting: lumbati, lumbate. pidḷ is in the sense of finding: binte, bindati. lipa is in the sense of smearing: limbati, limbate. ṣica is in the sense of sprinkling: siñcati, siñcate. By the rule beginning "sthā, seni, sedhati," it is ṣa: abhiṣiñcati, abhiṇiñcate, paraṣiñcate, niḥṣiñcite, niḥṣiñcate duḥ, ṣiñcate. These are the explained forms. kṛtī is in the sense of cutting: kṛnadati. khida is in the sense of crushing utterly: bindati. piśa is in the sense of parts: biśati. These are the eight beginning with luca.

ripī and riṣī are in the sense of going: ripati, reṣati. dhi is in the sense of holding. By the rule beginning "the first vowel ending in the letter i or the letter u," i becomes iya: dhiyati. kṣi is in the sense of dwelling and of going: kṣiyatī. ṣū is in the sense of impelling. u becomes uva: suvati. After a prefix, by the rule beginning "sunoti, suvati," it is ṣa: abhiṣuvati, bariṣuvati. braṣcu is in the sense of cutting. By the sūtra "grahi, jya and the rest," there is saṃprasāraṇa: pṛścati. richa is in the sense of going, of the faculties, of dissolution, and of becoming embodied. The cha is doubled. By the rule "the first, before an unvoiced letter," it is the first: ṛcchati.

kṛ is in the sense of scattering. gṝ is in the sense of swallowing down. By the rule "a root ending in ṝ, where there is no guṇa, takes ir," it becomes ir: kirati, girati. By the rule "optionally before a vowel," the ra becomes la: gilati. By the rule "of gira after apa," because it belongs to ruca and the rest, it takes the ātmanepada endings: avagirate, avagilete. By the rule "after saṃ, in the sense of making a promise," because it belongs to ruca and the rest, it takes the ātmanepada endings: sigirate, sigilate. In a sense other than making a promise: sigirati, saṃgilote. This is the meaning of the statement "optionally in the sense of declaring and of affirming."

uchi is in the sense of separating: ucchati. jarcca and jharjha are in the sense of rebuking and of threatening: jarccati, carccati, jarjhati. tvaca is in the sense of covering over: tvacati. ṛca is in the sense of praising: ṛcati. ubja is in the sense of the sky: ubjati. ujjha is in the sense of letting go: ujjhate. lubha is in the sense of bewilderment: lubhati. ripha is in the senses of speaking, fighting, reviling, harming and giving: ripati. tripha and trinā are in the sense of satisfaction. By this rule, "of those that do not have i as their indicatory letter, where there is no guṇa," the nasal (anuṣaṅga) of trinpha and the rest is not to be elided before an: triphati, trimphati. tupa, tuppa, tupha, tunpha, ṛpha and ṛnpha are in the sense of harming: tubati, tumbati, tuphati, tumpati, ṛphati, rimphati. dṛpha, drinpha and micca are in the sense of extreme affliction: driphati, dṛmphati, miccheti. gupha, gunma and pṛbhī are in the sense of knotting: guphati, guṃphati, dṛbhati. cvitī is in the sense of harming: crihati. ubha and unbha are in the sense of filling: ubhati, umbhati. śubha and śunbhra are in the sense of being beautiful: śubhati, śumbhati. These are the eight beginning with trinpha.

bidha is in the sense of accomplishing. juḍa and śuna are in the sense of going. priḍa and mriḍa are in the sense of making happy: bidhati, juḍati, śunti, pṛḍati, mṛḍati. pṛna is in the sense of pleasing: pṛnate. mṛn is in the sense of harming: mṛnati. druṇa is also in the sense of going: drunati. tuṇa is in the sense of crookedness: tunati. puṇa is in the sense of virtue: puṇati. puṇa is in the sense of making a promise: muṇati. kuṇa is in the sense of sound and of helping: kuṇate. ghuṇa and ghurṇṇa are in the sense of wandering: ghuṇati, ghurṇṇati.

praccha is in the sense of wishing to know. Because it belongs to grahi and the rest, there is saṃprasāraṇa: pṛcchati. By the rule "after a modification of ād, nu, in the sense of asking," because it belongs to ruca and the rest, it takes the ātmanepada endings: āpṛcchate. By the rule beginning "after saṃ, gam, ṛccha and pṛccha," it takes the ātmanepada endings: saṃpṛcchati. sṛja is in the sense of releasing: sṛjati, sasṛjati, utsijati, bisṛjati. ḍumasja is in the sense of purifying. By the rule "the dhaṭ letters become the third," the letter sa becomes the letter da. By the rule "the ta class joined with the ca and ṭa class," da becomes ja: majjati, nimajjata. rujo is in the sense of breaking: rujati. bhujo is in the sense of crookedness: bhujati, bibhujeti, udbhūjati. chupa and spṛśa are in the sense of touching all over: chupati, saṃspṛśati, upaspṛśati, spṛśati. ruśa and riśa are in the sense of harming: ruśati, riśate. liśa and biccha are in the sense of going: liśati, bicchati. biśa is in the sense of entering: biśati, prabiśati, upabiśati. Preceded by ni: by the rule "biśa after ni," it takes the ātmanepada endings: nibiśate. mṛśa is in the sense of being wholly angered: mṛṣati, āmṛśati. śadḷ is in the sense of decaying. By the rule "śadi before an," because it belongs to ruca and the rest, it takes the ātmanepada endings. By the rule "śadi becomes śīya," it changes to śīya: śīyate. ṣadli is in the sense of moving about. By the rule "sādi becomes sīda," it changes to sīda: sīdati. śura is in the sense of lordship and of blazing: surati. kura is in the sense of sound: kurati.

tṛhū, ṣṭihū and trinhū are in the sense of harming. The nasal is elided: tṛhati, strihati, tṛhati. iṣu is in the sense of desiring. By the rule "of gam, iṣu and yam, cha," ṣa becomes cha. It is doubled. Before an unvoiced letter it becomes the first: icchati. miṣa is in the sense of contending: miṣati. kila is in the sense of whiteness and of play: kilati. ila is in the sense of going: ilati. dila is in the sense of amorous gesture: hilati. śila, sila and uchi are in the sense of being high: śilati, silati. Because it has i as its indicatory letter, there is an augment: uñcati. tila is in the sense of oiliness: tilati. cila is in the sense of clothing: cilati. cala is in the sense of sporting: calati. bila is in the sense of splitting: bilati. ṇila is in the sense of obtaining: nilati. mila is in the sense of joining: milati. likha is in the sense of writing: likhati. kuṭa is in the sense of crookedness: kuṭati. puha and luṭa are in the sense of joining together: puṭati, luṭati. kunca is in the sense of contracting: kucati, saṃkucati, utkucati. byaca is in the sense of deceiving. By the sūtra "grahi, jya and the rest," there is saṃprasāraṇa: the letter ya becomes the letter a: bicate. guja is in the sense of sound: gujati. guḍa is in the sense of protecting: guḍati. ḍipa is in the sense of throwing: ḍipati. cuṇa, cuṭa, chuda and truṭ are in the sense of cutting: cuṇti, cuṭati, churati, bicchurati, truṭate. Forms made with yan are also heard.

sphuṭa is in the sense of opening out: sphuṭati. muṭa is in the sense of throwing about and of gaining: muṭati. ruṭa is in the sense of fighting: ruṭati. juḍa is in the sense of binding: juḍati. kaḍa is in the sense of being intoxicated: kaḍati. kṛḍa and ghaggha are in the sense of laughing: kṛḍite. kuḍi is also in the sense of what has been done: kuḍati. ghuṭa is in the sense of striking back: ghaṭiti. tuḍa is in the sense of tearing: tuḍati. thuḍa, sthuḍa, buḍa and bruḍa are in the sense of covering over: thuḍati, sthuḍati, buḍati, bruḍati. huḍa is in the sense of gathering: huḍahaṃ. sphura is in the sense of radiating: sphurati, basphurati. sphula is also in the sense of gathering: sphulati. ṇū is in the sense of praising. u changes to uva: nuvati. dhu is in the sense of shaking off: dhubati. gu is in the sense of voiding excrement: gupati. dhū is in the sense of going and of stability: dhrūpati. These are the explained parasmaipada forms. guri is in the sense of effort: gurati, apagurati. In the sense of understanding it is elided: ungurate. bhrari. kuda is in the sense of sound: kubate. Here the subgroup ends. These are the thirty-five beginning with kuṇa.

srid is in the sense of exerting oneself. By the rule "i, where there is no guṇa before an," it is the letter a. By the rule "the letter ṛ becomes ra," it is ra. By the rule beginning "before the first vowel," it changes to aya: priyate. mṛda is in the sense of giving up life. By the rule "mriṅ also after the āśiṣ and the adyatanī," because it belongs to ruci and the rest, it takes the ātmanepada endings: mṛyate, anumṛyate. bharttari. driṅ is in the sense of disrespect: diyate, ādriyate. phiṅ is in the sense of remaining: dhiyate, abadhiyate. juṣī is in the sense of joy and of serving: juṣate. obijā is in the sense of fearing and of shaking: bijate, udrijate. olajī and opji are in the sense of being ashamed.

lajate. By the rule "the dhuṭ letters become the third," the letter sa becomes the letter da. By the rule beginning "the ta class, with the ca and ṭa class," the letter da becomes the letter ja: lajjate. With the statement "these are ātmanepada forms," the roots with the vikaraṇa an and without guṇa, tud and the rest, are complete.

Now rudha and the rest will be stated. rudhiṅ is in the sense of obstructing. When an would be obtained, by the rule "after the vowel of rudha and the rest, the sound na follows," the procedure for the sound na is carried out. By the rule beginning "after ra, ṣa and the letter ṛ," na becomes ṇa. By the rule "after gha, dha and bha, but not after dha, ta and tha become dha," ta and tha become dha. By the rule "the fourth letters of the dhuṭ become the third," the letter dha becomes the letter da: ruṇaddhi. By the rule "the final of the vikaraṇa of rudha and the rest is elided," the letter a of the sound na is elided. Then come the anusvāra and the final nasal of the class: runddhaḥ, runddhanti. Before an unvoiced letter it becomes the first: ruṇatsi, runddhaḥ, runddhaḥ, runadhmi, rundhvaḥ, rundhāḥ, runddhe, rundhāte, rundhe, ruṃtse, rundhāthe, ruṃddhe, rundhe, rundhāhe, rundhmahe.

bhidir is in the sense of splitting: bhinatti, bhinttaḥ, bhindanti, bhinatsi, bhinnāḥ, bhintthaḥ, bhinadmi, bhintaḥ, bhīndmaḥ, bhintte, bhindāte, bhindata, bhindse, bhindathe, bhinddhe, bhinde, bhindvahe, bhindmahe. chidir is in the sense of dividing in two: chinatti, chinttaḥ, chindanti, chinatsi, chintthaḥ, chinttha, chindmi, chindvaḥ, chindmaḥ, chindhe, chindāte, chinadate, ucchentti, ucchinde, bicchinatti, bicchintte, abacchanatti kṣatra, abacchintte, byabacchinatte, byabacchintti, paracchinatti, paracchintti, ācchintti, ācchintte. Before aṅ and mā the cha is always doubled.

ricir is in the sense of scattering. By the rule "before a letter not like the cha class, ki," it is ki: riṇakti, ridktaḥ, rañcinti, riṇikṣi, ridkthaḥ, ridktha, riṇacmi, riñcvaḥ, riñmaḥ, riṅñi, riñcāto, riñcate, phiṛṇakti. In the sense of binding the belly: barddakoṣaḥ, biriktte. bijir is in the sense of being separated: binakti, biṅktaḥ, biñjanti, binakṣi, bidkthaḥ, bidktha, binajmi, biñjvaḥ, binjmaḥ, bidkte, biñjāte, biñjate. kṣudir is in the sense of impurity: kṣuṇatti, kṣuṇattiḥ, kṣurdanti, kṣuṇatsi, kṣuntha, kṣuntha, kṣuntha, kṣunadmi, kṣundvaḥ, kṣundmaḥ, kṣuntto, kṣundāte, kṣuntate.

yujir is in the sense of joining: yunakti, yudkta, yuñdanti, yukṣi, yudkthaḥ, yudktha, yunājmo, yuñjvaḥ, yuñjaḥ, yudkṭe, yuñjāte, yuñjate. By the rule "yujira, after a prefix ending in a vowel and the rest, where it is not of yajñapātra," it takes the ātmanepada endings: prayudkṣe, abayudkte, niyudkte. In the context of ajñadātva this does not happen: yajñapratam prayunakti. ucchṛdir is in the sense of blazing and of playing: chriṇatti, chrinttaḥ, chrindanti, chriṇaṇsi, chrindaḥ, chrinttha, chrinttha, chriṇadmi, chrindveḥ, chrindmeḥ, chintte, chrindāte, chrindate. utṛdir is in the sense of harming and of disrespect: trinatti, tṛnttaḥ, trindanti, triṇans, tṛndthaḥ, tṛndtha, tṛnadmi, tṛndva, tṛndma, tṛndha, tṛndhāti. These are forms in both voices. kritī is in the sense of encircling: kṛṇatti, kṛnttaḥ, kṛndanti, kṛnatme, krintthaḥ, krinttha, kṛṇatmi, kṛntaḥ, kṛttmaḥ.

śiṣḷ is in the sense of going apart. It is preceded by vi. After ṣa it becomes the ṭa class: biśinaṣṭiḥ, biśiṣṭaṃ, biṣiṣaṃnti, biśinakṣi, biśiṣṭhaṃ, biśiṣṭhe, biśinaṣmi, biśiṣvaṃḥ, biśiṃṣmaḥ. biṣḷ is in the sense of grinding to powder: binaṣṭi, biṣṭaḥ, biṣaṃnati, binakṣi, biṣṭheḥ, biṣṭhaṃ, binaṣmi, biṣaṃ, briṣmaṃḥ. bhañjo is in the sense of crushing completely. There is the sound na. It is ki. By the rule "after the sound na and also after the vikaraṇa," the nasal (anuṣaṅga) is elided: bhanakti, bhadktaḥ, bhañjanti, bhanakśi, bhadkta, bhanijma, bhañḍmaḥ, bhañḍma. bhuja is in the sense of protecting and of eating: bhunakti, bhurktaḥ, bhuñjanti, bhunakṣi, bhudkthaḥ, bhudktha, bhunajmi, bhuñjvaḥ, bhuñjmaḥ. By the rule "bhuji in the sense of food," it takes the ātmanepada endings: bhudkte, bhuñjate, bhuñjate.

tṛha and sihasi are in the sense of harming. There is the sound na. By the rule "after tṛha, iṭ, before the vikaraṇa, severally," there is the augment iṭ. The letter ṭa serves to mark an augment. By the rule "ha becomes ḍha," it is dha. By the rule "after gha, ḍha, dha and bha, but not after dha, ta and tha become dha," it is dha. After ṣa, the ta class becomes the ṭa class. ḍha is elided before ḍha, and the penultimate is also lengthened. The letter a with the letter i becomes e, and what follows is also elided: tṛṇeḍhi, tṛṇḍhaḥ, tṛhantri, tṛṇekṣi, tṛndhaḥ, tṛṇḍha, tṛṇejmi, tṛhvaḥ, trijmaḥ. hisi: because it has i as its indicatory letter, there is an augment. There is the sound na. By the rule "after the sound na and after the vikaraṇa," the nasal is elided: hinasti, hisanti, hiṃsanti, hinassi, histhaṃḥ, hiṃstha, hinasmi, hiṃsvaḥ, hismaḥ. undi is in the sense of moistening. As before, na is elided: unatti, untāḥ, undanti, yunṣsi, untthaḥ, unttha, unadmi, undvaḥ, ubhmaḥ. andu is in the sense of making clear, concealing and going. It is preceded by vi: byanakti, byadktaḥ, byeñjakti, byanakti, byadkthaḥ, byadktha, byanjmi, byañjmaḥ, byañjmaḥ. tancu is in the sense of contracting: tanakti, tadktaḥ, tañcanti. obijva is in the sense of fearing and of shaking: binakti, bidktaḥ, bañjanti, binakti, bidktha, bidktha, binajmi, biñjvaḥ, biñjmaḥ. pṛjā is in the sense of discarding: pṛnakti, pṛdktaḥ, priñjanti. pṛcī is in the sense of meeting: pṛṇakti, pṛdktaḥ, pṛñcanti. These are parasmaipada forms.

ñi indhī is in the sense of blazing. There is the sound na. It is elided as before: inddhe, inddhāte, indhate, indse, indhathe, indhāe, indhe, indhvete, indhmahe. khiṅ is in the sense of weakness: khintte, khindāte, khindate. bida is in the sense of discerning: bintte, bindāte, bindate. With the statement "these are ātmanepada forms," the roots with the vikaraṇa of the sound na, ru and the rest, are complete.

Now tan and the rest will be stated. tanu is in the sense of extending. When an would be obtained, by the rule "after tan and the rest, u," u is to be formed. By the rule beginning "in the singular forms, of those ending in a nāmin vowel," there is guṇa: tanoti, tanutaḥ, tanbante, tanoṣi, tanuthaḥ, tanutha, tanoni, tanubaḥ, tanumaḥ. By the rule "the letter u is optionally elided before va and ma," the letter u is elided.

tanvaḥ, tanmaḥ, tanute, tanvāte, tanate, tanuṣe, tanvāthe, tanudhve, tanve, tanubahe, tanumahe, tanvahe, tanvahe, setanati, setanute, bitanoti, bitanute, ātanoti, ātanute, byātanoti, byātanote. ṣuṇu is in the sense of giving: sanoti, sanutaḥ, sanvante, sanoṣi, sanuthaḥ, sanuthe, sanomi, sanubaḥ, sanumaḥ, sanvaḥ, sanmaḥ, sanute, sanvāte, sanvate. kṣaṇu and kṣiṇu are in the sense of harming: kṣaṇoti, kṣaṇutaḥ, kṣaṇvanti, kṣanoṣi, kṣaṇuthaḥ, kṣaṇutha, kṣaṇomi, kṣaṇubaḥ, kṣaṇumaḥ, kṣatute, kṣaṇvāte, kṣaṇvate. ṛṇu is in the sense of going: ṛṇoti, ṛṇutaḥ, ṛṇuntiḥ, ṛṇute, ṛṇvāte, ṛṇate. tṛṇu is in the sense of eating: tṛṇoti, tṛṇutaḥ, tṛṇvanti, tṛṇute, tṛṇvāte, tṛṇvate. ghṛṇu is in the sense of blazing: ghṛṇoti, ghṛṇuteḥ, ghṛṇvanti, ghṛṇute, ghṛṇvate, ghṛṇvete. Some say that, by the characteristic of being close to tan and the rest, the rule "of karoti" applies. By the rule "also of ṛṇu and the rest, as of karoti," there is guṇa: arṇṇote, arṇṇoti, tarṇṇati, tarṇṇuti, gharṇṇuti, gharṇṇuti.

ḍukṛña is in the sense of doing. The suffix is u. By the rule "of karoti," there is guṇa: karoti. By the rule "in all roots, where there is no guṇa, the a becomes the letter u," the letter a becomes the letter u: kurutaḥ. By the rule "the letter u becomes va," it is va: kurvanti, karoṣi, kuruthaḥ, kurutha, karomi. By the rule "always after karoti," the letter u is elided: kurvaḥ, kurmaḥ, kurute, kurvāte, kurvate, kuruṣe, kurvāthe, kurudhve, kurve, kurvahe, kurmahe. By the rule "kṛña after anu and pari," it takes the parasmaipada endings: anukaroti, parikaroti. By the rule "the suffix after the sound vi," it takes the ātmanepada endings: bikurute. By the rule "after a vowel, also when it has no object," it takes the ātmanepada endings: bikurute. These are the explained forms. vanu is in the sense of requesting: vanute. manu is in the sense of understanding: manute. These are ātmanepada forms. The roots with the vikaraṇa u, tan and the rest, are complete.

Now krī and the rest will be stated. ḍakṝña is in the sense of selling goods. When an would be obtained, by the rule "after krī and the rest, nā," nā is to be formed. Guṇa would be obtained by "also before a vikaraṇa," but by the restriction "only before an," it does not become guṇa: krīṇāti. By the rule "of both, before an initial consonant, where it is not dā, the letter ī," it is ī: kṝṇītaḥ. By the rule "of the vikaraṇa of krī and the rest," the letter i is elided: krīṇānti, krīṇasi, krīṇīthaḥ, krīṇītha, krīṇemi, krīṇībaḥ, krīṇīmaḥ, krīṇīte, krīṇāte, krīṇāte, krīṇīṣi, krīṇīthe, krīṇīdhve, krīṇve, krīṇībahe, krīṇīmahe. By the rule "krīña after pari, vi and ava," it takes the ātmanepada endings: barikrīṇīte, bikrāṇīte, apakrāṇīte. prīña is in the sense of satisfying, and also of beautifying: prīṇīte, prīṇīta, prīṇaṃnti, prīṇāte, priṇāta, prīṇati. śrīña is in the sense of cooking: śrīṇāti, śrīṇāte. skuña is in the sense of going everywhere: skuṇāti, skunīte. ṣiña and ṣuña are in the sense of binding: sinīta, sinīte, sunāti, sunīte. knuña is in the sense of sound: knunāti, knunīte. mīña and druña are in the sense of harming: mīnāti, mīnīti. By the rule "of henu, mīna and the rest," it is ṇa: pramaṇīto, pramiṇīti, druṇāti, druṇīte. pūña is in the sense of purifying. By the rule "pū and the rest are shortened," it is short: punāti, pūnīte, punanti, punīte. luña is in the sense of cutting: lunāti, lunīte. stṛña is in the sense of covering: stṛnāti, stṛṇīte. kṛña is in the sense of harming: kṛnāti, kriṇīti. prīña is in the sense of obstructing: bṝṇatī, bṝṇīti. dhuña is in the sense of shaking: dhunāti, dhunīte. graha is in the sense of taking up. By the sūtra "grahi, jyā and the rest," there is saṃprasāraṇa: it is the letter ṛ. By the rule beginning "after ra, ṣa and the letter ṛ," na becomes ṇa: gṛhṇate, gṛhṇite. These are the explained forms.

śṝ and spri are in the sense of harming: śrīṇāte, spriṇete. pri is in the sense of protecting and of filling: pṛṇāti. blī and pri are in the sense of obstructing: bliṇāti, bṛtāte. bhṝ is in the sense of rebuking: bhṝṇāti. dṝ is in the sense of splitting: dṛnāti. jṝ is in the sense of the loss of youth: jṛṇāti. nṛ is in the sense of conduct: ṇṛṇāti. gṛ is in the sense of sound: gṛṇāti. jya is in the sense of the loss of youth. By the sūtra "grahī, jyā and the rest," there is saṃprasāraṇa. By the rule "of those ending in ma, it is lengthened," it is long. By the rule "pū and the rest are shortened," it is short: jināti. ṛ is in the sense of going: ṛṇāti. ṝ: ṛṇatī. lī is in the sense of joining: lyināti. lyi as well: lyināti. Here the subgroup ends. pū and the rest are twenty-two; lū and the rest are twenty-one.

brī is in the sense of obstructing: brīṇāti. bhrī is in the sense of filling: bhrīnāti. ṇabha, tubha and kṣiṣa are in the sense of harming: nibhnāti, tubhnāti, kṣiṣṇāti. ñjā is in the sense of understanding. By the rule "also of jñā," it changes to jā: jānāti. By the rule "jā in the sense of denying," it takes the ātmanepada endings: jānīti. By the rule "after saṃ and prati, when it is not in the sense of remembering," it takes the ātmanepada endings: saṃjānīti, pratijānīte. banda is in the sense of binding. The suffix is nī. The nasal (anuṣaṅga) is elided: badhnati. śanthi is in the sense of releasing and of delighting severally: śrāthnati, śarānā. mantha is in the sense of churning: mathnate, bramathanti. grantha is in the sense of stringing together: grathnāti. kuntha is in the sense of afflicting thoroughly: kuthnāti. mṛṅ is in the sense of being fine: mṛdnāti. mṛḍa is in the sense of happiness: mṛdnāti. gudha is in the sense of anger: gudhāti. kuṣa is in the sense of drawing out: kuṣṇāti, nikuṣṇāti. kṣubha is in the sense of being agitated all over. Because this too is mentioned, it is ṇa in ṣubhnā and the rest: kṣudbhāti. kliśu is in the sense of harming: kliśnāti. aśa is in the sense of eating: aśnāti. udhasa is in the sense of height: dhasnāti. iṣa is in the sense of repetition: iṣṇāti. biṣa is in the sense of separating: biṣṇāti. pruṣa and bluṣa are in the sense of moistening, washing and filling.

pruṣṇāti. puṣa is in the sense of flourishing: puṣṇāti. muṣa is in the sense of stealing: muṣṇāti. khaba is in the sense of springing up from decay: khabnāti. By the rule "also in the fifth of che bdag, śa becomes uṭ," it is una. The letter ṭa serves to specify the rule "after the letter a, the vṛddhi of uṭ." By this very rule there is vṛddhi: the letter u becomes the letter o. By the rule "o with au, before the letter au," it is o, and what follows is also elided: khaunāti, khaunātiḥ, khonanti. These are parasmaipada forms. bṛd is in the sense of honoring properly: bṛṇīte. With the statement "this is an ātmanepada form," the roots with the vikaraṇa nā, krī and the rest, are complete.

By the statement "It should be explained that after stanbhu, stunbhu, skanbhu, skunbhu and sku there is also nu," there is the suffix nu, and also nā. The nasal (anuṣaṅga) is elided: stabhnāti, stabhnoti, stubhnāti, stubhnoti, skabhnāti, skabhnāoti, skubhnāti, skubhnāoti. These four, āḍhā, are roots that come from the sūtra. skuña is in the sense of instructing. It belongs to krāo and the rest: skunāti, skunoti, skunīti, skunuti. Krī and the rest are complete.

Now cura and the rest will be stated. cura is in the sense of stealing. By the rule "also after cura and the rest," there is the suffix in. The letter n serves to specify the rule "before in, ic and aṭ, the a of the penultimate is lengthened, and the nāmin vowels also take vṛddhi." The penultimate also takes its guṇa. By the rule "where cori stands, these are roots," it takes the designation of a root. There is yan. There is guṇa. It changes to aya: corayati, carayataḥ, corayanti. citi is in the sense of remembering. Because it has in as its indicatory letter, there is an augment: cintayati, cintayataḥ, cintayanti. yatri is in the sense of contracting: yantri, yati. sphuḍi is in the sense of jesting thoroughly: sphuṇḍayate. lakṣa is in the sense of viewing and of marking with signs: lakṣayati. bhakṣa is in the sense of eating: bhakṣayati. Because the penultimate is the penultimate of the penultimate, the letter a is not lengthened. kuṭi is in the sense of speaking untruth: koḍayati. laḍa is in the sense of attending closely. By the rule beginning "the a of the penultimate," it is lengthened, the letter ā: lāḍayati, ullāyati. By the rule "laḍi in the sense of rolling the tongue," it has m as its indicatory letter. By the rule "those with m as indicatory letter are shortened," it is short: the letter ā becomes the letter a: laḍayati. ñimadā is in the sense of moistening: medayati. olaḍi is in the sense of sowing: laṇḍayati. jala is in the sense of instructing definitely: jalayati, ujjalayati. bīḍa is in the sense of pressing together: bīḍayati. In the sense of one of the pair udmapa: bīdmapati, udbiḍayati, niṣbiḍayati, upapīḍayati, svaṭṭayati. ūrja is in the sense of strength and of giving life: ūrjjayati. cuṭa, cuṭa and kuṭṭa are in the sense of cutting: coṭayati, choṭiyati, ācchoṭayati, kuṭṭayati. puṭa is in the sense of becoming small: phoṭayati. muḍa is in the sense of grinding to powder: moṭayati. spiṭa and ṣuṭa are in the sense of disrespect: sbeṭayati, suṭṭayati. ghaṭṭa is in the sense of moving: ghaṭṭayati, uṭṭaṭṭeyate. chada and khaṭṭa are in the sense of covering over: chādayati, khaṭṭayati. śatha and śvātha are in the sense of refining and of going: śāthayati, śvāthayati. ṣaṭṭa, tuji, piji and basi are in the sense of living, strength, giving and dwelling: saṭṭayati, tuñjayati, piñjayati, pinsayati. śvalka and balka are in the sense of speaking: śvalkayati, balkayati. svatta, kabi and lathi are in the sense of going: svarttayiti, khamsayati, panthayati. kṣapi is in the sense of being beautiful: kṣamsayati. piccha is in the sense of striking: biccheyati. tāḍa is in the sense of beating all over: tāḍayate. khaḍa, khiḍi and kaḍi are in the sense of splitting: khāḍayati, khaṇḍayati, kaṭāyati. nakka, dhakka and pasi are in the sense of perishing: nakkayati, dhakkayati, baṃsayati. cakka and cukka are in the sense of afflicting: cakkayati, cukkayati. busta is in the sense of honoring and of dishonoring: pustayati. kṣala is in the sense of cleansing: kṣalayati. tala is in the sense of establishing firmly: dalayati. tula is in the sense of making mad: tolayati. dula is in the sense of hanging up: dolayati. cula is in the sense of sinking: colayati. kṣaji is in the sense of living by a group: kṣañjayati. māna is in the sense of worship: mānayati. cuda is in the sense of urging on properly: codayati. kala, pila and ḍipa are in the sense of throwing: kalayati, pilayati, ḍepayati. pala is in the sense of protecting: pālayati. lūṣa is in the sense of harming: lūṣayatai. śurṇa is in the sense of discarding: śurṇayati.

jñāpa: by the rule "it also has m as its indicatory letter," because it has m as its indicatory letter, it is shortened: jñāpayati. Otherwise: jñāpayati. jñā is only in the sense of understanding. By the rule "in, in the causative," and by the rule "of att, hrī, blī, rī, knu, īkṣma, ī and those ending in āt, the ending pa; pa is elided, and the nāmin vowels also take guṇa," it has the ending pa. By the rule "jñā in the senses of killing, delighting and sharpening," where it has the designation of having m as its indicatory letter, by the rule "those with m as indicatory letter are shortened," it is short. In the sense of killing: byadho mṛgān jñapayati. yama is also in the sense of serving. The letter ar signifies that it has m as its indicatory letter: yamayati. In a sense other than serving: yāmayati. byama is in the sense of exhaustion: byābayati. pūla, pasta and biḍa are in the sense of gathering: pūlayati, pustayati, paṇḍiyati. phunsa is in the sense of crushing manifestly: pusayati. ṭaki is in the sense of binding: ṭaṅkayati, niṭaṅkayati. dhūṣa is in the sense of adorning: dhūṣayati. kīṭa is in the sense of binding: kīṭayati. cūṇa is in the sense of contracting: cūṇayati. pūja is in the sense of worship: pūjayate. arkka is in the sense of praising: arkkayati. śutha is in the sense of laziness: śothayati. śuthi is in the sense of drying: śuṇṭhayati. juḍa is in the sense of sending: joḍayati. garjja and mārjja are in the sense of sound: garjjayati, mārjjayati. baci is in the sense of speaking at length: prapañcayati. tija is in the sense of sharpening: tijayati. kṝta is in the sense of proclaiming. By the specific instruction "kti of kārtti and iṣa," and by the rule "the penultimate of ir and ur," it is long: kīrttayati. partta is in the sense of cutting and of completing: bardhayati. halapa is in the sense of speaking clearly: hlāpayati. cuṭi is in the sense of cutting: cuṇḍayati. ila is in the sense of faith: elayati. By the rule "the letter a of a prefix is elided before the it and ot of a root," the letter a is elided.

brelayati. jasu is in the sense of harming: jāsayati. tatri is in the sense of holding the womb: tantrayati. matri is in the sense of speaking in secret: mantrayati. basta and garbdha are in the sense of eating: bastayati, gandhayati. hikka is in the sense of harming: hikkayati. niṣka is in the sense of measuring: niṣkayati. lala is in the sense of desiring: lālayati. snuṇa is in the sense of contracting: hūṇayati. tūṇa is in the sense of filling up: tūṇayate. yakṣa is in the sense of worshipping: yakṣayati. kutsa is in the sense of reviling: kutsayati. kūṭa is in the sense of pressing hard: kūṭayati. carcca is in the sense of reading: carccayati. cukka is in the sense of holding: cukkayate. ama is in the sense of illness: āmayati. caṭa is in the sense of splitting: cāṭeyati, ucchāṭayati. ghuṣira is in the sense of proclaiming abroad: ghoṣayati. mokṣa is in the sense of eating: mokṣayati. rakka is in the sense of tasting: rākkayati.

Now the roots ending in a are to be stated. katha is in the sense of a continuous stream. The affix is in. By the rule "a too is elided," the letter a is elided. By the rule "a substitute for a vowel, conditioned by what follows, is treated as the original with respect to a preceding operation," the letter a is to be regarded as still standing in its place in the form. Therefore, because the penultimate remains the penultimate of a, the penultimate is not lengthened: kathayati. bara is in the sense of desiring: barayati. gaṇa is in the sense of counting: gaṇayati. śatha and śvatha are in the sense of explaining correctly: śathayati, śvathayati. phaṭa and paṭa are in the sense of tying a knot: paṭayati, baṭayati. raha is in the sense of abandoning: rahayati. stana, gada and ṅaba are in the sense of sound: stanayati, gadayati, debayati. bata is in the sense of going: patayati. paṣa applies where there is no prefix: paṣayati. svara is in the sense of censuring: svarayati. raca is in the sense of exerting oneself: racayati. kala is in the sense of going, and also of counting: kalayati. caha is in the sense of stirring up: cahayati. mahā is in the sense of worshipping: mahayati. śāri, kṛpa and śvatha are in the sense of weakness: śārayati, kṛbayati, śvathayati. spṛha is in the sense of wishing to obtain: spṛhayati. śūca is in the sense of slander: śūcayati. goma is in the sense of smearing: gomayati. kumāra is in the sense of playing: kumārayati. śīla is in the sense of practicing: śirayati, upaśīlayati, bariśīlayati. sāma is in the sense of conciliating: sāmayati. bela is in the sense of indicating the time: belayati. bata is in the sense of happiness and of serving: vātayate. gabeṣa is in the sense of seeking: gabeṣayati. bāsa is in the sense of attending upon: bāsayati. nebāsa is in the sense of covering over: nibāsayati. bhāja is in the sense of the act of separating: bhājayati. sabhāja is in the sense of joy and of serving: sabhājayati. ūna is in the sense of diminishing: ūnayati. dhāna is in the sense of sound: dhvanayati. kūṭa is in the sense of burning: kūṭayati. keta, śrāma, kuna and guṇa are in the sense of ease: ketayati, śrāmayati, kunayati, kuṇagati. tena is in the sense of theft: stenayati. These are parasmai forms. From ā garbha on, they are ātmane forms. pada is in the sense of going: padayate. grihe is in the sense of grasping: gṛhayate. mraga is in the sense of seeking: mṛgayati. kuha is in the sense of wonder: kuhayate. śūran is in the sense of being heroic and overpowering: śūrayate. thūla is in the sense of growing: thūlayate. artha is in the sense of requesting: arthalati, brarthayate. satra is in the sense of continual play: satrayate. garbba is in the sense of pride: gatbayarte. These are ātmane forms. sūtra is in the sense of releasing completely: sūtrayati. mūtra is in the sense of destroying utterly: mūtrayati. rūṣa is in the sense of roughness: rūṣaṃyati. pāra and tāri are in the sense of completing an action: bārayati, tīdāyati. sagrāma is in the sense of battle: saṅgrāmayati. In the same way, up to this point, these are the forms of the present tense (vartamānā) seen for the agent.

By the rule "the ātmane endings are for the state and the object," the ātmane endings also occur for the state and for the object. By the rule "also for the state and the object," stated generally, and by the rule "yaṇ after all roots," the affix yaṇ comes about. The letter ṇ serves the distinction made in the rule "not where there is an anubandha ṇ, nor before ce, kṛ and yita." Here, this holds up to the end of the affix āpaya. Where gopāya stands, yaṇ is added. By the rule "a too is elided," the letter a is elided: gopāyyate, dhūpāyyate, vicchabote, panāyyate, pānāyyate, ṛtībete.

Roots ending in ā: ḍudañ is in the sense of giving. dāna is in the sense of giving. deṅ is in the sense of protecting. do is in the sense of cutting into pieces. By the rule "for roots ending in a diphthong, ā, where there is no vikaraṇa," it becomes ā. By the rule "dā and dhā, other than dāp, are bā," it receives the name dā. By the rule "ī for dā, mā, gāyati, pibati, sthā, syati and jahāti before a consonant," it becomes ī: dīyate. Likewise ḍudhāñ and dhaṭa: dhīyate. Because dāp and dīp do not receive the name dā, they do not become ī: dāyate. meṅ is in the sense of giving in exchange. mā is in the sense of measuring: mīyate. kai, gai and rai are in the sense of sound: gīyate. pā is in the sense of drinking: pīyate. pā is in the sense of protecting: pāyate. sthā: sthīyate. ṣo is in the sense of bringing to an end: apasīyate. ohāk: hīyate. But from hād: hāyate; elsewhere it does not become ī. yā is in the sense of attaining: yāyate. bhā: bhāyate. snā: snāyate. glai, because it ends in a diphthong, becomes ā: glāyati. mlai: mlāyata. trai: trāyati. kai: kāyati. rai: rāyati. dhyai: dhyayati. hveñ becomes ā. By the sūtra "of svapi, vaci, yaji and so on," there is samprasāraṇa, and by the rule "of those ending in a nāmin vowel," it is lengthened: hūyate.

Roots ending in i: aciñ and the rest. The affix is yaṇ. By the rule "of those ending in a nāmin vowel," it is lengthened: cīyate. ji: jīyate. kṣa is in the sense of wasting away: kṣīyate. hi: hīyate. prābīyate. After kami, iṅ is the kārita. By the rule "kārita of kāmi is elided where there is no ām, iṭ or vikaraṇa," the kārita is elided: kāmyate.

Roots ending in ī: ṇeñ is in the sense of obtaining: nīyate. ḍī: ḍīyate, uḍīyate. śīd: by the rule "before ya, ī becomes aya," it is changed to aya: śabeta.

Roots ending in u: ṣuñ, because it ends in a nāmin vowel, is lengthened: sūyate. ṣṭuñ: stūyate. duñ: dūyate. Roots ending in u: bhū is in the sense of being: dhūyate, anudhūyate. lūñ: lūyate. pūñ: pūyate. Roots ending in ṛ: ḍukṛñ is in the sense of doing.

By the rule "before ya in yaṇ and āśiṣ," the letter i is an augment. By the rule "r of the letter ṛ," it becomes r: kṛyate. hṛñ: hriyate. dṛñ: ādṛate. bṛñ: ābriyate. ṛ is in the sense of going. smṛ and dhyai are in the sense of thinking. By the rule "guṇa of atri and of the first member of a reduplication," there is guṇa: aryate, smaryāte. jāgṛ: by the rule "before ya in yaṇ and āśiṣ," there is guṇa: jāgaryate.

Roots ending in ṝ: by the rule "ir for what ends in ṝ where there is no guṇa," it becomes ir. Before the r and v of a nāmin vowel, the penultimate is long. kṝ: kṛryate. jṝ: jṛryate. gṝ: gṝryate. prā: biryate. śṝ: śriryate. tṝ: triryate. stṛ: stiryate. pṝ: by the rule "ur also for a labial penultimate," it becomes ur. Before r and v the penultimate is long: pūryata.

Roots ending in a consonant: śiṣyate, bhikṣyate, pacyate, gamyate. khanu is in the sense of digging through. ṣanu is in the sense of giving. jānī is in the sense of being born. dhāṭapa. By the rule "of khani, sani and jan," while it still applies, the explanation "optionally before ya" gives ā, not the fifth letter of the class: khāyate, khanyate, sāyate, sanyate, jāyate, janyate. By the rule "for tanoti too, before yaṇ, it is to be stated as optional," it optionally becomes ā: tāyate, tanyate. grahā: by the sūtra "grahi, jyā and so on," there is samprasāraṇa, that is, the letter ṛ in place of the r: gṛhyate. jyā: by the rule "of those ending in a nāmin vowel," it is lengthened: jīyate. vyadha: bidhate. From the prohibition "not where there is samprasāraṇa," where samprasāraṇa has been done, it does not undergo samprasāraṇa again. baśa: uśyate. byaca: micyate. praccha: pṛcchate. baścu: pṛṣcyate. bhrasja: bhṛjjyate. ñiṣapa: by the rule "of svapi, vaci, yaji and the rest, in yaṇ, parokṣā and āśiṣ," there is samprasāraṇa: supyate. bruñ: by the rule "vaci for brū," it is changed to vaci. vaca is in the sense of explaining: ucyate.

yajaḥ, bapaḥ and baha,
beñ and byeñ, and likewise hvaati,
bada and basa, and śvayati:
these are the nine, yaja and the rest.

yaja: ijyete. basa: upyate. baha: uhyate. beñ, because it ends in a diphthong, becomes ā. There is samprasāraṇa, and by the rule "of those ending in a nāmin vowel," it is lengthened: ūyata. byeñ: bīyate. hveñ: hūyate, āhūyate. badā: yuryate. basa: by the rule "also of śāsi, vasi and ghasi," it becomes ṣ: uṣyate. ṭuośvi: śūyate. as is in the sense of being. By the rule "bhū for asti," it is changed to bhū: bhūyate. cakṣi: cakṣiṅ is changed to khyāñ: khyāyate, ākhyāyate. aja: by the rule "bī for aji," it is changed to bī: bīyate. ūha is in the sense of conceptual thought, with saṃ before it. Some say, "it is short after a prefix of ūhi": samuhyate. Here it is in the sense of applying the naṃ of bahi. Because śransu and dhvansu do not have an it as anubandha, the anuṣaṅga is elided: śrabyate, dhasyate. kapi: bākambyate. madi: mantyate. baci is in the sense of speaking at length: prapañcate. śāsu is in the sense of instructing. By the rule "it for the penultimate of śāsi before an and before a consonant," it becomes ya. By the rule "also of śrāsi, vasi and ghasi," it becomes ṣ: śiṣyate. But from āśāsti, āśābyaste: the distinction is in the statement "only where the object is itself the agent." As it is said:

When the object, even as it is being acted upon,
brings itself to completion
by its own qualities as object and agent,
know that agent as the object-agent.

By the rule "the object-agent is treated like the object," it takes the object, and there are ātmane endings for the state and the object: pacyate odanaḥ, the rice cooks by itself alone; bhidhyate kuśūla, the granary splits by itself alone. The rule "the object-agent is treated like the object" blocks what it would otherwise receive by being treated like the object. For duha, the procedure of the ātmane endings is to be applied for the agent: dughe gauyayāmepa. Likewise, by the rule "praise and bowing, by itself," there are ātmane endings for the agent: prastuti gauḥ svayameba, namate daṇṭaḥ svayanamepa. kuṣa is in the sense of pulling out. rañja is in the sense of dyeing. By the rule "it should be stated that for kuṣa and raja there are parasmai endings only," there are parasmai endings: kuṣyahi pātaḥ svayameba, rajyate basti sva a ba me ba. Because ati is not an anubandha, the anuṣaṅga is elided. Here it is because yaṇ goes with the ātmane endings: kuṣyate padaḥ svayamepa, rajyata baste svayamega.

This concludes the first section, showing the domain of the vartamānā, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown.

Now the saptamī is to be stated. By the rule "the saptamī also for accomplishing and so on," the saptamī comes about. As before, there is an, and also guṇa and the change to aya. By the rule "of the sound yā too, in the saptamī," the place of the sound yā is changed to the letter i. Before the letter i, the letter a is also elided, with yeta following: jayata, jayete. By the rule "iyam and iyus for yām and yus," the yam and yus that follow the letter a become iyam and iyus: jayeyuḥ, jayeḥ, jayete, jayeta, jayebaya, jayeba, jayema. In the ātmane endings: bijaata, bijayeyātī, bijayerana, bijayethāḥ, bijayeyāṃthām, bijayedhām, bijayeya, bijayebahi, bijayemahi. It is the same everywhere. ad and the rest: adyāta, adyātām, adyuḥ, hanyātāṃ, hanyuḥ. In the ātmane endings: āghīha, aghrīyātām, āghrīrana. psāyāta. By the rule "also before the saptamī," there is no guṇa: bityata, saṃbidāti, syatā, syatā, syātam, syaḥ. Because the word "also" has a plural sense, it becomes ṣ: abhiṣyāta. mṛja: mṛjyāta. bacyāta, rudyāta, svabyāta, jagṛyāta, daritriyāta, cakṣasyāta, śiṣyāta. These are parasmai forms. cakṣiṅ: byācakṣita, samīrīta, iḍīta, iśīta, āsāṃta, āśīsīta, śayīta, adhiīta, nihūbīte. These are ātmane forms.

dīdvaṣyātā, dviṣyātāṃ, diṣyuṃḥ, dvaiṣīta, dviṣīyātām, paiṣīrana. duha: duhāti, duhātī, duhyuḥ, duhīta, duhīyātām, duhīran. diha: didyāti, dihyātai, dihyuḥ, dihīta, dīhīyātāṃ, dihīrimalaṃha, lihyāta, lihyātāṃ, lihyuḥ, lihīha, lihīāhāṃ, lihīrin. prorṇṇu: yāta, prortuyātām, prorṇṇuyuḥ, prorṇṇuyuṃ, prorṇṇutriyātī, prorṇṇubārina. stuyāta, stuyātī, stuyuḥ, stupītā, tubīyāti, stubīrina. brūyata, brūyātā, brūyuḥ, brūbītaṃ, brūbāṃyātāṃ, brūbīrina. These are the explained forms.

hu: juhuhuyata. bī: bibhīyata. From the statement "it is to be stated as optional for bhī," it optionally becomes i: bibhīyāta. jihrīyāta, piprīyāta. ohāk: where it would become i, by the rule "elision in the saptamī of jihāti," the letter ā is elided: johyuta, johyātā, jehyuṃḥraṃ. ispriyāta, samisprāta, dīdhīṣyāta, dādhanyātā, jajanyāta. These are parasmai forms. nenijyāta, nenijīta, pebijyāta, bebijyīta, bebiṣyāta, pebiṣīta. ḍudāñ: dabyatā, dadīta, dadhyatā, daṃdhāta, bibhrīyāta, bibhrīta. These are forms of both voices. mādamimīta. ohāṅ: jihīta. These are ātmane forms. The same holds for ad and the rest. dibu: bībyāta, sībyeta. ṣuñ: sunuyāta, sunīta, bṛṇuyāta, bṛṇīta. These are forms of both voices. ṭuṭu: ḍunuyāta, hinuyāta, āsnuyāta, dhinuyāta, kraṇuyāta, śṛṇuyāta. ḍutuda: tudeta. rudhari: rundhāta, rundhīta. tanu: tanuyāta, tanrīta. ḍukṛñ: by the rule "also before ya," the letter u is elided: kuryāta, kurbbita, kṛṇīyāta, kṛṇīta, coryata.

In the sense of inviting: iha bhuñjita. In the sense of calling upon: ehāsīta. In the sense of requesting: māṇapakaṃ madhyābayeta. In the sense of asking properly: kinnu khalu bhyo byākaraṇam adhīyīya, utacchindaḍa dhīyīya. In the sense of seeking: labhiya bhikṣī.

For the state and the object there are ātmane forms: jīyeta, adhāyeta, sthīyeta, sthīyeta, śabyeta, bathota, gamyeta, duhyeta. In the same way, the others too are to be seen.

This concludes the second section, showing the domain of the saptamī, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown.

Now the pañcamī is to be stated. By the rule "the pañcamī for permission," the pañcamī comes about. As before, there is an, together with guṇa and the change to aya. ja: atu. From the statement "in blessing, tātaṇ optionally for tu and hi," there is tātana. By the rule "not where there is an anubandha ṇ, nor before ca, krī and yita," there is no guṇa. Then: jayātāta, jayatām, jayantu. By the rule "of hi after a, but not after hanti," hi is elided: jayā, jānaṇa, jayatāt, jayatām, jayata, jayani, jayāpa, jayāma. In the ātmane endings: bikayatām. By the rule "after a, i for the first letter of ātām and āthām," it becomes the letter i. The letter a with the letter i becomes e, and what follows is also elided: bajiyatām, bijiyantām, bijayasva, bijayethām, bijayadhām, bijayai, bijayābahai, bijayamahai. It is the same everywhere.

ad: attu, tātaṇa, atatāta, attama, adantu. By the rule "dhi for hi after hu and a dhuṭ": addhi, tātaṇa, attata, attām, atta, adāni, adāba, adām. hana: hantu, tātaṇa. By the rule "not where there is an anubandha ṇ, nor before ca, kri and yita," there is no guṇa. By the rule "of hanti before a dhuṭ, in all the roots," na is elided: hatāt, hatām. The penultimate is also removed, and gha comes in: ghāntu. hanti. By the rule "ja before hi," it is changed to the letter ja. There, from the prohibition "but not after hanti," the hi is not elided: jahi, tātāṇa, hatāta, hate, hata, hanāni. By the rule "parasmai also in the dual and the plural," in the parasmai endings, because the later pañcamī endings have guṇa, the penultimate is not elided: hanāba, hanama. In the ātmane endings: āhatām, āghnatām, āghāntām, āhāsva, āghāthām, āhadhvam. By the rule "for all the roots, in the ātmane, where it is not the later pañcamī," there is the pañcamī in the ātmane endings. Because the later endings are not without guṇa, the penultimate is not elided: āhanai, āhanābahe, āhanāimahe.

dveṣa: dveṣṭu, tātaṇa, dveṣḍāta, dviṣṭām, dviṣantu. By the rule "also before hi," there is no guṇa. dhvi is the state. It becomes the third letter of the dhuṭ class, and also the ṭ class: dviḍḍhai, tātaṇa, dviṣṭata, dviṣṭama, dveṣāṇi, dveṣaba, dveṣāma, dveṣṭāpa, dveṣātama, dviṣātama, dvikṣā, dviṣāthami, dviṣḍhama, dveṣa, dveṣābahai, dveṣāmahai. duha: dogdhu, dugdhāma, duhanu, dugdhi, tātaṇa, dugdhāta, dugdhama, dugdha, dugdhāma, duhatāma, duhitāma, dukā. liha: liḍhu, tātaṇa. ḍh is elided and the penultimate is lengthened: līḍhāta, līḍhāma, līhantu, liḍhaṃ, tātaṇa, līḍha, lehīna, lehāba, lehāma, liḍhama, litātāma, lihātama, likṣva. diha: degdhu, tātana, digdhāta, digdhām, dihanutu, digdhi, tāhaṇa, dighata, digdhama, digdha, digdhama, dihātāma, dihatāma, dikṣvaṃ. cakṣiṅ: ācaṣṭama, ācakṣātāma, ācakṣatraṃ. īttama, īṭṭāma, īḍātama, īḍātāma, īḍaṣva. īśa: īṣḍāma, īśātāma, īśatāma, īśiṣva. āsa: āstāma, āsātāma, āsatāma, āssva. śāsa after āṅ: āśāstama, āśāsātāṃ, āśāsatā, āśāsspa. basa: bastam. ṣūṅ: prasutāma, prasubātāma, prasubatāma, prasūṣva, prasubāthāṃ, prasūdhvaṃ. By the rule "for sūti, before the pañcamī," there is no guṇa: prasubai, brasubābatai, brasubāmahe. dīdhīṅ: ādīdhītāma, ādīdhyātāma, ādīdhyātāma, ādīdhīṣva, ādīdhyāthāṃ, ādīdhīdhvama. By the rule "also of dīdhī and vevī," there is no guṇa.

īdīdhyai, īdīdhyābahai, īdīdhyāmahai. In the same way: ābebītāma, ābebyātāma, ābebyatāma, abebīṣva. yu: yautu, tātaṇa, yutāta, yutāma, yubantu, yuhi, hātaṇa, yutāta. iṇa: etu, tātaṇa, itāt, ētāma, yantu, ehi, tātaṇa, itāta. i: idhītām, adhīyātām, adhīmatāṃ, adhāṣva. ik: adhyetu, tātaṇa, adhītāt, idhātāṃ, adhīyantu. bī: bettu, tātaṇa, bītāt, bītām, bīyantu. bā: bātu, tātaṇa, bātāt, bātām, bāntu, bāhi, tātaṇa, bātāt. bida: bettu, bīdāṃ karotu, tātana, bittāna, bittāta, bittāma, bidantu, bidhī, tātaṇa, bīttāt, bittāma, bitta, bidāni.

as is in the sense of being: astu, tātaṇa, stāt, stāma, santu. By the rule "after asti," dhi comes about. By the rule "e for dā and asti, and elision of the reduplication too," the letter s becomes the letter e: edhi, tātaṇa, stāta, stam, sta, āsāni, āsāba, asāma.

mṛjū: mārṣṭu, tātaṇa, mṛṣṭata, mṛṣṭam, mṛjantu, mārjjantu. Because it belongs to bhīja and the rest, it becomes ṣ, and after ṣ it becomes the ṭ class: mṛḍḍhi, tātaṇa, mṛṣṭāta, mṛṣṭam, mṛṣṭa, mārjāni, mārjāba, mārjāma. baci: baktu, tātaṇa, bagtata, baktām, bacantu. The c class becomes k, and then the third letter: bagdhi, baktāta, baktam, bakta, bacāna, bācāka, bacāma. ruda: roditu, tātaṇa, ruditāta, ruditāma, rudantu. It also takes in: prāṇitu, tātaṇa, prānitāt, prāṇitama, prāṇantu. jakṣa: jikṣitu, tātaṇa, jakṣitāt, jakṣitāma, jakṣītu. jāgṛ: jāgrartu, tātaṇa, jagrihātāta, jāgrittama, jagratu. dirdrātu, tātaṇa, daridritāt, daridratām, daridratu. śāsu: śāstu, tātaṇa, śīṣṭata, śīṣṭām, śāsatu. By the rule "śā for śāsti," it becomes dhi, and is also changed to śā: śādhi.

hu: juhotu, tātaṇa, juhutāt, juhutāṃ, juhutu. By the rule "dha for hi after hu and a dhuṭ," dhi comes about: juhudhi. ohāk: jahātu, tātaṇa, jahītāta, jahītāma, jahetu. Before hi too, it is said to become ā and ī: jahāhi, jahīti, jahīti. ḍudāñ: ḍudātu, tātaṇa. By the rule "a of the reduplicated forms," the letter ā is elided, and it becomes the first letter: dattāt, dattāma, dadatu. By the rule "e for dā and asti, and elision of the reduplication too," it becomes e, and the reduplication is elided as well: dehi, tātaṇa, dattāta, dattam, datu. ḍudhāñ: dadhādu, tātaṇa, dhattāta. By the rule "of dadhāti" and so on, it becomes the fourth letter: dhāttam, dadhatu, dhihi, tātaṇa, dhattāta, dhattām, dhatu. ṇajari: ninaktu, tātaṇa, neṇiktāta, neniktaṃ, ninijotu, nenighi, tātaṇa, neniktata, neniktām, nenakti. By the rule "for the reduplicated too, before a vowel, where the penultimate nāmin of any root takes guṇa," the penultimate nāmin does not take guṇa: nenijāna, nenijāb, nenijāma.

dibu: dibyatu, tātaṇa, dībyatāta, dībyātām, dāphyantu. By the rule "of hi after ā, but not after hanta," hi is elided: dībya, tātaṇa, dībyatāta. ṣuñ: sunotu, tātaṇa, sunutāta, sunutām, sunvantu. By the rule "also after nu, from a vikaraṇa that is not reduplicated," hi is elided: sunu, tātaṇa, sunutāt, sunatam, sunuta, sunabāni, sunabāba, sunābāsa, sunutam, sunātām, sunvātāma. tuda: tudatu, tātaṇa, tudatāt, tudātam, tudetām, tudentum. rudhira: ruṇaddhu, tātaṇa, runddhata, rundhāma, rundhāntā, ruṃndhi, tātaṇa, rundhāta, rundhama, rundha. chidira: chinattu, tātaṇa, chintāṃ, chindhāma, chindantu, chindhī, tātaṇa, chindhāt. tanu: tanotu, tātaṇa, tanutāt, tanutām, tanvantu. By the rule "also after the letter u," hi is elided: tanu, tātaṇa, tanutāta, tanutāma, tanuta, tanutāṃma, tamātāma. kriñ: rotu, tātaṇa, kurutāta, kurutām, kurbbantu, kuru, tātaṇa, kurutāt, kurutām, kuruta, karbāma, karabāma, kurutām, kurbbātām, kurbbatāma. ṭukṛñ: krīnātu, krīṇītāt, krītītāma, krītīntā, krītīta, krīṇātāta, krīṇītām, krīṇīta.

bandha is in the sense of binding. The anuṣaṅga is elided: badhnātu, badhnitāta, badhnitāma, badhnantu. By the rule "ānā for hi after a consonant ending," after the consonant ending of krī and the rest, ānā, which bears the name vikaraṇa, comes after it, and hi is elided: badhāna, badhītāt. puṣa is in the sense of flourishing: puṣṇāsu, puṣṇītāt, budhātāṃ, puṣṇantu, buśāṇa, puṣṇutāt. curi takes in, and the light penultimate takes guṇa: corayatu, corayatāt, corayatām, corayantu, coraya, tātaṇa, corayatāt.

By the rule "for ability and for wishing," there is the pañcamī: samutram abiśoṣayāṇo, barbbatam abyutpaṭayāni. For wishing too: jībatu bhabān, nantatu bhabān. It is also used for urging and the rest: kaṭaṃ karotu, iha bhuṅkṣa bhabān, ehāstā bhabān, māṇabakam adhyāpayatu bhabān, kanuṃ khalu byākaraṇam adhyaye, utacchando ḍadhyayai, dehi me bhikṣāṃ.

For the state and the object there are ātmane forms. graha takes yaṇ. By the sūtra "grahi and the rest," there is samprasāraṇa: gṛhyītāṃ. jyā: jīyatām. byadha: bidhyatām. baśa: uṣyatām. byaca: bicyatām. pracCha: pricchatām. praścu: biścyatāma. bhasja: bhṛjjatām. ñiṣvasa: by the sūtra "svapi, vaci and the rest," there is samprasāraṇa: supyātām. paca: for brū too, vaci: ucyatām. yija: yijyatām. ṭupapa: ubyatām. paha: uhyatām. byeñ: saubīyatām. poha: uyatām. hveñ: hūyatām. bada: udyatām. basa: uṣyetām. ṭuoṣi: śūyatām. as: bhu for asti: bhuyatām. khyāñ for cakṣiṅ: ākhyāyatām. bhyi for aji: bīyatām.

This concludes the third section, showing the domain of the pañcamī, from the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown.

Now the hyastanī is to be stated. By the rule "also those that have the action of what has happened," there is the hyastanī. By the rule "aṭ at the beginning of the root for the hyastanī, the adyatanī and the kriyātipatti," aṭ comes as an augment.

The letter ṭ of aṭ serves the distinction made in the rule "aṭ of sunoti, suvati, syati, stauti and stobhati after a prefix, and in other cases too." The letter i serves the distinction made in the rule "of di and si after a consonant." The operations of the vikaraṇa an and the rest are as before: ajayatī, ajayatām, ajayan, ajayaḥ, ajayatām, ajayata. This is the procedure for a and a diphthong: ajayama, ajayāma. In the ātmane endings: byajayeta, byajayatām, byajayanta, byajayathāḥ, byajayathām, byajayadhvaṃ, byajaye, byajayābatī, byajayāmayi. It is the same everywhere.

aṅ: an is elided. By the rule "aṭ after aṅ," before the following di and si, the affix at comes in between. By the rule "ā, by exception to the augment aṭ," the letter a becomes the letter ā: ātat, ātām, ādan, adaḥ, āttāma, ātta, ādam, ādma, adma. baśa: by the rule "of di and si after a consonant," di and si are elided, and it becomes ṣ. By the rule "optionally at the end of a word," the letter ṣ becomes the letter ṭ: abaṭ. There is samprasāraṇa. By the rule "afterwards, vṛddhi of the first of the initial vowels," there is vṛddhi: auṣṭāma, auśana. hana is in the sense of harming and of going. an is elided, and there is aṭ. After a consonant, di and si are elided: ahan. By the rule "of hanti before a dhuṭ, where it is not of all the roots," na is elided: ahatām. The penultimate is removed, and gha comes in: aghān, anaṅhatam, ahata, ahanam, ahanva, ahanma. In the ātmane endings: āhata, āghātām, āghāta, āhathāḥ, aghatham, āhadhvam, āghni, āhanvahi, ahanmahi.

yu: there is vṛddhi of u: āyauta, ayutām, ayuban. an: there is vṛddhi of the initial vowels: aita, aitām. By the rule "also of en," it becomes ya. By the rule "before the ya of at," there is iṭ, and the letter a becomes the letter ā: āyan. ik behaves like iṇ, and so does ik: adhyaite, adhyaitām, adhyāyan. ā: ayāt, ayātām. By the rule "for an, after sic, the reduplicated and bid and the rest, but not after bhū," us takes the place of an. And because it belongs to bid and the rest, by the rule "not always when combined," it is optional: ayān. By the rule "the letter a before us," the letter a is elided: ayuḥ.

bida: by the rule "of di and si after a consonant," di and si are elided. By the rule "optionally at the end of a word," it becomes the first letter, and the letter d becomes the letter t: abad, abittām. By the rule "not always when combined," it is optional: abidana, abiduḥ. Before si at the end of a word, it becomes r, because it is stated for those that do not have r by nature as well: abe, abeta. In the ātmane endings: samabitta, samabidātām. By the rule "it is to be stated as optional after vetti," it optionally becomes r: samabitvata, samabidata.

as: by the rule "after asti, before di and si," there is iṭ: āsīti. By the rule "of asti," the letter a becomes the letter ā: āstām, āsan, āsīḥ. mṛju: an is elided. marja becomes mārjji. Because it belongs to bhṛja and the rest, it becomes ṣ. By the rule "optionally at the end of a word," the letter ṣ becomes the letter ṭ: amārṣa, amṛṣṭam. Before a vowel without guṇa it is to be stated as optional: amṛjana, amarjan. paca: abak, abakttama, abacan. rudira: by the rule "also after ruṅ and the rest," there is iṭ after di and si: arodāti. Some also want aṭ for rud: arodata. By the rule "after rud and the rest, for all the roots," iṭ comes as an augment: aruditām, arudana, arodīḥ, arodaḥ. ñiṣvas: asvabīta, asvapata, asvabitām, asvapan, asvabṣiḥ, asvapaḥ. śvasa: aśvasvita, aśvasata, aśvasitām, aśvasan, aśvasvīḥ, aśvasaḥ. It also takes an: prāṇīta, prāṇata, prāṇitām, prāṇan, prāṇḍiḥ, prāṇaḥ. jakṣa: ajakṣiti, ajakṣitām. By the rule "jakṣa and the rest too," it receives the name "reduplicated." Because it is reduplicated, us takes the place of an: ajakṣuḥ, ajakṣiḥ, ajakṣaḥ. jigṛ: ajāgaḥ, ajāgṛtāma. Because it belongs to jakṣa and the rest, us takes the place of an. By the rule "of the reduplicated, before us," there is guṇa: ajajāgruḥ. daridrī: adaridrāt, adaridritām, adaridruḥ. By the rule "of the a of the reduplicated," the letter ā is elided. cakrāsṛ: by the rule "t for s before the di of the hyastanī," the letter s becomes the letter t: acakāt, acakāstām, acakāsuḥ. By the rule "it is to be stated as optional before the si of the hyastanī," it optionally becomes the letter t: acakāta, acakāḥ. śāsuṃ: āśāt, āśīṣṭāma, āśasuḥ.

cakṣiṅ: byācaṣṭa, byācakṣātā, byācakṣata, byācaṣṭaḥ, byācakṣathām, byācaddham. āṃḍi: by the rule "vṛddhi of the first of the initial vowels," there is vṛddhi: aitta, aiḍātām, aiḍata, aiḍṭhaḥ. iśa: aiṣṭa, aiśātām, aiśati, aiṣṭhāḥ, aiṣāthām, iśadhvam. āsa: āsta, āsātāṃ, āsata. śāsa after āṅ: āśāsta, īśāsātām, āśāsatā, āśāsthaḥ. basa: abasta, abasātām, abasatā. ṣūṅ: asnyuta, asubātām, asubata. śiṅ: aśeta, aśayatām, aśerata. iṅ: adhyaita, adhyayātām, adhyāyāt. dīdhīṅ: adīdhīta, adhīdhyātām, adīdhyata. hnuba: ahnuta, hnupātām, ahnubata. dviṣa: adveṭa, adiṣṭam. Because it belongs to bida and the rest, by the rule "not always when combined," it is optional: adveṣvana, adveṣu, advaṭa, adviṣṭam, adviṣṭa, adveṣam, adiṣva, adviṣma, adviṣṭa, adviṣātām, adviṣata, adviṣṭhaḥ. duha: by the rule "also when removed, before di and si," it becomes the fourth letter, and the letter d becomes the letter dh: adhok, adughām, aduhana, odhok, adugdham, adoham, aduhva, aduhma, adugdha, aduhātāma, aduhata, adugdhā, aduhāthām, adugdhām. diha: adhek, adigdhām, adihan, adigdha, adihatām, adihata. lihi: aleṭ. Before ḍh, ḍh is elided, and the penultimate is also lengthened: alīḍhām, aliḍhan, aliḍha, alihātaṃ, alihāta. By the rule "also in the hyastanī," from the exception to vṛddhi, there is always guṇa: brauṛṇuta, prauṛṇutām, prorṇuban, prauṛṇuḥ, prauṛṇuta, prauṛṇubātām, prauṛṇubata. bruñ: by the rule "īṭ in the first person after brū," there is īṭ.

abrabīt, abrūtām, abrūban, abrūta, abrūpātām, abrupata. tu: ajuhota, ajuhātām, ajuhabuḥ. ñibhī: abibheta, ābebhetāṃ, abhibhitām, abibheyuḥ. By the rule "of reduplicated stems, before us," there is guṇa. hrī: ajihreta, ajehrītām, ajihrayuḥ. pṛ: apibaḥ, abisṛtām, apibaruḥ. ohāk: ajihāt. "In all sārvadhātuka endings beginning with a consonant, i is optionally to be stated": ajahitām, ajahuḥ. ṛ: aiyaḥ, eyṛtām, eyaruḥ. ṇijir: anenik, ānenik­tām, anenijuḥ, anenikta, anenijātām, anenijata. bijir: abebek, abebiktām, abebijuḥ, abebikta, abebijātām, abebijata. biṣḷ: abebeṭā, abebeṣṭām, abebiṣuḥ, ababiṣṭa, abebiṣātāṃ, abebiṣata. ḍudāñ: adadāt, adattām, adaduḥ, adatta, adadātām, adadata. dhā: adādhāt. By the rule beginning "of dedhāti, also before t and th," there is the fourth letter: the letter d becomes the letter dh. adhāttām, adadhuḥ, adhatta, adadha tām, adadhata. ḍubhṛñ: abibhaḥ, abibhṛtām, abibharu, abibhṛt, abibhratām, abhibhrata. māṅ: amimīti, amimātāṃ, amimata. ohāṅ: ajihīta, ajihītām, ajihata. Likewise ad and the rest.

dibu: ādebyata, adībyatam, adībyan, adībyaḥ, adībyatam, adībyata, adībyam, adībyāba, adhībyāmaḥ. ṣuñ: asunota, asunutām, asunvan, asunoḥ, asunutam, asunuta, asunabam, asunama, asunam, usunva, asunma. tud: atudat.

rudhir: aruṇata, arunddham, arundhan. By the rule beginning "before si at the end of a word," there is also ṇ. aruṇaḥ, aruṇata, arundham, arundha, aruṇadhi, arundha, arundhā, arundhā, arundhātam, arundhāta. bhidir: abhinata, abhinattāṃ, abhindan. Optionally also: abhinaḥ. In the passive: bhinata, abhintā, abhinātām, abhindāta. tṛha: atriṇeta, atṛṇḍhām, atriṃhan.

tanu: ahanotam, atanutām, atenvan, atanoḥ, atanutām, atanuta, atanupama, atanuba, atanuma, atanu, atanma.

krī: akrīṇāt, akrīnītām, akrīṇan, akrītāḥ, akrīṇītām, akrīṇīta, akrīṇām, akrīṇība, akrīṇīma, akrīttita.

cur: acoryat, acoryātām, acorayan. In the impersonal and the passive there is the ātmanepada: ajīyata, ajīyātām, ajīyanta, adyata, idādyata, asūyata, atudyata, atudhyata, atāyata, atanyata, akrīyata, acormata. Likewise in all cases.

This is the fourth chapter, setting out the domain of the hyastanī, in the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown. Thus the sārvadhātuka is complete.

From here on is what is not sārvadhātuka. Here the adyatanī is to be stated. yā: in the sense of reaching. By the rule "and those that have a past action," the adyatanī. By the rule "at the beginning of the root, aṭ, before the hyastanī, the adyatanī, and the conditional," the augment aṭ comes at the beginning of the root. The letter ṭ of aṭ serves to distinguish it in the rule "after a preverb, of suniti, subati, syate, staute, and stobhati, also before aṭ and elsewhere." By the rule "before the adyatanī, sic," there is sic. The letter i and the letter c serve to distinguish it in the rule "of roots ending in a vowel, before sic in the parasmaipada." By the rule "after sic," there is aṭ before the s endings. By the rule beginning "yami, rami," iṭ also comes at the end of sic. By the rule "after iṭ, also before īṭ," sic is elided. There is lengthening of the like vowels, and what follows is also elided: ayāsīt, ayāsīṣṭām. By the rule "after sic, a reduplicated stem, and vid and the rest, but not after bhū, an becomes us," the ān that follows sic is changed into us. ayasiṣuḥ, ayāsīḥ, ayāsviṣṭaṃ, ayāsiṣṭa, ayāsviṣṭam, a yāsiṣmam. bā: abāsīt. bhā: abhāsit. ṣṇā: asnāsīt. glai: by the rule "for roots ending in a diphthong, ā, when not before a vikaraṇa," there is the letter ā: aglasīt. iṇ: by the rule "gā for iṇ," it is changed into gā. By the rule "after iṇ, sthā, dā, pibati, and bhū, before sic in the parasmaipada," sic disappears: agāt, āgātām. By the rule "of ā, before us," the letter ā is elided: aguḥ. ik: by the sūtra "for ik, as for iṇ," it is changed into gā: adhyagāt, adhyagātām, adhyaguḥ. stha: asthāta, asthātāma, asthaḥ. Those that bear its name, dāṇ, ḍudāñ and do: adāt, adātām, aduḥ. dheṭ: adhāta, adhātām, adhuḥ. pā, in the sense of drinking: apāt, apātām, apuḥ. gai, in the sense of sound. bā, in the sense of protecting. With these, sic does not disappear: aghāsīt, agāsīṣṭām, aghāsīṣuḥ, apāsīt, apāsiṣṭām, apāsiṣuḥ. By the explanation "for ghrā, śā, cchā, sā, and dheṭ, it is optionally to be stated," sic disappears: aghrāt, aghrāsīt. śo, in the sense of making thin: aśāt, aśāsīt. In the sense of cutting: acchat, acchāsīt. ṣo, in the sense of bringing to an end: asāt, asāsīt. dheṭ: adhāt, adhāsīt. By the explanation "for śva and dheṭ, it is optionally to be stated," there is caṇ. The letter ṇ serves to distinguish it in the rule "not with a ṇ marker, nor before ce and krīyita." The letter c serves to distinguish it in the rule "before caṇ, the parokṣā, the cākrīyita, and roots ending in san." By this very rule the root is reduplicated. By the rule "in what is not sārvadhātuka, ā is elided," the letter ā is elided: adabhat, addhatam, adan.

In the ātmanepada sic does not disappear. By the rule "for sthā and dā, before the adyatanī in the ātmanepada, i," there is the letter i. By the rule "after a short vowel also, when there is no iṭ," sic is elided. By the rule "and of sthā and dā," there is no guṇa: samasthāta, samasthaṣātām, samasthaṣata, samasthithāḥ, samasthiṣāthām. By the rule "after a root ending in a nāmin vowel, before the āśīḥ, the adyatanī, and the parokṣā, dh," there is ḍh: samasthiḍhvam, samasthiḍvi, samasthiṣvahi, samasthiṣmahi. adita, adiṣātām, adiṣata, adhita. cakṣeṅ: by the rule "khyāñ for cakṣiṅ," it is changed into khyāñ. asu: by the rule "after bāci, khyāti, mi, bi, si, ci, and hva, aṇ," there is the affix aṇ. The letter ṇ serves to distinguish it, as before. By the rule "in what is not sārvadhātuka, a is elided," the letter a is elided.

ākhyad, ākhyatam, ākhyan. In the ātmanepada: ākhyata, ākhyatām, akhyanta. hveñ: aṇ, as before. āhvata, āhvatām, āhān. Here, from the general rule that the arrangement is optional, by the rule "for lis and the rest, optionally in the ātmanepada," the affix aṇ is optional: āhvata, āhvetām, āhvanta, ahvāsta, āhvāsātam, āhasata.

ṅaridrā: because the augment together with iṭ is not invariable, it is only by the explanation. adaridrīta, adaridriṣḍām, adaridriṣuḥ, adaridrāsīt, adaridrāsiṣṭam, adaridrasiṣuḥ.

ciñ: by the rule "of roots ending in a vowel, before sic in the parasmaipada," there is vṛddhi: āciṣīt, acaiṣam, acaiṣuḥ. In the ātmanepada there is no vṛddhi: acaṣṭa, aceṣātam, aceṣata.

id: before the adyatanī and the conditional, the change into gī is to be accepted as optional: abhyagisṭe; in the other alternative: adhyeṣṭa. ji: ajeṣāt, byajeṣṭa, parājeṣṭa.

ṭuośvi: there is the augment it. The rule "guṇa for śvi and jāgṛ" gives guṇa as an exception to vṛddhi: aśvayāti, aśvayiṣṭām, aśvayiśutho. By the rule "for śvi and dheṭ, it is optionally to be stated," there is caṇ, and the root is reduplicated. It is changed into iy: aśiśviyat, aśiśiyatām, aśiyan. By the rule "also of jrā, śvi, standhu, mrucu, mlucu, and glu," the affix aṇ is optional. By the rule "for śvi, at is to be stated," the letter i is changed into the letter a. Then comes the procedure for a and the diphthong: aśvat, aśvatām, aśvan.

śviñ: by the rule "after śvi, pru, sru, gami, and roots ending in grāti, caṇ in the sense of the agent," there is the affix caṇ. The root is reduplicated: aśiśriyad.

Roots ending in ī. ṇīñ: aiṣita, anaiṣṭām, aneṣuḥ. ḍīṅ: aḍayiṣṭa. śīṅ: aśayiṣṭa. bhī: ābheṣīta. Joined with mā: by the rule "not when joined with mā and mā sma," the augment aṭ is prohibited. Some say that the teaching of the augment is not invariable. īṭ does not occur: mā bhaiḥ.

Roots ending in u. hu: ahauṣīta, ahauṣṭam, ahauṣuḥ. By the rule "after ṣṭuñ, su, and dhuñ, in the parasmaipada," there is iṭ: astābīt, asābīt, adhābīt. In the ātmanepada: astoṣṭa, asoṣṭa, adhoṣṭa. dhūñ, in the sense of shaking: madhābīt, adhābiṣṭuma. yu: ayābīta. ru: arābīta. urṇṇuñ: in the rule "also before the hyastanī," the word "also" here serves to include what is not stated. Therefore before iṭ there is optionally guṇa. u is changed into ab: prauṛṇubīt. In the guṇa alternative, by the explanation "optionally for urṇoti," there is vṛddhi: prorṇṇabīt, prauṛṇubīt. By the rule "after śri, dru, sru, kami, and roots ending in kāritaṃ, caṇ in the sense of the agent," there is caṇ in the sense of the agent: adudrubat, asusrubat.

Roots ending in ū. lūñ: alābīta. būñ: abābīt. In the ātmanepada: ilabiṣṭa, ababiṣṭa. bhū: by the sūtra "in, sthā, and the rest," sic disappears. By the rule "when the sic of bhū disappears," there is no guṇa. By the rule "after bhū, when sic disappears," there is no iṭ: abhūta, abhūtāṃ. Because of the prohibition "not after bhū," an does not become us. By the rule "for bhū, b, before the parokṣā and the adyatanī ending in ri," the letter b is the augment.

Roots ending in ṛ: ākārṣīt, āhāṣīt. In the ātmanepada, with "and before sic and the āśīḥ in the ātmanepada" carried over, by the rule "and of those ending in ṛ," there is no guṇa. By the rule "after a short vowel also, when there is no iṭ," sic is elided: akṛta, akṛṣrātām, akṛṣrata, akṛthāḥ, akṛṣāthām. By the rule beginning "after a root ending in a nāmin vowel, before the āśīḥ," there is ḍh: akṛḍhvama, akṛṣi, akṛṣvahi, akṛṣamahi, ahrit. mṛṅ: by the rule "mṛṅ also before the āśīḥ and the adyatanī," it takes the ātmanepada: amṛta. bṛñ: abārīt. From the rule "after ṛt, for bṛṅ and briñ, iṭ is optional before san," and "before sic and the āśīḥ, in the ātmanepada," and by the rule "what is denoted by ṛti after an initial conjunct and so on is not established when it has become external," the augment iṭ is optional: prābariṣṭa, prābriha. "After ṛt, also for bṛṅ and bṛñ, there is optionally a long vowel, that is, of iṭ, when not before the parokṣā and the āśīḥ." By the separate formulation "it is not prescribed before sic in the parasmaipada," the vowel is optionally long: prābarīṣṭa. By the rule "in the parasmaipada also, after puṣ and the rest, dyut and the rest, those with the marker ḷ, irta, sarti, and śāsti," there is the affix aṇ. By the rule "for arti and sarti, before aṇ," there is guṇa. ṛ: ārat. sṛ: asarat. jāgṛ: by the rule "guṇa for śvi and jāgṛ," guṇa replaces vṛddhi as an exception: ajāgārīt.

Roots ending in ṝ. stṝñ: astārṝt. By the rule "it is not prescribed before sic in the parasmaipada," the vowels are long: astāriṣṭām, astāriṣuḥ. In the ātmanepada, by the rule beginning "after ṛt, for bṛṅ and bṛñ," iṭ is optional: astariṣṭa, astarīṣātām, āstirīṣata. In the alternative without iṭ, guṇa is prohibited here by the rule "and of those ending in ṛt," because the letter ṛ is meant as an indicator of the class. By the rule "ir for a root ending in ṛt when there is no guṇa," there is ir. By the rule beginning "of a nāmin before r and v," the penultimate is lengthened: astīrṣṭa, astīrṣṭatām, astīrṣata. By the rule "also of jṝ, śvi, stanbhu, mrucu, mlucu, and glucu," the affix aṇ is optional. By the rule "for arti and sarti, before aṇ," there is guṇa, because the letter ṛ is meant as an indicator: ajarat, ajārīt.

Roots ending in a consonant. bac: the a is also lengthened: abākṣit. By the rule "after a dhuṭ, before a dhuṭ also," sic is elided: āpākhāma, ābākṣuḥ. In the ātmanepada: apañ, apakṣātām, apakṣeta. By the separate formulation "of a also, a long vowel," even a vowel that is not the penultimate is lengthened. bhañj: abhādhīta. bas: abāssīta. bah: by the rule "ḍh for h," there is ḍh. By the rule "k for ṣ and ḍh before s," there is k. Because of the conditioning factor, there is ṣ: abākṣita. When the lengthening has been made by the rule "of a also, a long vowel," there is ḍh by the rule "ḍh for h." By the rule "after a dhuṭ, before a dhuṭ also," sic is elided. By the rule "after gh, ḍh, dh, and bh, but not after dhā, dh for t and th," there is dh.

By the rule "after ṣ and the ṭ class, the ṭ class in place of the t class," the ṭ class is substituted. By the rule "for sahi and vaha, o in place of the letter a," ḍh is elided and the letter a also becomes o: aboḍhāma, abākṣuḥ, abākṣīḥ. In the ātmanepada: aboḍha, abakṣātam, abākṣata, aboḍhāḥ.

rudhir: by the rule "optionally after a root with the marker ir," the affix aṇ is optional in the parasmaipada: arudhat, akṣudata, abhidata, acchidata, atraṃdata, acchidata. When there is sic, by the rule "of roots ending in a consonant, when there is no iṭ," there is vṛddhi. By the rule "for the unvoiced, not before ṣiṭ, the first," the first letter of the class is substituted: arautsita, akṣautsita, abhaitsīta, acchaitsī, atāttsīta, acchattsīta. In the dual, by the rule "after a dhuṭ, before a dhuṭ also," sic is elided: arauddhāma, akṣaudhāma, abhaittāma, acchettāma, atārttām, acchattām. In the ātmanepada, by the rule "and before sic and the āśīḥ in the ātmanepada," there is no guṇa. By the rule "after a dhuṭ, before a dhuṭ also," sic is elided: aruddha, akṣatḍa, abhitta, acchitta, adṛtta, acchitta.

budh, in the sense of understanding: sic is elided: abuddha. By the rule "optionally after dīp, jan, budh, pūri, tāyi, and pyāyi," there is optionally ic: abodhi.

dibu: adebīt. kuṣ: akoṣīta. muṣ: amoṣīta. By the rule "optionally for pha, ṭha, and the rest beginning with a consonant, with iṭ, those not having the marker iṭ, those ending in gaṅ(?), kṣaṇ, and śvas," the vowel is optionally long: apāṭhīta, apathīta, akāṇīta, akaṇīta, arāṇīta, araṇīta. By the rule "and of vad, vraj, and those ending in r and l," the vowel is always long. vad: abādīt. braj: abrājīta. cari: acārīte. kṣar: akṣarīta. cal: acālīta. jval: ajrālīt. Because of the prohibition in the rule "not with the marker it, and of kṣaṇ and śvas," roots with the marker et and the rest are not lengthened. rage, in the sense of suspicion; lage, in the sense of attachment; ṣage; hrage: aragīta, alagīta, asagīta, ahragīta. hlage: ahlagīta. sthage: asthagīta, asthagiṣṭāma, asthagiṣuḥ. grah: agrahīta. ṭubam: abamīta. byay: abyayīta. kṣaṇā: akṣaṇīta. śvas: aśvasita.

anju: since it bears the marker ut, an option would apply; but by the rule "after añji, before sic," there is always iṭ: āñjāta. By the rule "and of yami, rami, and roots ending in āt, the end of si," there is iṭ and also the ending of si. After the augment there is anusvāra: ayaṃsvīt. By the rule "after vi, āṅ, and pari, before ra," it takes the parasmaipada: byaraṃsīta, āraṃsīta, paryaraṃsīta.

In the ātmanepada: "after hani, before sic, the ātmanepada is to be seen; also after yami in the sense of making manifest." This is stated only in the explanation, in the sense of giving away a bride. By the rule "after gami likewise, before sic and the āśīḥ," the fifth letter is elided. By the rule "after a short vowel also, when there is no iṭ," sic is elided: āyata, āyasātān, āyasata. In the sense of giving away a bride, it is stated only in the explanation: upāyaṃsta kanyāṃ chāttraḥ, upāyata kanyāṃ chāttraḥ.

kamu: by the explanation "for kamu, ay and the rest, optionally in what is not sārvadhātuka," in is elided. When it is without iniṅ, by the rule "after śrī, dru, sru, kami, and roots ending in kārita, caṇ in the sense of the agent," there is caṇ in the sense of the agent. By the rule "before caṇ, the parokṣā, the cekrīyita, and roots ending in san," the root is reduplicated: acakramata, acakrametām, acakamanta. When there is iniṅ, there is caṇ because it ends in kārita. By the rule beginning "before in, of the like," the vowel is short. Before caṇ the root is reduplicated. Because by the rule beginning "of the like, not effaced" it becomes as before san, there is i. By the rule "a long vowel for a light syllable," it is lengthened: acīkamata, acīkametām, acīkamanta.

han: by the rule "also before the adyatanī," it is changed into bardhi. Because it has not become a root ending in n, there is no prohibition of iṭ under the rule "after hani and manyati, after n." Because it does not end in n, there is iṭ. By the rule "optionally for those beginning with a consonant, with iṭ, not having the marker iṭ, ending in hmi, kāṇ, and śvas," it is optionally not lengthened, because the indicated i takes its own form: abadhīta, abadhīṣṭāma, abadhiṣuḥ. Here, from the rule that the arrangement is optional, by "smṛti(?)" it is optionally changed into badhi in the ātmanepada. By the rule beginning "after hani, before sic, the ātmanepada is to be seen," the fifth letter is elided: āhata, āhasātāma, āhasata, āpadhiṣṭa, abadhiṣātāma, āpadhiṣata.

Here, from the general rule that the arrangement is optional, by the explanation "optionally after spṛś, mṛś, kṛṣ, tṛp, and dṛp," there is sic: assārkṣāti, aspārṣṭama, aspārkṣuḥ, amārkṣuki, akārkṣate, atārssita, atārbtām, atārpsuḥ, adārpsāti. By the rule "everywhere, of roots ending in a consonant that have no iṭ," there is vṛddhi. When, by the rule "optionally for spṛś and the rest," the letter a is the augment: asprakṣīta, aspraṣṭāṃ, asprakṣuḥ, asprakṣīta, atrassīta, atrastāma, atrussuḥ, adrāssīta. In the other alternative, by the rule "saṇi after a root ending in ṣit, with no iṭ, having a nāmin penultimate, other than dṛś," there is saṇ: aspṛkṣata, amṛkṣata, akṛkṣata. For tṛbi and dṛpi, because they belong to puṣ and the rest, there is aṇ: atripata, atribtām, atripanā, adripata, adriptām, adripa.

By the rule "for sṛj and dṛś, the letter a is the augment after the vowel before a dhuṭ, when guṇa or vṛddhi would apply," the letter a is the augment. The a is also lengthened: asrākṣita, asvaṣṭām, asvākṣuḥ, adrākṣīta. For dṛśi, san is to be prohibited by the words "other than dṛś"; then there is sic. Otherwise, by the rule "optionally after a root with the marker ir," there is aṇ. By the rule "for arti and sarti, before aṇ," there is guṇa, because the letter ṛ is meant as an indicator: adārśat, adarśatām, adarśana.

biśi: there is saṇ: abikṣat, abikṣatām, abikṣana. śliṣ: by the words "in the sense of embracing," ṇ comes only in the sense of embracing: aślikṣat tanyāṃ chāttraḥ. Otherwise, because it belongs to puṣ and the rest, there is only aṇ: samāśliṣata. dviṣ: adbikṣata. druh: there is gh. The first becomes the fourth, that is, the letter dh.

By the rule "for the unvoiced, not before ṣiṭ, the first," the first letter is substituted, that is, the letter k. Because of the conditioning factor, there is ṣ: adhukṣata. deh: adhikṣata. lih: there is ḍh: alikṣata. guh: there is ḍh: aghukṣata. With "the arrangement is optional" carried over, from the statement "for duh, dih, and lih, in the ātmanepada before the t class alone, in the alternative with saṇ, sic also does not occur," there is saṇ in one alternative: adugdha, adhukṣata, adigdha, adhikṣata, alīḍha, alikṣata, aguḍha, aghukṣata. By the rule "a is elided before a vowel, for saṇ, when not in the plural(?)," the letter a of saṇ is elided: aghukṣātam, aghukṣānta, aghuḍhāḥ, aghukṣāthaḥ, aghukṣāthām, aghuḍhama, aghuḍhvam, aghukṣi, aghukṣābahi, aghukṣamahi.

From dahi, because it does not have a nāmin penultimate, saṇ does not occur; there is sic: adhākṣāta, adāgdhami, adhākṣuḥ. By the rule "dh for the h of nah and nāh," there is dh. By the rule "for the unvoiced, not before ṣiṭ, the first," the first letter is substituted: anātsīta, anūddhūma, anātsuḥ.

asu: by the rule "after asu, baci, khyāti, li, si, ca, and hva, aṇ," there is the affix aṇ. By the rule "th at the end of asyati," the letter th is the augment: āstha, āsthatāṃ, āsthana. By the rule "after a preverb, for asyati and uhi, optionally," it takes the ātmanepada: samāsthatā, samāsthetām, samāsthanta.
