By "from those meaning going, only in the sense of crookedness," there is the suffix ya. Kramu: kuṭilaṃ kramati, caṅkramyate. Dram: dandramyate. By "from lup and the rest, in the sense of contempt," there is the suffix ya. Garhitaṃ lumpati, lolupyate. Śadḷ: śāśadyate. By "for car and phal, also u for the following a," there is the augment of anusvāra, and the following letter a also becomes u. The penultimate of r and v is long. Cañcūryate, pamphulyate. By "also of jap and the rest," there is the augment of anusvāra. Jañjapyate, dandahyate, dandaśyate.

Bhañjo, in the sense of crushing. For those that do not have i as an indicatory letter, where there is no guṇa, the anuṣaṅga is deleted. Bambhajyate. Paś: a root that occurs in the sūtra. Pampaśyate. By "for vañci, sraṃsi, dhvaṃsi, bhraṃśi, kasi, pati, padi and skand, nī at the end," there is the augment nī, and the anuṣaṅga is deleted. Vanīvacyate, sanīsrasyate, danīdhvasyate, banībhraśyate, canīkasyate, panīpatyate, panīpadyate, canīskadyate.

Bhid: bebhidyate. Budh: bobudhyate. Vṛtu, vṛdhu: by "rī for those containing ṛ," there is the augment rī. Varīvṛtyate, varīvṛdhyate. Dṛśir: darīdṛśyate. Jarījṛmbhyate, sarīsṛpyate, narīnṛtyate, śarīśṛdhyate, jarīgṛdhyate, parīpṛśyate, parīpṛcchyate. Ḍhaukṛ: ḍoḍhaukyate. Traukṛ: totraukyate.

Sūc, sūtr, sūtr, mūtr: because they belong to the cur class, there is in. By "also after ṛ and the rest," there is the suffix ya. By "of the kārita, before what is not ām, iṭ or a vikaraṇa," the kārita is deleted. Sosūcyate, sosūtryate, momūtryate. Aś: aśāśyate. Aṭ: aṭāṭyate.

In the impersonal and the passive, there is yaṇ. By "deletion also of a," the letter a is deleted. By "of ya, where there is no an," the letter ya is deleted. Pāpaṭhyate, bebhidyate. It should be seen likewise in every case.

Saptamī: lolūyeta. Pañcamī: lolūyatām. Hyastanī: alolūyata. Adyatanī: alolūyiṣṭa. Parokṣā: the augment comes from the very end of the suffix. Lolūyāñcakre. Śvastanī: lolūyitā. Āśīḥ: lolūyiṣīṣṭa. Bhaviṣyantī: lolūyiṣyate. Kriyātipattiḥ: alolūyiṣyata. Thus the domain of the cekrīyita has been taught.

The deletion of the cekrīyita is now to be stated. By the separate rule "its deletion before ac," the cekrīyita is deleted. By "some say carkarītam also," there are parasmaipada endings. From ad and the rest, an is deleted. By "optionally after the carkarīta," before a sārvadhātuka that begins with a consonant and takes guṇa, there is optionally the augment īṭ. Lolavīti, loloti, lolūtaḥ. By "both are abhyasta," it is given the name abhyasta. By "after an abhyasta, the n of anti is deleted," n is deleted. Loluvati. Lolavīṣi, loloṣi, lolūthaḥ, lolūtha. Lolavīmi, lolomi, lolūvaḥ, lolūmaḥ.

Vad: vāvadīti, vāvatti, vāvattaḥ, vāvadati.

Kruś: by "also not for the penultimate of an abhyasta" and "before a vowel in a sārvadhātuka taking guṇa," guṇa is prohibited. Cokruśīti, cokruṣṭaḥ, cokruśati.

Mud, in the sense of joy. Momudīti, momotti, momuttaḥ, momudati. Saptamī: momudyāt. Pañcamī: momudītu, momottu, momuttāt, momuttām, momudatu. By "after hu and a dhuṭ, dhi for hi," there is dhi. Momuddhi, momuttāt, momuttam, momutta, momudāni, momudāva, momudāma. Hyastanī: amomudīt, amomot, amomuttām, amomuduḥ. Adyatanī: by "after iṭ, also before īṭ," sic is deleted. Amomodīt, amomodīt, amomodiṣṭām, amomodiṣuḥ, amomodīḥ. Khādṛ: acākhādiṣṭām, acākhādiṣuḥ, acākhādīḥ.

Gṛdhu, in the sense of longing. By "rī for those containing ṛ," there is the augment rī. Jarīgṛdhīti. By "ri and r also when the cekrīyita is deleted," it also becomes ri and r. Jarigṛdhīti, jargṛdhīti, jarīgardhi, jarigardhi, jargardhi. Hyastanī: ajarīgṛdhīd, ajarīgṛdhīt, ajarigṛdhīt, ajargṛdhīt, ajarīghart, ajarighart, ajarghart, ajarīgṛddhām, ajarigṛddhām, ajargṛddhām, ajarīgṛdhuḥ, ajarigṛdhuḥ, ajargṛdhuḥ. With s: there is guṇa. By "also when deleted, before d and s," the first becomes the fourth, the letter g becoming gh. Because this is expressed by "before s at the end of a word" and so on, it becomes r. The r before r is deleted, and the preceding vowel is lengthened. Ajarghāḥ. Spardh: apāspāḥ. Kurd: acokūḥ. Rādh and sādh, in the sense of accomplishment: arārāḥ. Nardh: anānāḥ. Such forms and others are to be looked into. Thus the domain of the deletion of the cekrīyita has been taught.

In is now to be stated.

Yā, in the sense of reaching. Where someone else prompts the one who goes (yāti), that one becomes its cause. By this, and by "also from a root, in the sense of the cause," there is the suffix in. The letter n serves to distinguish it in "of the penultimate a" and so on. By "arti, hrī, vlī, rī" and so on, the letter p is augmented. Yāpayati. Because it ends in in, there are endings of both voices. Yāpayate. Ḍudāñ: dāpayate. Ḍudhāñ: dhāpayatām, dhāpayati, dhāpayate, sannidhāpayati.

Śo, in the sense of making thin. Cho, in the sense of cutting. Ṣo, in the sense of bringing to an end. Hveñ, in the sense of rivalry. Vyeñ, in the sense of covering. Veñ, in the sense of weaving yarn. Dheṭ and pā, in the sense of drinking. Because these end in a diphthong, there is ā. Niśāyayati, avacchāyayati, sāyayati, hvāyayati, vyāyayati, vāyayati. By "for śā, chā, sā, hvā, vyā, ve and pā, before in, y," the ā is changed to āy.

Pā, in the sense of protecting. By "for pāti, l at the end," the consonant l is augmented. Pālayati.

Smiṅ, in the sense of smiling slightly. By "for smi, ji, krī and i, before in," there is ā, and the letter p is augmented. By "for bhī and smi, before in, when the causal agent is meant," there are ātmanepada endings. Vismāpayate. Because smiṅ is stated for fear that comes from the causal agent, here, where fear comes from an instrument, this does not happen: kuñcikayā vismāpayati.

Ji, in the sense of conquering. Ḍukrīñ, in the sense of trading goods. Iṅ, in the sense of reading. There is ā, and the letter p is augmented. Jāpayati, krāpayati, adhyāpayati.

The in-form of bhī applies also to fear that comes from the causal agent, and because of the rule on ruc and the rest, there are ātmanepada endings. Muṇḍo bhāpayate: by "for bhī and smi, before in, when the causal agent is meant," there are ātmanepada endings. Because it is said "bhiṣi cinti," bhī also has a final ṣ. Muṇḍo bhīṣayate. Otherwise there are parasmaipada endings, with vṛddhi and the change to āy. Kuñcikayā bhāyayati.

Sphur, in the sense of radiating, with vi prefixed. By "vispharati," in the sense of the state, there is ghañ, and guṇa. By "for sphuri and sphuli, ā for o before ghañ," there is ā. By "he makes that," there is in. When the cause is to be expressed, there is in again. By "of the kārita, before what is not ām, iṭ or a vikaraṇa," the kārita is deleted. Visphārayati. By "he prompts one who is radiating," there is in in the sense of the cause. Visphorayati.

Cap, in the sense of continuing, and also of gathering. Cāpayati. Ciñ: cāpayati. Ṭuvap, also in the sense of begetting. Puro vāto gāḥ pravāpayati: pravāpayati is in the sense of begetting; the meaning is "makes them conceive."

Rādh and sādh, in the sense of accomplishment. Where it does not concern the world beyond: grāmaṃ sādhayati, annaṃ sādhayati. Where it concerns the world beyond: tapaḥ sādhayati, dharmaṃ sādhayati. Ṣidhu, in the sense of going: annaṃ sedhayati, grāmaṃ sedhayati. Also of one who accomplishes: tāpasas tapaḥ sedhayati.

Rā and lā, in the sense of taking. By "from lāti, also in the senses of deceiving and humiliating," there are ātmanepada endings. Kas tvām ullāpayate. Śyeno vartikām ullāpayati. This is because it is stated that, in the senses of deceiving and overpowering, lī is not used with the kārita.

Mīñ and druñ, in the sense of harming. Ḍumiñ, in the sense of throwing. Diṅ, in the sense of wasting away. By "for mā, mī, mi and dī, where guṇa or vṛddhi stands," there is ā, and the letter p is augmented. Māpayati, pramāpayati, upadāpayati.

Ṛ, in the sense of going. Hrī, in the sense of shame. Vlī, in the sense of covering. Rī, also in the sense of harming. Knūyī, in the sense of sound. Kṣmāyī, in the sense of trembling. By "for arti, hrī, vlī, rī, knūyī, kṣmāyī and those ending in ā, p at the end, with deletion of y and guṇa of the nāmin," the letter p is augmented. Deletion of y and guṇa of the nāmin also take place as is fitting. Arpayati, samarpayati, hrepayati, vlepayati, repayati, knopayati, kṣmāpayati.

Śṛñ: śrāpayati. Ṇīñ: nāpayati. Dhyañ, in the sense of moving. Prīñ, in the sense of satisfying. Dhūñ: by "for prīṇāti and dhūnoti, n," the letter n is augmented. Vidhūnayati, prīṇayati. Because it is specified as "prīṇāti and dhūnoti," here the letter n is not augmented: Dhū, in the sense of shaking; prīṅ, in the sense of joy. Vidhāvayati, prāvayati.

Vilīnaṃ karoti: from the in-ending, when the cause is to be expressed, there is in again, and the kārita is deleted. Ghṛtaṃ vilīnayati. Lal, in the sense of desiring: ghṛtaṃ vilālayati. Sneha, in the sense of melting: this is because it is stated that for lī the use with the kārita is optional.

Vaj, in the sense of going, and also of moving. In places, upavājayati. Or else, in the sense of moving, it does not take the kārita.

Du: dāvayati. Dru: drāvayati. Sru: srāvayati. Lūñ: lāvayati. Bhū: bhāvayati. Ṣṭuñ: stāvayati.

Roots ending in ṛ. Kṛñ: kārayati. Hṛñ: hārayati. Vṛñ: vārayati. Pṝ: pārayati. By "optionally before a vowel," it becomes l. Nigālayati.

Roots ending in consonants. Vācayati, pāṭhayati. Han: there is in. The penultimate a is long. By "t for hanti," the letter t. By "for the h of hanti, gh before in and ic," the letter h becomes gh. Ghātayati. Ñiṣvap: svāpayate.

Adi and psā, in the sense of eating. By "a prohibition should be stated for adi and khādi," the designation of object for the agent of the in-ending is prohibited. Ādayati, vādayati.

Bhidir: bhedayati. Chidir: chedayati. Yudh, in the sense of fighting: yodhayati. Vṛtu, in the sense of occurring: vartayati. Kṛpū: by "l for the r of kṛp," it becomes l. Kalpayati. Krīḍṛ: krīḍayati. Kūj: kūjayati. Ḍhaukṛ: ḍhaukayati. Sic: secayati. Dhvaṃsu: dhvaṃsayati.

Duṣa, in the sense of becoming corrupt. By "for duṣ, before the kārita," the letter o becomes ū. Dūṣayati. Optionally, where it means the mind's freedom from attachment: prajñāṃ dūṣayati, prajñāṃ doṣayati.

Sphāy: by "for sphāyi, change to v," the letter v. Sphāvayati.

Śadḷ, in the sense of decaying. By "for śadi, when not meaning going, t," it is changed to the letter t. Phalāni śātayati. Otherwise: gāḥ śādayati, which means "makes them go."

By "for ruh, p, also in the sense of growing": vrīhīn ropayati, kāryam adhyāropayati, artham samāropayati. But from ruhi: ārohayati hastī hastipakam.

The m-anubandha is now to be stated. Ghaṭ, in the sense of striving. Ghaṭ and the rest have m as an indicatory letter. The penultimate a is long, but for those with m as an indicatory letter it is short. Ghaṭayati. Vyath, in the sense of suffering, fear and trembling: vyathayati. Prath, in the sense of being proclaimed: prathayati. Mrad, in the sense of crushing: mradayati. Skhad, in the sense of eating: skhadayati. Kṣaji, in the sense of going and also of giving. Dakṣ, also in the sense of harming. On the strength of their being recited among ghaṭ and the rest, the penultimate is not lengthened either. Kṣañjayati, dakṣayati. Krap: krapayati. Kandayati, krandayati, kradayati, kladayati. Ñitvarā: tvarayati; otherwise, tvārayati. Jvar: jvarayati. Gaḍ: gaḍayati. Heḍ, in the sense of surrounding. By "the diphthongs change to short, e and u," the letter e becomes i. Hiḍayati. Paṭayati, bhaṭayati. Naṭ, in the sense of going: naṭayati; otherwise, nāṭayati. Ṣṭak, in the sense of resisting: stakayati. Cak, in the sense of being satisfied: cakayati. Kakhe, in the sense of laughing: kakhayati. Rage, in the sense of pleasing: ragayate. Lage, in the sense of clinging. Hrage, hlage, ṣage and sthage, in the sense of covering. Lagayati, hragayati, hlagayati, sagayati, sthagayati. Kage, whose meaning is not stated: kagayati. Ak and ag, in the sense of going crookedly: akayati, agayati. Kaṇ and raṇ, in the sense of going: kaṇayati, raṇayati. Caṇ, śaṇ and śraṇ, in the sense of giving: caṇayati, śaṇayati, śraṇayati. In a sense other than giving: vicāṇayati, śāṇayati, śrāṇayati. Sūth, kūth, krath and klath, meaning killing: suthayati, kuthayati, krathayati, klathayati. Can also: canayati. Vanu also, whose meaning is not stated: vanayati. Jval, in the sense of blazing: jvalayati. Hval and hmal, in the sense of shaking: hvalayate, hmalayate. Smṛ, in the sense of recollecting: smarayati; otherwise, vismārayati. Dṝ, in the sense of fear: darayate; in a sense other than fear, dārayati kāṣṭham. Nṝ, in the sense of leading: narayati. Śrā, in the sense of cooking: śrapayati; in a sense other than cooking, śrāpayati.

Jñā, in the senses of killing, pleasing and sharpening. For killing: saṃjñapayati paśūn, meaning "he kills them." For pleasing: jñapayati rājānam, meaning "he gratifies him." For sharpening: jñapayati śarān vyādhaḥ, meaning "he makes them sharp." Cali, for causing to move: calayati; otherwise, cālayati. Chadi, for giving strength: chadayati; otherwise, chādayati ghoṣān. Laḍi, for rolling the tongue: laḍayati; otherwise, lāḍayati. Madi, for joy and for falling ill: madayati; otherwise, mādayati.

By "also jani, jṝṣ, knasu, rañj and those ending in am," they have m as an indicatory letter, and those with m as an indicatory letter are short. Janayati, jarayati, knasayati. On the strength of this very m-anubandha, for rañji in the sense of hunting deer, the anuṣaṅga is deleted: rajayati mṛgān. Otherwise: rañjayati vastram. Those ending in am: śamayati, bhramayati, ramayati, gamayati. Gami: by "in the sense of patience," there are ātmanepada endings. Āgamayasva tāvat kiñcit kālam, meaning "please be patient."

By "optionally for jval, hval, hmal and nam without a prefix," the m-anubandha is optional. Jvalayati, jvālayati, hvalayati, hvālayati, hmalayati, hmālayati, namayati, nāmayati. With a prefix they always have m as an indicatory letter: prajvalayati, prahvalayati, prahmalayati, praṇamayati, pariṇamayati. By "also of glā, snā, vanu and vami," for those without a prefix the m-anubandha is optional. Glapayati, glāpayati, snapayati, snāpayati, vanayati, vānayati, vamayati, vāmayati. For those with a prefix, it is as it would otherwise apply: praglāpayati, prasnāpayati, upavanayati, udvamayati.

Not for kami, ami and cami: what would apply from their ending in am is prohibited. Kāmayati, āmayati, ācāmayati. For śam, when not about seeing: śamayati rogam. But about seeing: niśāmayati rūpam. For yam, when not about serving food: yamayati. Otherwise: yāmayati brāhmaṇān, meaning "he has them served food."

For skhad, also after apa and pari: apaskhadayati, pariskhadayati. Here there appears to be a contradiction between what comes before and what comes after; since the Indian manuscript reads just like this, it should be examined. So it appears as an annotation in the source copy. In another translation: praskhādayati, upaskhadayati. For the simple root: skhadayati.

Phaṇ, in the sense of going: phaṇayati; otherwise, phāṇayati phāṇṭam. By "seventy-five," these are the roots with m as an indicatory letter.

Iṇ, in the sense of going. By "gami for iṇ, when not meaning understanding," it is changed to gami. Gamayati. By "also of ik, as of in," it is changed to gami: adhigamayati, according to some. Here it is not established in the sense of understanding.

By "for gṛdhi and vañci in the sense of deceiving," there are ātmanepada endings. Śvānaṃ gardhayate, ahiṃ vañcayate. By "kṛñ, for a wrong repeated utterance," there are ātmanepada endings. Padaṃ mithyā kārayate. By "from budh, yudh, naś, jan, iṅ, pru, sru and dru, before in," there are parasmaipada endings. Bodhayati, yodhayati, nāśayati, janayati, adhyāpayati, prāvayati, srāvayati, drāvayati. By "from one that without ṇi takes no object and whose agent is conscious," there are parasmaipada endings. Ās, in the sense of sitting near: āsayati, pāsayati. By "not for pā, dam, āṅyam, āyas, parimuh, ruci, nṛti, pad and vas," the parasmaipada endings are prohibited, and from ruc and the rest there are ātmanepada endings. Pāyayate: dheṭ is to be supplied.

Dhāpayate, damayati, āyāmayati, āyāsayate, parimohayate, rocayate, nartayati, pādayate, vāsayate.

In the impersonal and the passive, there is yaṇ. By "of the kārita, before what is not ām, iṭ or a vikaraṇa," the kārita is deleted. Kāryate, hāryate. Under "the agent that is also the object is like the object," it does not become like the object, because it belongs to kir and the rest: kārayate kaṭaḥ svayam eva. Saptamī: kārayeta. Pañcamī: kārayatām. Hyastanī: akārayata.

Adyatanī: by "caṇ for the agent after śri, dru, sru, kami and those ending in the kārita," there is caṇ. The letter ṇ serves to distinguish it in "not for those with ṇ as an indicatory letter, or for the cekrīyita." The letter c serves to distinguish it in "before caṇ, the parokṣā, the cekrīyita and those ending in san." By this very statement, "whatever was done before in is as if still standing in all cases," there is reduplication. By "the penultimate is short before caṇ, where no homogeneous vowel before in has been deleted," it is short. By "where no homogeneous vowel has been deleted, before a light syllable followed by in and caṇ, it is like san," it becomes like san, and so there is i. By "a light syllable is lengthened," it is long. The kārita is deleted. Acīkarat. Likewise: ajīharat, apīpaṭhat, apīpacat, ajījayat, apīpavat, alīlalat, ayīyapat.

By "it should be stated: optionally for śru, sru, dru, pru, plu and cyuṅ," there is optionally i. Aśiśravat, aśuśravat, aśuśravat, asisravat, asusravat, adidravat, adudravat, apipravat, apupravat, apiplavat, apuplavat, acicyavat, acucyavat.

Ṭuośvi: while "optionally for śvayati" carries over, by "also before the kārita, before san and caṇ," there is optionally samprasāraṇa. Aśiśvayat, aśūśavat, aśūśavat. Hveñ: by "always for hvayati," there is samprasāraṇa. Ajūhavat.

By "also a for tvar and the rest," an exception to becoming like san, it is changed to a. Atatvarat, asasmarat, adadarat, apaprathat, amamradat, atastarat, apaspaśat. Veṣṭ, in the sense of wrapping. Ceṣṭ, in the sense of moving. By "optionally for veṣṭi and ceṣṭi," it is also changed to a. Aviveṣṭat, avaveṣṭat, aciceṣṭat, acaceṣṭat. Gaṇ, in the sense of counting. By "for gaṇ, ī also," the abhyāsa optionally has it. Ajīgaṇat, ajagaṇat. Where a homogeneous vowel has been deleted, it does not become like san: acakathat. Var, in the sense of wishing: avavarat.

Iṅ: some say that, as explained by "for in, before san and caṇ," it is changed to ga. Here, however, "for in, before san and caṇ" applies also to gāyati in the sense of reading: adhyajīgapat. Or else: adhyāpipat.

Aṭi: where "where no homogeneous vowel before in" and so on stands, it is short. By "of the second syllable of one beginning with a vowel," there is reduplication. Then the letter a becomes ā. Āṭiṭat. By the prohibition "not when joined with mā and mā sma," āṭ and the rest do not occur: mā bhavān aṭiṭat. By "not n, b, d or r when first in a conjunct," the letters n, y, b, ṅ and r are not reduplicated.

Undi, in the sense of moistening. By "vṛddhi of the first, for those beginning with a vowel," there is vṛddhi of the first. Aundidat. Ubj, in the sense of straightening: aubjijat. Aḍ, in the sense of applying oneself: āḍiḍat. Arc, in the sense of worship: ārcicat.

Vṛdhu, mṛjū, kṛpū, kṛt. Some say: "before in and caṇ, ṛt for ṛ." Here, however, because it is preceded by the name guṇa, and because "kt for kīrti and iṣ" is known not to be permanent, there is guṇa; or else it is changed to kīrti. Avīvṛdhat, avavardhat, amīmṛjat, amamārjat, acīkḷpat, acakalpat, acīkṛtat, acikīrtat.

Śās and those with ṛ as an indicatory letter: ṭuvasṛ, ṭuyācṛ. Where "the penultimate is short before caṇ, where no homogeneous vowel before in has been deleted" would apply, it is prohibited by "not for śās or those with ṛ as an indicatory letter." Aśaśāsat, apipepat, ayayācat. By "it should be stated: optionally for bhrāj and the rest," it is optionally short. Ababhrājat, abibhrajat, abībhasat, apībhasat, ababhāṣat, adidīpat, adīdipat, ajijīvat, ajījivat, amimīlat, amīmilat, apīpiḍat, apapīḍat, acakāṇat, acīkaṇat, ararāṇat, arīraṇat, apapāṇat, apīpaṇat. Śraṇ: aśaśrāṇat, aśiśraṇat. Heṭh: ajīheṭhat, ajiheṭhat. Lupḷ: alūlupat, alulopat. Here, however, bhrājanam, bhrājastam and acīkarat are established by the ending ghañ and so on.

The division of the vibhakti: by "also ā of the abhyāsa," the penultimate ā is deleted, and the abhyāsa also takes ī. Apīpyat, apīpyatām, apīpyan. By "i for tiṣṭhati," the penultimate of tiṣṭhati becomes i. Atiṣṭhipat, atiṣṭhipatām, atiṣṭhipan. By "optionally for jighrati," the penultimate of jighrati optionally becomes i. Ajighripat, ajījighrapat.

By "also in the impersonal and the passive," there is ic, and the kārita is deleted. Akāri. In the dual and the rest, sic occurs: akārayiṣātām, akārayiṣata. Ghaṭ: by "optionally before ic," the penultimate ā becomes a. Aghaṭi, aghāṭi. Because the agent that is also the object does not become like the object, the agent alone takes caṇ: acīkarata kaṭaḥ svayam eva.

Parokṣā: by "ām in the parokṣā after cakās, kās and those ending in a suffix," there is the suffix ām. Guṇa. It is changed to ay. Kārayāñcakāra. By "also as and bhū, in the parasmaipada," it becomes as and bhū. Kārayāmāsa, kārayāmāsa, kārayāmbabhūva. Śvastanī: kārayitā. Āśīḥ: kāryāt, kārayiṣīṣṭa. Bhaviṣyantī: kārayiṣyati. Kriyātipattiḥ: akārayiṣyat. Thus the domain of the in-ending has been taught.

The in-ending with the san-ending is now to be stated. Kārayitum icchati: san, and reduplication. By "i for a before san," there is i. Cikārayiṣati, jihārayiṣati, jighrāpayiṣati. By "for u before a following labial, semivowel or j," the abhyāsa takes i.

Lilāvayiṣati, pipāvayiṣati, bibhāvayiṣati.

While "optionally for śvayati" carries over, by "also before the kārita, before san and caṇ," there is samprasāraṇa. Śiśvāyayiṣati, śuśāvayiṣati. Hveñ: by "always for hvayati," there is samprasāraṇa. Juhāvayiṣati. Iṅ: some say that, as explained by "for in, before san and caṇ," it is changed to gā. Here, however, "for in, before san and caṇ" applies also to gāyati in the sense of reading: adhijigāpayiṣati. Iṅ: adhyāpipayiṣati. San comes also after the cekrīyita-ending: lolūyiṣate, popūyiṣate. From the wording "of what has no abhyāsa," what has already been reduplicated is not reduplicated again. Thus the domain of the san-ending has been taught.

Nominal roots are now to be stated. Putram icchati: by "yin after a noun, in the sense of wanting for oneself," there is the suffix yin. The letters i and n serve to distinguish it in "ī for a before yin." By "the case endings standing there are to be deleted," the case ending is deleted. When the case ending of any stem has been deleted, that stem takes on just the nature it had before. Then, by "ī for a before yin," the letter a becomes ī. Where putrīya stands, by "these are roots," it has the name root, and the suffix follows the root. By "the rest," there are parasmaipada endings for the agent, and for the agent there is an. Putrīyati, kuṭīyati, kambalīyati, prāvārīyati. Khaṭvām icchati: khaṭvīyati. By "also deletion of n," the letter n is deleted. Rājīyati, takṣīyati.

It is to be accepted that for aśva, kṣīra, vṛṣa and lavaṇa, in the sense of their own desire, there is the augment suk. For aśva and vṛṣa, in the sense of wanting to mate: aśvasyati vaḍavā, vṛṣasyati gauḥ. Otherwise: aśvīyati, vṛṣīyati. For milk and salt, in the sense of wanting to get them: kṣīrasyati bālaḥ, lavaṇasyati gauḥ. Otherwise: kṣīrīyati, lavaṇīyati.

After all nominal stems, in the sense of craving to eat, as is to be stated: dadhyasyati, madhvasyati. Because some accept it, su is to be stated: dadhisyati, madhusyati.

In the very expressions "one who wants food eats," "one who wants water drinks," and "one who wants wealth craves," aśanāya, udanya and dhanāya are known as set particles for hunger, thirst and greed. Thus, as is fitting, there is ā; the k optionally; and the deletion of a is also treated as a set particle. Aśanāyati, udanyati, dhanāyati. In other senses: one who wants food in order to give it, aśanīyati; one who wants water for bathing, udakīyati; one who wants wealth in order to give it, dhanīyati. Here, however, aśanāya is a root in the sense of hunger, udanya in the sense of thirst, and dhanāya in the sense of greed. In the very expressions "one who wants food eats" and so on, yan is to be stated.

By "also kāmya," it becomes the suffix kāmya, in the sense of wanting. Putram icchati, putrakāmyati. Idam icchati, idaṅkāmyati. Svar icchati, svaḥkāmyati.

By "from a standard of comparison, in the sense of behaving," there is the suffix yin. One who behaves as toward a son: putrīyati māṇavakam. By "āyi for the agent, and also deletion of s," there is the suffix āyi, and also deletion of s. One who behaves like a hawk: by "also after those ending in āyi," there are ātmanepada endings. Śyenāyate. Sārasa ivācarati: sārasāyate. The letter s is deleted: ojāyate, apsarāyate. From payas, as explained, the letter s is optionally deleted: payāyate, payasyate. Some say that, because of the meaning intended by the word "optionally" there, the y of āyi is deleted. One who behaves like a donkey: gardabhati, aśvati. Likewise it is said: "Always from ojas and apsaras; from payas, as explained. The deletion of āyi is also to be understood, and also yin for aśva and gardabha." Some say: "of galbha, klība and hoḍa, as explained." From ruc and the rest, there are ātmanepada endings. Galbhate, galbhāyate, klībate, klībāyate, hoḍate, hoḍāyate.

Likewise, by "in the sense of cvi, after bhṛśa and the rest, also deletion of t and s," there is the suffix ā, and also deletion of t, d and s. What is not abundant becomes abundant: bhṛśāyate, unmanāyate, durmanāyate. Vehat: vehāyate. Śaśvat: śaśvāyate. From the word "optionally," cvi also occurs: bhṛśībhavati.

Some say that by "ḍāc in the sense of cvi, after lohita and the rest," there is the suffix ā. Here, however, in the expression paṭatpaṭatkaroti, by "ḍāc for imitating an indistinct sound," there is reduplication in the domain of ḍāc. By "deletion of t is also accepted," t is deleted. Then, by "ḍāc after an imitation of indistinct sound that is not of one vowel, when not before iti," there is the suffix ḍāc. The letter c serves to distinguish it in "ḍāc for imitating an indistinct sound," and the letter ḍ serves to distinguish it in "where ḍ is an indicatory letter, deletion of the final vowel and what follows." By this very rule the final vowel and what follows are deleted.

"It behaves like cloth, like cloth": by "āyi for the agent, and also deletion of s," there is the suffix ā. Because it belongs to the ordinary group of yaj and the rest, there are endings of both voices. Paṭapaṭāyate. Chamaṭ: chamacchamāyati, chamacchamāyate. Ṛṇat: ṛṇaṛṇāyati, ṛṇaṛṇāyate. One who behaves like something red: lohitāyati, lohitāyate.

Kaṣṭāya kramaṇe kramate: kaṣṭāyate. Likewise pāpāyate, kakṣāyate, satrāyate, gahanāyate. This does not occur other than for engagement in evil: kaṣṭaṃ tapaḥ krāmati. Romanthaṃ vartayati gauḥ. One who makes vapor rise: bāṣpāyate; likewise ūṣmāyate, phenāyate. It is to be understood for pleasure and the rest: sukhāyate; likewise duḥkhāyate. One who makes sound and the rest: śabdāyate; likewise vairāyate, kalahāyate, abhrāyate, kaṇvāyate, meghāyate, sudināyate, durdināyate, nīhārāyate. From namas, tapas and varivas, there is yin.

One who pays homage: namasyati. Likewise tapasyati and varivasyati. Because of the restriction stated in the rule "and also elision of n," the final here does not become visarga.

Citaṅ: in the sense of wonder. The letter ṅ indicates the ātmanepada. One who makes a picture in wonder: citrīyate. From kaṇḍū and the rest there is yaṇ. Kaṇḍūñ: making an itch. The letter ñ indicates both voices. The letter ṇ indicates the absence of guṇa. Kaṇḍūyati, kaṇḍūyate. Maheṅ: mahīyate. These and the rest are to be followed accordingly.

For "he takes up a plough, a kali," the suffix in comes by the rule "in the sense of the root, in, and it is kārita." The letter n serves to distinguish it, as in the rule beginning "in, of the stem." Halayate, kalayate. Likewise kṛtayati, varṇayati, tvacayate. Tūstāni vihanti: vitustayati. Keśān vilīkaroti: this means "he loosens the sounds."

One who covers all over with cloth: saṃvastrayati. One who puts on armor: saṃvarmayati. One who crushes all over with powder: avacūrṇayate. By the rule "for doing that, and for saying it at that time," there is in. One who shaves the head: muṇḍayati. Likewise miśrayati, ślakṣṇayati, lavaṇayati, sūtrayati. From vrata, for food and also for its opposite, the suffix in occurs. Payo vratayati: this means "he consumes." Vṛṣalānnaṃ vratayati: this means "he does not eat."

By the rule "he passes beyond by means of it," there is in. One who passes beyond by means of a train of elephants: the preceding word ati is a particle. Atihastayati. In the sense of agent and instrument: one who sings beside it with a vīṇā: upavīṇayati. One who draws out with cotton: avatūlayati. One who praises with verses: upaślokayati. One who advances against with an army: by the rule beginning "sthā and seni" there is ṣ. Abhiṣeṇayati. In some cases, for seeing: rūpaṃ paśyati: rūpayati. Many of these are indeed seen.

Iniṅ: also for a limb that does not stay in place. In the sense of a limb not staying in place, the suffix iniṅ occurs. Hastau nirasyati: hastayate. Likewise pādayate. Because the word "also" in "also for not staying in place" has the sense of "many": for lifting the tail, puccham utkṣipati: utpucchayate. Likewise one who swishes the tail about: paripucchayate gauḥ. One who gathers vessels all together: saṃbhāṇḍayate. One who smooths or arranges the robe: saṃcīvarayate bhikṣuḥ.

Of śvetāśva, aśvatara, galoḍita and āhvaraka there is also elision of aśva, tara, ita and ka. By the letter "also," in and iniṅ are meant to be included for the sense of the root. For speaking of a white horse: "he passes beyond by means of it." Or optionally. Elision of aśva: śvetayati. For speaking of a mule (aśvatara): elision of tara: aśvayate. For speaking of galoḍita: elision of ita: galoḍayate. For speaking of āhvaraka: elision of ka: āhvarayate. Because of the sense of "many," also with the suffix in: śvetayati, aśvayati, and so on.

Also for the elision of mantu, vantu and vin. Here aniṭ need not be stated again. Īśa: in supreme lordship. For īśana there is īṭ. From saṃpad and the rest there is kvip. By the statement "for the rest, mantu is desired" in the rule "īṭ when it exists in this," there is the suffix mantu. By the rule "for speaking of that which has it," when in is made in the sense of the root, mantu is elided. By the principle "when the cause is absent, what is caused is also absent," the stem remains fixed in its own form. Īśayati. For speaking of one possessed of cows: gavayati. For speaking of one possessed of a ladle: after a final dhuṭ, mantu is elided: srucayati. By the rule "vin comes after māyā, medhā and sraj": for speaking of one wearing a garland: srajayati.

Praśasya becomes śra. For praśasya, when in is made by the rule "for speaking," it is changed to śrā. By the rule "also of monosyllables ending in at," there is the augment āp. Śrāpayati. Vṛddha also becomes jya. By the letter "also," praśasya does too. For speaking of something increased, praśasya also optionally. In. It is changed to jya. As before, the augment āp. Jyāpayati.

Antika and bāḍha become neda and sādha. For speaking of something near: in. It is changed to neda. Nedayati. For speaking of something strong: in. It is changed to sādha. Sādhayati.

Yuvan and alpa optionally become kan. For speaking of one who is youthful: in. It is changed to kan. Kanayati. In one alternative, by the rule beginning "sthūla, dūra, yuvan and kṣipra," there is elision of the final and what follows it, and also guṇa. Yavayati. For speaking of something small: kanayati; in one alternative, alpayati.

Of sthūla, dūra, yuvan, kṣipra and kṣudra there is elision of the final and what follows it, and also guṇa. For speaking of something stout: in. Elision of the final and what follows it, and also guṇa of the nāmin vowel. Sthavayati. For speaking of something far: davayati. For speaking of one who is youthful: yavayati. For speaking of something quick: kṣepayati. For speaking of something minute: kṣodayati.

Of bahu, ya and the rest, and bhū also. By the rule "for speaking of many," there is the letter ya, ḍa, and in. Bahu too is changed to bhū. Bhūyayati.

Of priya, sthira, sphira, uru, guru, bahula, tṛpra, dīrgha, hrasva, vṛddha and vṛndāraka there are pra, stha, spha, vara, gara, baṃha, trapa, drāgha, hrasa, varṣa and vṛnda. For speaking of what is dear: in. It is changed to pra. There is also the augment āp. Prāpayati. Likewise, for speaking of something firm: sthāpayati. For speaking of something abundant: sphāpayati. For speaking of something broad: varayati. For speaking of something heavy: garayati. For speaking of something plentiful: baṃhayati. For speaking of something satisfied: trapayati. For speaking of something long: drāghayati. For speaking of something short: hrasayati. For speaking of something grown: varṣayati. For speaking of a multitude: vṛndayati.

After satya, artha and veda, the ending āpa is kārita. For speaking of what is true: in. By this, āpa.

Satyāpayati. Likewise arthāpayati and vedāpayati. By the rule "of monosyllables ending in at": śāpayati. Because of the statement "it is kārita," it does not occur here. Śreṣṭhaḥ, śremā, śreyān.

This completes the Clear Words composed by the master Joyful Renown.

This was translated into Tibetan from the Indian manuscript that was the personal copy of the Chal translator, Pal Chökyi Zangpo, by the Śākya monk Dharmaśrībhadra, through the kindness of the venerable great abbot and great translator Butön. The place of translation was the great Dharma center of Riphuk, blessed by the paṇḍita Dīpaṃkaraśrījñāna.

This is a Sanskrit grammatical work in the Kātantra tradition (Kumāra, Śarvavarman, Durga and Trilocana are Kātantra authorities); the Tibetan reproduces the Sanskrit endings and tense names in Tibetan letters, and these are given as spelled, including their variant spellings. Here རྒྱུད is taken as "work" rather than "tantra." The sentence about stems ending in the letter a "occurring as ones to be joined" is obscure and its rendering is tentative. In the rules on person, བྱེད and དང are read as the evident ཁྱེད "you" and ངེད "we," as the explanation that follows shows. In this grammatical tradition the "first person" is the one used with nouns (our third person) and the "last person" is the one used with "we." The syllable ཁལ after the list of prefixes makes no sense in context and is left untranslated. The rendering of ཡངས་པ ("left aside") for the list of roots beginning with sṛ is tentative. This is a technical Sanskrit grammatical text (verb conjugation in the manner of the Kātantra); the Sanskrit roots and verb forms are given exactly as the Tibetan spells them, including evident irregularities.
